FILE: AGENTS.md

   1 | # CLAUDE.md
   2 | 
   3 | This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
   4 | 
   5 | ## What This Is
   6 | 
   7 | Dikarya is a Flask web application for fungal phylogenetic analysis. Users submit DNA sequences (FASTA or GenBank accessions), which are run through a configurable bioinformatics pipeline (alignment → trimming → tree building) via background workers, with real-time status updates via Server-Sent Events. Results are displayed in an interactive tree viewer.
   8 | 
   9 | **Stack:** Flask + Gunicorn, PostgreSQL + SQLAlchemy, Redis + RQ, Biopython, Tailwind (template-based UI).
  10 | 
  11 | ## Commands
  12 | 
  13 | ```bash
  14 | # Activate the virtual environment first
  15 | source .venv/bin/activate
  16 | 
  17 | # Database migrations
  18 | flask db migrate -m "description"
  19 | flask db upgrade
  20 | 
  21 | # Run web app (dev)
  22 | export FLASK_APP=wsgi.py
  23 | export FLASK_ENV=development
  24 | flask run --port=5000
  25 | 
  26 | # Start background worker (required for job processing)
  27 | flask run-worker
  28 | 
  29 | # Start metrics collector
  30 | flask run-metrics
  31 | 
  32 | # Add a What's New changelog entry (agents should use this to announce changes)
  33 | flask whats-new-add --title "Title here" --body "Description of what changed." --category feature
  34 | # Categories: feature, fix, improvement, update
  35 | 
  36 | # List existing What's New entries
  37 | flask whats-new-list
  38 | ```
  39 | 
  40 | Production runs under systemd as `dikarya-web.service` (Gunicorn) and `dikarya-worker.service` (RQ worker).     Many changes require dikarya-web to be restarted, which requires root access, so ask me if you need it to be restarted.
  41 | 
  42 | ## Architecture
  43 | 
  44 | ### Request Flow
  45 | 
  46 | 1. User submits sequences at `/tree` → job created in PostgreSQL, enqueued in Redis Queue
  47 | 2. `app/workers/tasks.py:run_phylo_job()` orchestrates the pipeline in a background worker
  48 | 3. Each step publishes events to Redis PubSub channel `job:{job_id}`
  49 | 4. Frontend subscribes via SSE at `/api/job/<id>/events` for real-time updates
  50 | 5. Results stored on disk at `var/jobs/{job_id}/`; tree viewer loads from `/job/<id>/view`
  51 | 
  52 | ### Pipeline Steps (in order)
  53 | 
  54 | `INPUT → ORIENT → BLAST → ALIGN → TRIM → TREE → POST`
  55 | 
  56 | ORIENT (sequence orientation), BLAST (homolog search), and TRIM are optional. The tree viewer at `/job/<id>/view` allows post-hoc pruning, rerooting, and renaming.
  57 | 
  58 | ### Blueprints
  59 | 
  60 | - `journal/` — public-facing journal website pages
  61 | - `main/` — sequence entry form, job status page, tree viewer
  62 | - `auth/` — login/register/logout
  63 | - `user/` — user job dashboard
  64 | - `api/` — all JSON endpoints used by the frontend JS
  65 | - `monitoring/` — health checks, worker status, system metrics
  66 | 
  67 | ### Services (`app/services/`)
  68 | 
  69 | Each bioinformatics step has its own service module. All external tool invocations go through `subprocess_utils.py`. Input validation and path safety live in `security_utils.py`. The `access_control.py` module enforces job ownership.
  70 | 
  71 | ### Job Parameters
  72 | 
  73 | `app/models.py` defines the dataclasses: `JobParams`, `AlignmentParams`, `TreeBuilderParams`, `TrimmingParams`. These are serialized to `var/jobs/{job_id}/input_info.json`.
  74 | 
  75 | ### File Layout on Disk
  76 | 
  77 | ```
  78 | var/jobs/{job_id}/
  79 |   input.fasta, oriented.fasta, aligned.fasta, trimmed.fasta
  80 |   blast_results.json
  81 |   tree/tree.newick, tree.nexus, tree_metadata.json, tree_state.json
  82 |   logs/{step}.log
  83 | ```
  84 | 
  85 | ### External Tool Binaries
  86 | 
  87 | Configured via environment variables (with fallback defaults in `app/config.py`): `RAXML_BINARY`, `IQTREE_BINARY`, `MRBAYES_BINARY`, `MAFFT_BINARY`, `MUSCLE_BINARY`, `CLUSTALO_BINARY`, `TRIMAL_BINARY`, `BMGE_BINARY`, `FASTTREE_BINARY`.
  88 | 
  89 | ## Frontend
  90 | 
  91 | ### Tree Builder Page (`/tree`)
  92 | 
  93 | The main Tree Builder UI is implemented in `templates/sequence_entry.html`.
  94 | 
  95 | This template contains:
  96 | 
  97 | - HTML for imports, BLAST, queue, and submit controls
  98 | - A large inline `<script>` block that owns page state and behavior:
  99 |   - `sequenceQueue` state
 100 |   - Queue rendering (`updateQueueDisplay`)
 101 |   - MycoMap / iNaturalist import handlers
 102 |   - BLAST submission
 103 |   - Submit payload construction
 104 | 
 105 | Shared frontend helpers live in `templates/base_modern.html` (global `showStatus()`, dark mode, layout shell).
 106 | 
 107 | ### Tree Viewer Page (`/job/<id>/view`)
 108 | 
 109 | Three large JS files handle the tree viewer:
 110 | 
 111 | - `app/static/js/tree_viewer_controller.js` (orchestration)
 112 | - `app/static/js/tree_viewer_phylotree_v2.js` (D3 rendering)
 113 | - `app/static/js/phylotree.js` (library)
 114 | 
 115 | **Change comment convention for `.js`:** Every edit to files ending in .js (Javascript) must include an inline comment in the format `// Alan M/D/YY - <brief description of what changed and why>` placed on the line immediately above the changed code.
 116 | 
 117 | Job status updates live in `job_status.js`.
 118 | 
 119 | ### Frontend Data Contracts
 120 | 
 121 | #### `sequenceQueue` Item Shape
 122 | 
 123 | `sequenceQueue` is an array of objects stored in the inline JS in `templates/sequence_entry.html`. Queue operations should preserve extra metadata fields on entries (do not strip unknown keys).
 124 | 
 125 | ```json
 126 | {
 127 |   "name": "string (required)",
 128 |   "sequence": "string (required)",
 129 |   "organism": "string (optional)",
 130 |   "source": "string (optional, e.g., 'mycomap')",
 131 |   "hit_source": "string | null (e.g., 'ncbi', 'local')",
 132 |   "query_cover": "number | null",
 133 |   "subject_cover": "number | null",
 134 |   "identity": "number | null",
 135 |   "blast_metrics_available": "boolean (optional)"
 136 | }
 137 | ```
 138 | 
 139 | **Note:** Not all queue entries will have source-specific fields (e.g., manual FASTA, file upload, iNaturalist), so UI/filter logic must handle missing values safely.
 140 | 
 141 | #### API Response Conventions (Frontend-facing)
 142 | 
 143 | When changing `/api/` endpoints used by `sequence_entry.html`:
 144 | 
 145 | - Prefer additive JSON changes (new optional fields) over breaking changes.
 146 | - Do not rename/remove existing fields unless explicitly requested.
 147 | - Preserve current success/error response behavior expected by existing frontend handlers.
 148 | 
 149 | Endpoints frequently used by `/tree`:
 150 | 
 151 | - `/api/blast`
 152 | - `/api/mycomap`
 153 | - `/api/inaturalist`
 154 | - `/api/job`
 155 | 
 156 | ### MycoMap Integration Notes (Important)
 157 | 
 158 | `services/mycomap_service.py` currently fetches FASTA from MycoMap endpoints:
 159 | 
 160 | - `fasta` (NCBI results)
 161 | - `localFasta` (local/MycoBLAST results)
 162 | 
 163 | If a feature needs BLAST metrics (e.g., query coverage / subject coverage / identity), it typically requires:
 164 | 
 165 | 1. An additional fetch/parsing step for the BLAST results table/page.
 166 | 2. Matching parsed hit rows back to imported FASTA entries.
 167 | 3. Preserving those metrics on queue entries for frontend filtering.
 168 | 
 169 | Current `validate_mycomap_url(...)` does strict hostname and scheme validation and extracts `r<digits>` blast IDs. Preserve this security behavior.
 170 | 
 171 | ## Restarting Dikarya services
 172 | 
 173 | Agents may restart the Dikarya systemd services when needed after making changes. The `tree` user has limited passwordless sudo access to three root-owned wrapper scripts only:
 174 | 
 175 | ```bash
 176 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-web
 177 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-worker
 178 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-metrics
 179 | ```
 180 | 
 181 | Use these wrappers instead of running sudo systemctl restart ... directly.
 182 | 
 183 | The most commonly needed restart is:
 184 | 
 185 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-web
 186 | 
 187 | Run this after changes that affect the web application runtime, routes, views/templates, Python application code, configuration read by the web process, dependencies, or other behavior served by the Dikarya web app.
 188 | 
 189 | Use the worker or metrics restart wrappers only when the change affects those specific background services.
 190 | 
 191 | Do not attempt to use sudo for anything else. The tree user is intentionally restricted and should not be able to open a root shell, run arbitrary systemctl commands, edit sudoers, or restart unrelated services.
 192 | 
 193 | ## What's New / Changelog System
 194 | 
 195 | The "What's New" page at `/whats-new` shows changelog entries stored in PostgreSQL. It is linked in the header nav between "Journal Home" and "Tree Builder".
 196 | 
 197 | **When you (an agent) make a user-visible change, add an entry so users know about it.   At the end of the agent output, tell the user that you have done this, and tell the user the exact text added to the What's New page**
 198 | 
 199 | ### Adding an entry
 200 | 
 201 | ```bash
 202 | source .venv/bin/activate
 203 | FLASK_APP=wsgi.py flask whats-new-add \
 204 |   --title "Short description of the change" \
 205 |   --body "Longer explanation for users. Can include plain text or simple HTML." \
 206 |   --category feature
 207 | ```
 208 | 
 209 | Categories:
 210 | - `feature` — new capability added
 211 | - `fix` — bug fixed
 212 | - `improvement` — existing feature made better
 213 | - `update` — dependency, content, or configuration change
 214 | 
 215 | ### How it works
 216 | 
 217 | - `WhatsNewEntry` model in `app/models.py` stores entries (title, body, category, published_at).
 218 | - `WhatsNewView` tracks each user's (or IP's) last visit to the page.
 219 | - Entries newer than the visitor's last view are shown with full contrast + a gold "New" badge.
 220 | - Older entries are slightly dimmed.
 221 | - A gold dot on the nav link appears whenever there are unseen entries.
 222 | - The context processor in `app/__init__.py` (`inject_whats_new_badge`) injects `whats_new_has_new` into all templates.
 223 | 
 224 | ### Listing existing entries
 225 | 
 226 | ```bash
 227 | FLASK_APP=wsgi.py flask whats-new-list
 228 | ```
 229 | 
 230 | ## Key Conventions & UI Patterns
 231 | 
 232 | - Flask app factory in `app/__init__.py`; extensions initialized in `app/extensions.py`.
 233 | - All API responses use JSON; the frontend is a SPA-style UI talking to `/api/` endpoints.
 234 | - FASTA sequence headers are sanitized on input and restored on download/display (see `fasta_utils.py`).
 235 | - RAxML-NG jobs use named presets (`fast_good`, `standard`, `publication`, `maximum`) defined in `tree_builder_service.py`.
 236 | - Job IDs are UUIDs; always validate with regex before using in file paths.
 237 | - Use existing Tailwind utility style patterns from `templates/sequence_entry.html` and `templates/partials/*.html`.
 238 | - Reuse modal structure from `templates/partials/add_sequences_modal.html`.
 239 | - Support dark mode (`dark:*` classes) for all new UI.
 240 | - Reuse existing color/style tokens (`journal-dark`, `journal-gold`, etc.).
 241 | - `showStatus()` is global and provided by `templates/base_modern.html`.
 242 | 
 243 | ## Change Strategy for AI Agents (Minimal Churn)
 244 | 
 245 | - **Do NOT use TDD** — just make the requested changes. The human developer will test the code manually.
 246 | - Prefer targeted edits and minimal, production-safe modifications over large refactors.
 247 | - Prefer small helper functions over large rewrites when adding features.
 248 | - Do NOT split large inline scripts into separate JS files unless explicitly requested by the user.
 249 | - Preserve existing DOM IDs and event handler wiring whenever possible.
 250 | - Preserve queue behavior strictly:
 251 |   - Count badge updates.
 252 |   - Empty/non-empty state toggles.
 253 |   - Clear/remove actions.
 254 |   - Outgroup dropdown refresh (`populateOutgroupDropdown`) after queue changes.
 255 |   - Duplicate handling in `addSequences(...)`.
 256 | 
 257 | ## Manual QA Checklist for `/tree` Changes
 258 | 
 259 | > **Note for AI:** The following is a manual QA checklist for the human developer. You do not need to execute these tests or write test code for them; just ensure your code changes do not break these existing flows.
 260 | 
 261 | ### Queue Basics
 262 | 
 263 | - Add custom FASTA sequences works.
 264 | - File upload → parse → add to queue works.
 265 | - Remove one sequence (×) works.
 266 | - Clear all works.
 267 | - Sequence count badge updates correctly.
 268 | 
 269 | ### Imports
 270 | 
 271 | - MycoMap import works (NCBI/local checkboxes respected).
 272 | - MycoMap duplicate removal checkbox behavior still works.
 273 | - iNaturalist analyze + add/BLAST flows still work.
 274 | - BLAST from manual input works.
 275 | 
 276 | ### Submission
 277 | 
 278 | - One-Click Quick Tree still submits successfully.
 279 | - Advanced panel toggle still works.
 280 | - Standard submit still sends valid payload.
 281 | - Edit/clone job modes still work (`edit_job_id`, `clone_job_id`).
 282 | 
 283 | ### UX
 284 | 
 285 | - New UI works in dark mode.
 286 | - Status messages display via `showStatus()`.
 287 | - No console JS errors.
 288 | 
 289 | ## Ops / Debugging Notes
 290 | 
 291 | - Production runs under systemd (Gunicorn web + worker services).
 292 | - If route changes do not appear in production, Gunicorn reload/restart may be required.
 293 | - Check app logs for 500 errors before changing code.
 294 | 
 295 | ## Documentation Split
 296 | 
 297 | - **CLAUDE.md** = implementation guidance, conventions, and safe-edit rules.
 298 | - **ARCHITECTURE.md** = deeper system design and reference details.
 299 | 
 300 | If making substantial changes, consult `ARCHITECTURE.md` for subsystem context, but keep code changes aligned with the conventions in this file.

   9 | from app.config import config
  10 | 
  11 | def create_app(config_name='default'):
  12 |     app = Flask(__name__)
  13 |     app.config.from_object(config[config_name])
  14 | 
  15 |     # Configure Logging for Gunicorn
  16 |     import logging
  17 |     if __name__ != '__main__':
  18 |         gunicorn_logger = logging.getLogger('gunicorn.error')
  19 |         if gunicorn_logger.handlers:
  20 |             app.logger.handlers = gunicorn_logger.handlers
  21 |             app.logger.setLevel(gunicorn_logger.level)
  22 |     # Initialize extensions
  23 |     from app.extensions import db, login_manager, migrate, csrf, limiter
  24 |     db.init_app(app)
  25 |     migrate.init_app(app, db)
  26 |     csrf.init_app(app)
  27 |     login_manager.init_app(app)
  28 |     
  29 |     # Configure Limiter storage from config
  30 |     app.config.setdefault("RATELIMIT_STORAGE_URI", "memory://")
  31 |     if app.config.get('REDIS_URL'):
  32 |         app.config["RATELIMIT_STORAGE_URI"] = app.config['REDIS_URL']
  33 |     limiter.init_app(app)
  34 | 
  35 |     # CSRF error handler - return JSON for API routes
  36 |     from flask_wtf.csrf import CSRFError
  37 |     from flask import request, jsonify
  38 | 
  39 |     @app.errorhandler(CSRFError)
  40 |     def handle_csrf_error(e):
  41 |         if request.path.startswith("/api/"):
  42 |             return jsonify(error="CSRF token missing or invalid", message=e.description), 400
  43 |         return "CSRF error", 400
  44 | 
  45 |     # Register Blueprints
  46 |     # Journal blueprint handles the root route and static pages
  47 |     from app.journal import bp as journal_bp
  48 |     app.register_blueprint(journal_bp)
  49 | 
  50 |     from app.main import bp as main_bp
  51 |     app.register_blueprint(main_bp)
  52 | 
  53 |     from app.api import bp as api_bp
  54 |     app.register_blueprint(api_bp, url_prefix='/api')
  55 | 
  56 |     from app.auth import bp as auth_bp
  57 |     app.register_blueprint(auth_bp, url_prefix='/auth')
  58 | 
  59 |     from app.user import bp as user_bp
  60 |     app.register_blueprint(user_bp, url_prefix='/user')
  61 | 
  62 |     from app.monitoring import bp as monitoring_bp
  63 |     app.register_blueprint(monitoring_bp)
  64 | 
  65 |     # Inject What's New badge indicator into all templates
  66 |     @app.context_processor
  67 |     def inject_whats_new_badge():
  68 |         try:
  69 |             from app.models import WhatsNewEntry, WhatsNewView
  70 |             from flask_login import current_user
  71 |             from flask import request as req
  72 |             latest = WhatsNewEntry.query.order_by(WhatsNewEntry.published_at.desc()).first()
  73 |             if not latest:
  74 |                 return {'whats_new_has_new': False}
  75 |             view_record = None
  76 |             if current_user.is_authenticated:
  77 |                 view_record = WhatsNewView.query.filter_by(user_id=current_user.id).first()
  78 |             else:
  79 |                 view_record = WhatsNewView.query.filter_by(ip_address=req.remote_addr).first()
  80 |             has_new = view_record is None or latest.published_at > view_record.last_viewed_at
  81 |             return {'whats_new_has_new': has_new}
  82 |         except Exception:
  83 |             return {'whats_new_has_new': False}
  84 | 
  85 |     # Register CLI commands
  86 |     from app import cli
  87 |     cli.register(app)
  88 | 
  89 |     return app

  18 | # =============================================================================
  19 | # BLAST API Endpoint
  20 | # =============================================================================
  21 | 
  22 | def _is_genbank_accession(text):
  23 |     """Check if text looks like a GenBank accession number."""
  24 |     # Common nucleotide patterns: NC_012345, NM_001234567, OR807397.1, etc.
  25 |     pattern = r'^[A-Z]{1,6}_?\d{5,9}(?:\.\d+)?$'
  26 |     return bool(re.match(pattern, text.strip(), re.IGNORECASE))
  27 | 
  28 | 
  29 | MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_ACCESSIONS = 200
  30 | MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP = 5000
  31 | 
  32 | 
  33 | def _parse_genbank_accession_tokens(value):
  34 |     """Parse comma/whitespace-separated GenBank accessions."""
  35 |     if isinstance(value, list):
  36 |         raw_text = " ".join(str(item) for item in value)
  37 |     else:
  38 |         raw_text = str(value or "")
  39 | 
  40 |     tokens = [token.strip() for token in re.split(r'[\s,;]+', raw_text) if token.strip()]
  41 |     accessions = []
  42 |     invalid = []
  43 |     seen = set()
  44 | 
  45 |     for token in tokens:
  46 |         normalized = token.upper()
  47 |         if not _is_genbank_accession(normalized):
  48 |             invalid.append(token)
  49 |             continue
  50 |         if normalized not in seen:
  51 |             accessions.append(normalized)
  52 |             seen.add(normalized)
  53 | 
  54 |     return accessions, invalid
  55 | 
  56 | 
  57 | def _split_fasta_header(header):
  58 |     """Split FASTA header into ID (first token) and description."""
  59 |     header = (header or "").strip()
  60 |     if not header:
  61 |         return "", ""
  62 |     parts = header.split(None, 1)
  63 |     seq_id = parts[0]
  64 |     rest = parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""
  65 |     return seq_id, rest
  66 | 
  67 | def _make_unique_id(base_id, used_ids):
  68 |     """Make ID unique by appending suffix if needed."""
  69 |     if base_id not in used_ids:
  70 |         used_ids.add(base_id)
  71 |         return base_id
  72 |     # Start with simple _added suffix first if not present?
  73 |     # User logic: _added{i} starting at 2?
  74 |     # Or just _added, then _added2?
  75 |     # Let's follow user example: {base_id}_added{i}
  76 |     
  77 |     # Check simple _added first?
  78 |     candidate = f"{base_id}_added"
  79 |     if candidate not in used_ids:
  80 |         used_ids.add(candidate)
  81 |         return candidate
  82 |         
  83 |     i = 2
  84 |     MAX_SUFFIX = 10000  # Prevent infinite loops
  85 |     while f"{base_id}_added{i}" in used_ids:
  86 |         if i > MAX_SUFFIX:
  87 |             # Fallback: use timestamp or random suffix
  88 |             import time
  89 |             new_id = f"{base_id}_added_{int(time.time()*1000) % 1000000}"
  90 |             used_ids.add(new_id)
  91 |             return new_id
  92 |         i += 1
  93 |     new_id = f"{base_id}_added{i}"
  94 |     used_ids.add(new_id)
  95 |     return new_id
  96 | 
  97 | def _parse_fasta_sequences(text):
  98 |     """Parse FASTA text into list of {name, sequence} dicts.
  99 |     
 100 |     Preserves the full header (after >) for display in tree tips.
 101 |     """
 102 |     sequences = []
 103 |     current_name = None
 104 |     current_seq = []
 105 |     
 106 |     for line in text.strip().split('\n'):
 107 |         line = line.strip()
 108 |         if line.startswith('>'):
 109 |             if current_name is not None:
 110 |                 sequences.append({
 111 |                     'name': current_name,
 112 |                     'sequence': ''.join(current_seq)
 113 |                 })
 114 |             # Keep the full header (everything after >) for tree tip labels
 115 |             current_name = line[1:].strip()
 116 |             current_seq = []
 117 |         elif line and current_name is not None:
 118 |             current_seq.append(line)
 119 |     
 120 |     # Don't forget the last sequence
 121 |     if current_name is not None:
 122 |         sequences.append({
 123 |             'name': current_name,
 124 |             'sequence': ''.join(current_seq)
 125 |         })
 126 |     
 127 |     return sequences
 128 | 
 129 | 
 130 | def _fetch_genbank_sequences_for_queue(accessions, max_sequence_bp=MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP):
 131 |     """Fetch GenBank accessions and shape them for frontend queue entries."""
 132 |     from app.services.blast_service import fetch_fasta_for_accessions
 133 |     from app.services.fasta_utils import clean_dna_sequence
 134 | 
 135 |     fasta_content = fetch_fasta_for_accessions(accessions)
 136 |     parsed_sequences = _parse_fasta_sequences(fasta_content)
 137 | 
 138 |     sequences = []
 139 |     skipped = []
 140 |     found_versions = set()
 141 |     found_bases = set()
 142 | 
 143 |     for seq in parsed_sequences:
 144 |         raw_header = (seq.get("name") or "").strip()
 145 |         seq_id, description = _split_fasta_header(raw_header)
 146 | 
 147 |         if seq_id:
 148 |             found_versions.add(seq_id.upper())
 149 |             found_bases.add(seq_id.split(".")[0].upper())
 150 | 
 151 |         sequence = clean_dna_sequence(seq.get("sequence", ""), min_length=1)
 152 |         if not sequence:
 153 |             skipped.append({
 154 |                 "accession": seq_id or raw_header or "unknown",
 155 |                 "reason": "empty_sequence"
 156 |             })
 157 |             continue
 158 | 
 159 |         if len(sequence) > max_sequence_bp:
 160 |             skipped.append({
 161 |                 "accession": seq_id or raw_header or "unknown",
 162 |                 "reason": "too_long",
 163 |                 "length": len(sequence),
 164 |                 "max_length": max_sequence_bp
 165 |             })
 166 |             continue
 167 | 
 168 |         sequences.append({
 169 |             "name": seq_id or raw_header,
 170 |             "organism": description,
 171 |             "sequence": sequence,
 172 |             "source": "genbank"
 173 |         })
 174 | 
 175 |     for accession in accessions:
 176 |         requested = accession.upper()
 177 |         if "." in requested:
 178 |             found = requested in found_versions
 179 |         else:
 180 |             found = requested in found_bases
 181 | 
 182 |         if not found:
 183 |             skipped.append({
 184 |                 "accession": accession,
 185 |                 "reason": "not_found"
 186 |             })
 187 | 
 188 |     return sequences, skipped
 189 |

 192 | @bp.route('/blast', methods=['POST'])
 193 | def run_blast():
 194 |     """
 195 |     Run BLAST on a single sequence or accession.
 196 |     
 197 |     Request: { "query": "<sequence or accession>" }
 198 |     Response: { "status": "success", "sequences": [...], "message": "..." }
 199 |     """
 200 |     data = request.get_json() or {}
 201 |     query = data.get('query', '').strip()
 202 |     
 203 |     if not query:
 204 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "No query provided"}), 400
 205 |     
 206 |     is_valid_query, error_msg = validate_blast_query(query)
 207 |     if not is_valid_query:
 208 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": f"Invalid query: {error_msg}"}), 400
 209 |     
 210 |     try:
 211 |         from app.services.blast_service import blast_from_sequence, blast_from_accessions
 212 |         from pathlib import Path
 213 | 
 214 |         # Extract parameters
 215 |         min_identity = float(data.get('min_identity', 90.0))
 216 |         max_sequences = int(data.get('max_sequences', 50))
 217 |         
 218 |         # Determine if query is an accession or a sequence
 219 |         if _is_genbank_accession(query):
 220 |             logger.info(f"BLAST API: Detected accession: {query}")
 221 |             result = blast_from_accessions([query], Config, min_identity=min_identity, max_sequences=max_sequences)
 222 |         else:
 223 |             # Assume it's a raw sequence
 224 |             logger.info(f"BLAST API: Using sequence query ({len(query)} chars)")
 225 |             result = blast_from_sequence(query, Config, min_identity=min_identity, max_sequences=max_sequences)
 226 |         
 227 |         # Read FASTA content from the file path returned by blast service
 228 |         fasta_path = result.get('fasta_path', '')
 229 |         fasta_content = ''
 230 |         if fasta_path:
 231 |             path = Path(fasta_path)
 232 |             if path.exists():
 233 |                 fasta_content = path.read_text()
 234 |         
 235 |         sequences = _parse_fasta_sequences(fasta_content)
 236 |         
 237 |         # Merge organism info from hit_details into sequences
 238 |         hit_details = result.get('hit_details', [])
 239 |         organism_map = {h['accession']: h.get('organism', '') for h in hit_details}
 240 |         
 241 |         for seq in sequences:
 242 |             # Extract accession from the full name (first word, without version)
 243 |             # Full name could be "AY702745.1 Mycena amicta..." - we need "AY702745" for lookup
 244 |             first_word = seq['name'].split()[0] if seq['name'] else ''
 245 |             acc_no_version = first_word.split('.')[0]
 246 |             seq['organism'] = organism_map.get(acc_no_version, organism_map.get(first_word, ''))
 247 |         
 248 |         return jsonify({
 249 |             "status": "success",
 250 |             "sequences": sequences,
 251 |             "accessions": result.get('hit_accessions', []),
 252 |             "message": f"Found {len(sequences)} related sequences"
 253 |         })
 254 |         
 255 |     except Exception as e:
 256 |         logger.error(f"BLAST API error: {e}", exc_info=True)
 257 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": str(e)}), 500
 258 |

 260 | @bp.route('/genbank/accessions', methods=['POST'])
 261 | def fetch_genbank_accessions():
 262 |     """
 263 |     Fetch one or more GenBank accessions for direct queue insertion.
 264 | 
 265 |     Request: { "accessions": ["OR807397", "OR807397.1"] }
 266 |     Response: { "status": "success", "sequences": [...], "skipped": [...] }
 267 |     """
 268 |     data = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}
 269 |     raw_accessions = data.get("accessions", data.get("input", ""))
 270 | 
 271 |     accessions, invalid = _parse_genbank_accession_tokens(raw_accessions)
 272 |     if invalid:
 273 |         return jsonify({
 274 |             "status": "error",
 275 |             "error": f"Invalid GenBank accession(s): {', '.join(invalid[:10])}"
 276 |         }), 400
 277 | 
 278 |     if not accessions:
 279 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "No GenBank accessions provided"}), 400
 280 | 
 281 |     if len(accessions) > MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_ACCESSIONS:
 282 |         return jsonify({
 283 |             "status": "error",
 284 |             "error": f"Too many accessions (max {MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_ACCESSIONS})"
 285 |         }), 400
 286 | 
 287 |     try:
 288 |         sequences, skipped = _fetch_genbank_sequences_for_queue(
 289 |             accessions,
 290 |             max_sequence_bp=MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP
 291 |         )
 292 | 
 293 |         if not sequences:
 294 |             too_long = [item for item in skipped if item.get("reason") == "too_long"]
 295 |             if too_long:
 296 |                 return jsonify({
 297 |                     "status": "error",
 298 |                     "error": f"No sequences were added. GenBank sequences must be {MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP:,} bp or shorter.",
 299 |                     "skipped": skipped,
 300 |                     "max_sequence_bp": MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP
 301 |                 }), 400
 302 | 
 303 |             return jsonify({
 304 |                 "status": "error",
 305 |                 "error": "No GenBank sequences found for the provided accession(s).",
 306 |                 "skipped": skipped,
 307 |                 "max_sequence_bp": MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP
 308 |             }), 404
 309 | 
 310 |         message = f"Fetched {len(sequences)} GenBank sequence{'s' if len(sequences) != 1 else ''}"
 311 |         if skipped:
 312 |             message += f" ({len(skipped)} skipped)"
 313 | 
 314 |         return jsonify({
 315 |             "status": "success",
 316 |             "sequences": sequences,
 317 |             "skipped": skipped,
 318 |             "max_sequence_bp": MAX_CUSTOM_GENBANK_SEQUENCE_BP,
 319 |             "message": message
 320 |         })
 321 |     except Exception as e:
 322 |         logger.error(f"GenBank accession API error: {e}", exc_info=True)
 323 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": str(e)}), 500
 324 |

 326 | @bp.route('/mycomap', methods=['POST'])
 327 | def fetch_mycomap():
 328 |     """
 329 |     Fetch sequences from a Mycomap BLAST results URL.
 330 |     
 331 |     Request: { 
 332 |         "url": "<mycomap URL>", 
 333 |         "include_ncbi": true,
 334 |         "include_local": true 
 335 |     }
 336 |     Response: { "status": "success", "sequences": [...], "message": "..." }
 337 |     """
 338 |     data = request.get_json() or {}
 339 |     url = data.get('url', '').strip()
 340 |     include_ncbi = data.get('include_ncbi', True)
 341 |     include_local = data.get('include_local', True)
 342 |     
 343 |     if not url:
 344 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "No URL provided"}), 400
 345 |     
 346 |     # Validate at least one checkbox is selected
 347 |     if not include_ncbi and not include_local:
 348 |         return jsonify({
 349 |             "status": "error", 
 350 |             "error": "Select at least one result type (NCBI or Local)"
 351 |         }), 400
 352 |     
 353 |     try:
 354 |         from app.services.mycomap_service import validate_mycomap_url, fetch_mycomap_fasta
 355 |         from app.services.fasta_utils import clean_dna_sequence
 356 |         
 357 |         # Validate and extract blast_id
 358 |         blast_id = validate_mycomap_url(url)
 359 |         if not blast_id:
 360 |             return jsonify({
 361 |                 "status": "error",
 362 |                 "error": "Invalid Mycomap URL. URL must be from mycomap.com and contain a result ID (e.g., r12345)"
 363 |             }), 400
 364 |         
 365 |         logger.info(f"Mycomap API: Fetching sequences for blast_id={blast_id} (ncbi={include_ncbi}, local={include_local})")
 366 |         
 367 |         # Fetch FASTA from Mycomap
 368 |         result = fetch_mycomap_fasta(blast_id, include_ncbi, include_local)
 369 |         
 370 |         # Check for errors
 371 |         if result['errors'] and not result['fasta_content']:
 372 |             return jsonify({
 373 |                 "status": "error",
 374 |                 "error": "; ".join(result['errors'])
 375 |             }), 502
 376 |         
 377 |         # Parse FASTA into sequences
 378 |         sequences = _parse_fasta_sequences(result['fasta_content'])
 379 | 
 380 |         # Tag hit_source based on position (NCBI sequences come first in combined FASTA)
 381 |         ncbi_count = result['ncbi_count']
 382 |         for i, seq in enumerate(sequences):
 383 |             seq['source'] = 'mycomap'
 384 |             if include_ncbi and include_local:
 385 |                 seq['hit_source'] = 'ncbi' if i < ncbi_count else 'local'
 386 |             elif include_ncbi:
 387 |                 seq['hit_source'] = 'ncbi'
 388 |             else:
 389 |                 seq['hit_source'] = 'local'
 390 | 
 391 |         # Identify NCBI accessions that are missing meaningful descriptions
 392 |         # (MyCoMap often returns NCBI records as just '>ACCESSION' without metadata)
 393 |         from app.services.blast_service import fetch_fasta_for_accessions
 394 |         
 395 |         accessions_to_enrich = []
 396 |         for seq in sequences:
 397 |             # Check if name is a pure accession with no or few spaces
 398 |             parts = seq['name'].split()
 399 |             if len(parts) == 1 and _is_genbank_accession(parts[0].split('.')[0]):
 400 |                 accessions_to_enrich.append(parts[0].split('.')[0])
 401 |                 
 402 |         if accessions_to_enrich:
 403 |             logger.info(f"Mycomap API: Enriching {len(accessions_to_enrich)} sparse NCBI accessions...")
 404 |             enriched_fasta = fetch_fasta_for_accessions(accessions_to_enrich)
 405 |             if enriched_fasta:
 406 |                 enriched_seqs = _parse_fasta_sequences(enriched_fasta)
 407 |                 # Create a lookup table using the base accession (no version)
 408 |                 enriched_map = { s['name'].split()[0].split('.')[0]: s for s in enriched_seqs if s['name'].strip() }
 409 |                 
 410 |                 # Replace sparse Mycomap sequences with our highly-detailed XML headers and sequences
 411 |                 for seq in sequences:
 412 |                     parts = seq['name'].split()
 413 |                     if len(parts) == 1:
 414 |                         base_acc = parts[0].split('.')[0]
 415 |                         if base_acc in enriched_map:
 416 |                             seq['name'] = enriched_map[base_acc]['name']
 417 |                             seq['sequence'] = enriched_map[base_acc]['sequence']
 418 |                             
 419 |         # Apply cleaning to all sequences and filter out empty results
 420 |         original_count = len(sequences)
 421 |         for seq in sequences:
 422 |             seq['sequence'] = clean_dna_sequence(seq['sequence'])
 423 |         sequences = [s for s in sequences if s['sequence']]
 424 |         dropped_count = original_count - len(sequences)
 425 | 
 426 |         # Build success message
 427 |         parts = []
 428 |         if include_ncbi:
 429 |             parts.append(f"{result['ncbi_count']} NCBI")
 430 |         if include_local:
 431 |             parts.append(f"{result['local_count']} local")
 432 |         msg = f"Fetched {' + '.join(parts)} sequences from Mycomap"
 433 |         
 434 |         # Report dropped sequences due to cleaning
 435 |         if dropped_count > 0:
 436 |             msg += f" ({dropped_count} dropped due to invalid/short sequences)"
 437 |         
 438 |         # Include warnings if there were non-fatal errors
 439 |         if result['errors']:
 440 |             msg += f" (warnings: {'; '.join(result['errors'])})"
 441 |         
 442 |         return jsonify({
 443 |             "status": "success",
 444 |             "sequences": sequences,
 445 |             "ncbi_count": result['ncbi_count'],
 446 |             "local_count": result['local_count'],
 447 |             "message": msg
 448 |         })
 449 |         
 450 |     except Exception as e:
 451 |         logger.error(f"Mycomap API error: {e}", exc_info=True)
 452 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": str(e)}), 500
 453 | 
 454 | 
 455 | @bp.route('/inaturalist', methods=['POST'])
 456 | def fetch_inaturalist():
 457 |     """
 458 |     Fetch DNA sequences from iNaturalist observations.
 459 |     
 460 |     Request: { 
 461 |         "url": "<iNaturalist URL>",
 462 |         "action": "analyze" | "fetch_sequences"
 463 |     }
 464 |     
 465 |     Response (analyze): {
 466 |         "status": "success",
 467 |         "total_observations": int,
 468 |         "dna_count": int,
 469 |         "provisional_species": [{"name": "...", "count": int}, ...],
 470 |         "is_single": bool,
 471 |         "can_blast": bool
 472 |     }
 473 |     
 474 |     Response (fetch_sequences): {
 475 |         "status": "success", 
 476 |         "sequences": [...],
 477 |         "message": "..."
 478 |     }
 479 |     """
 480 |     data = request.get_json() or {}
 481 |     url = data.get('url', '').strip()
 482 |     action = data.get('action', 'analyze').strip()
 483 |     
 484 |     # Input sanitization
 485 |     if not url:
 486 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "No URL provided"}), 400
 487 |     
 488 |     # Limit URL length to prevent abuse
 489 |     if len(url) > 2000:
 490 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "URL too long"}), 400
 491 |     
 492 |     # Validate action
 493 |     if action not in ('analyze', 'fetch_sequences'):
 494 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Invalid action"}), 400
 495 |     
 496 |     try:
 497 |         from app.services.inaturalist_service import (
 498 |             validate_inaturalist_url,
 499 |             fetch_inaturalist_data
 500 |         )
 501 |         
 502 |         # Validate URL format first
 503 |         url_info = validate_inaturalist_url(url)
 504 |         if not url_info:
 505 |             return jsonify({
 506 |                 "status": "error",
 507 |                 "error": "Invalid iNaturalist URL. Please enter a valid observation URL "
 508 |                          "(e.g., https://www.inaturalist.org/observations/12345) or "
 509 |                          "observations search URL."
 510 |             }), 400
 511 |         
 512 |         logger.info(f"iNaturalist API: Processing URL type={url_info['type']}, action={action}")
 513 |         
 514 |         if action == 'analyze':
 515 |             # Fetch and analyze observations (default mode='all' gets both ITS and PSN stats)
 516 |             result = fetch_inaturalist_data(url, mode='all')
 517 | 
 518 |             # Return analysis without full sequences
 519 |             # Defensive: check sequences list exists and has items before accessing
 520 |             sequences = result.get('sequences', [])
 521 |             seq = sequences[0]['sequence'] if sequences else None
 522 |             
 523 |             # Determine if this is a single result based on actual ITS data
 524 |             # Use total_its_observations if available, falling back to total_observations
 525 |             total_its = result.get('total_its_observations', result['total_observations'])
 526 |             
 527 |             # is_single reflects the URL type (single observation URL)
 528 |             is_single_url = result.get('is_single', False)
 529 |             
 530 |             # can_blast requires exactly one usable ITS sequence found
 531 |             can_blast = bool(total_its == 1 and result['dna_count'] == 1 and len(sequences) == 1 and seq)
 532 |             
 533 |             return jsonify({
 534 |                 "status": "success",
 535 |                 "total_observations": result['total_observations'],
 536 |                 "total_its_observations": result.get('total_its_observations', 0),
 537 |                 "total_psn_observations": result.get('total_psn_observations', 0),
 538 |                 "fetched_its_count": result.get('fetched_its_count', 0),
 539 |                 "fetched_psn_count": result.get('fetched_psn_count', 0),
 540 |                 "dna_count": result['dna_count'],
 541 |                 "provisional_species": result['provisional_species'],
 542 |                 "is_single": is_single_url,
 543 |                 "can_blast": can_blast,
 544 |                 # For single obs with DNA, include the sequence for BLAST
 545 |                 "sequence": seq if can_blast else None,
 546 |                 "truncated": result.get('truncated', False),
 547 |                 "total_available": result.get('total_available', 0),
 548 |                 "message": f"Found {result['dna_count']} observation(s) with DNA Barcode ITS"
 549 |             })
 550 |         else:
 551 |             # Optimize: Only fetch ITS sequences for queue adding (skip PSN stats)
 552 |             result = fetch_inaturalist_data(url, mode='its_only')
 553 |             
 554 |             # Return full sequences for queue
 555 |             return jsonify({
 556 |                 "status": "success",
 557 |                 "sequences": result['sequences'],
 558 |                 "truncated": result.get('truncated', False),
 559 |                 "total_available": result.get('total_available', 0),
 560 |                 "message": f"Fetched {len(result['sequences'])} DNA sequences from iNaturalist"
 561 |             })
 562 |         
 563 |     except ValueError as e:
 564 |         logger.warning(f"iNaturalist API validation error: {e}")
 565 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": str(e)}), 400
 566 |     except Exception as e:
 567 |         logger.error(f"iNaturalist API error: {e}", exc_info=True)
 568 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": str(e)}), 500
 569 |

 573 | @bp.route('/job', methods=['POST'])
 574 | def create_job():
 575 |     data = request.get_json() or {}
 576 |     
 577 |     # Extract Tree Params for Validation
 578 |     tree_method = data.get("tree_method", "nj")
 579 |     
 580 |     # Basic params
 581 |     job_params = {
 582 |         "input_type": data.get("input_type", "unknown"),
 583 |         "notes": data.get("notes", ""),
 584 |         "sequence": data.get("sequence", ""),
 585 |         "accessions": data.get("accessions", []),
 586 |         "alignment_method": data.get("alignment_method", "default"),
 587 |         "trimming_method": data.get("trimming_method", "none"),
 588 |         "alignment_options": data.get("alignment_options", {}),
 589 |         "tree_method": tree_method,
 590 |         "tree_model": data.get("tree_model", "GTR+G"),
 591 |         "bootstrap": data.get("bootstrap", 1000), # Legacy field
 592 |         "mcmc_generations": data.get("mcmc_generations", 50000),
 593 |         "mcmc_nruns": data.get("mcmc_nruns", 2),
 594 |         "mcmc_nchains": data.get("mcmc_nchains", 4),
 595 |         
 596 |         # New RAxML-NG params - Extract raw first
 597 |         "run_preset": data.get("run_preset", "fast_good"),
 598 |         "bootstrap_preset": data.get("bootstrap_preset", "standard"),
 599 |         "bootstrap_cap": data.get("bootstrap_cap"),
 600 |         "enable_bootstrap": data.get("enable_bootstrap", True),
 601 |         "start_tree_override": data.get("start_tree_override"),
 602 |         "moose_enabled": data.get("moose_enabled", False),
 603 |         "early_stopping": data.get("early_stopping", False),
 604 |         "seed": data.get("seed"),
 605 |         "outgroup": data.get("outgroup")
 606 |     }
 607 | 
 608 |     # Validate/Clamp RAxML params if method is raxml
 609 |     if tree_method == 'raxml':
 610 |         try:
 611 |             from app.services.raxml_validator import validate_and_resolve_raxml_params
 612 |             
 613 |             # Use data_type assumption (can't fully detect here without MSA, assume DNA for initial clamp or check sequence?)
 614 |             # For now default to DNA validation rules, the worker does final check.
 615 |             # But the validator mainly checks Integers which are agnostic.
 616 |             resolved = validate_and_resolve_raxml_params(job_params, data_type="DNA")
 617 |             
 618 |             # overwrite with safe values to ensure what is stored/shown matches execution
 619 |             job_params.update({
 620 |                 "bootstrap_cap": resolved.bootstrap_cap,
 621 |                 "enable_bootstrap": resolved.enable_bootstrap,
 622 |                 "seed": resolved.seed,
 623 |                 "outgroup": resolved.outgroup,
 624 |                 "moose_enabled": resolved.enable_moose, 
 625 |                 "early_stopping": resolved.enable_early_stopping
 626 |             })
 627 |             
 628 |             # Store validation warnings separately
 629 |             if resolved.warnings:
 630 |                 job_params['validation_warnings'] = resolved.warnings
 631 |                 
 632 |         except Exception as e:
 633 |             logger.error(f"Validation error: {e}")
 634 |             # Continue but verify in worker
 635 |             
 636 |     try:
 637 |         job_id = enqueue_job(job_params)
 638 |         
 639 |         # Create DB record
 640 |         job_record = Job(
 641 |             id=job_id,
 642 |             status="queued",
 643 |             job_dir=str(Config.JOB_DIR / job_id),
 644 |             input_type=job_params["input_type"],
 645 |             metrics={
 646 |                 "tree_method": job_params["tree_method"],
 647 |                 "notes": job_params["notes"],
 648 |                 "alignment_method": job_params["alignment_method"],
 649 |                 "trimming_method": job_params["trimming_method"],
 650 |                 "run_preset": job_params.get("run_preset"),
 651 |                 "bootstrap_cap": job_params.get("bootstrap_cap")
 652 |             }
 653 |         )
 654 |         
 655 |         if current_user.is_authenticated:
 656 |             job_record.user_id = current_user.id
 657 |             
 658 |         db.session.add(job_record)
 659 |         db.session.commit()
 660 |         
 661 |         return jsonify({"status": "queued", "job_id": job_id}), 202
 662 |     except Exception as e:
 663 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "message": str(e)}), 500
 664 | 
 665 | @bp.route('/job/<job_id>/make_editable_copy', methods=['POST'])
 666 | def make_editable_copy(job_id):
 667 |     """
 668 |     Create a new owned job from an existing job's input.
 669 |     """
 670 |     # 1. Check view access to source (anyone can copy if they can view)
 671 |     db_job, error_msg, status_code = check_job_access(job_id, mode="view")
 672 |     if error_msg:
 673 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": error_msg}), status_code
 674 |         
 675 |     # 2. (Authentication Removed) - Anonymous copies allowed
 676 |     # if not current_user.is_authenticated:
 677 |     #     return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Login required to create a copy."}), 401
 678 |     
 679 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id
 680 |     
 681 |     # 3. Locate source FASTA
 682 |     # Try raw input first
 683 |     source_fasta = job_dir / "input" / "input_raw.fasta"
 684 |     if not source_fasta.exists():
 685 |         # Fallback? Maybe aligned?
 686 |         # Let's try grabbing from sequences (unlikely to be reconstituted easily if just alignment exists)
 687 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Source FASTA (input_raw.fasta) not found."}), 404
 688 |     
 689 |     try:
 690 |         fasta_content = source_fasta.read_text()
 691 |         
 692 |         # 4. Create new job params (Rapid defaults)
 693 |         # Replicating "Quick Tree" defaults: MAFFT + FastTree + GTR+G
 694 |         job_params = {
 695 |             "input_type": "fasta",
 696 |             "notes": f"Copy of Job {job_id}",
 697 |             "sequence": fasta_content,
 698 |             "accessions": [],
 699 |             "alignment_method": "mafft",
 700 |             "trimming_method": "none",
 701 |             "alignment_options": {},
 702 |             "tree_method": "fasttree",
 703 |             "tree_model": "GTR+G",
 704 |             "bootstrap": 1000,
 705 |             "mcmc_generations": 50000
 706 |         }
 707 |         
 708 |         # 5. Enqueue
 709 |         new_job_id = enqueue_job(job_params)
 710 |         
 711 |         # 6. Create DB Record
 712 |         new_job = Job(
 713 |             id=new_job_id,
 714 |             status="queued",
 715 |             job_dir=str(Config.JOB_DIR / new_job_id),
 716 |             input_type="fasta",
 717 |             # user_id assigned below
 718 |             metrics={
 719 |                 "tree_method": job_params["tree_method"],
 720 |                 "notes": job_params["notes"],
 721 |                 "alignment_method": job_params["alignment_method"],
 722 |                 "trimming_method": job_params["trimming_method"],
 723 |                 "copied_from": job_id
 724 |             }
 725 |         )
 726 |         
 727 |         if current_user.is_authenticated:
 728 |             new_job.user_id = current_user.id
 729 |         
 730 |         db.session.add(new_job)
 731 |         db.session.commit()
 732 |         
 733 |         return jsonify({

 924 |     _, error_msg, status_code = check_job_access(job_id, mode="edit")

 940 |                 params_dict = json.load(f)
 941 |

 954 |         )

 965 |             bootstrap_preset=params_dict.get("bootstrap_preset", "standard"),
 966 |             bootstrap_cap=params_dict.get("bootstrap_cap"),
 967 |             enable_bootstrap=params_dict.get("enable_bootstrap", True),

1020 |     response.headers["Pragma"] = "no-cache"

1142 | # SSE Real-Time Events Endpoint
1143 | # =============================================================================
1144 | 
1145 | def _read_log_tail(log_path, max_bytes=65536, max_lines=200):
1146 |     """
1147 |     Read the last N lines from a log file efficiently.
1148 |     
1149 |     Reads last max_bytes of file, splits into lines, returns last max_lines.
1150 |     """
1151 |     try:
1152 |         if not log_path.exists():
1153 |             return []
1154 |         
1155 |         file_size = log_path.stat().st_size
1156 |         
1157 |         with open(log_path, 'r', encoding='utf-8', errors='replace') as f:
1158 |             if file_size > max_bytes:
1159 |                 f.seek(file_size - max_bytes)
1160 |                 # Skip partial first line
1161 |                 f.readline()
1162 |             
1163 |             lines = f.readlines()
1164 |         
1165 |         # Strip and return last max_lines
1166 |         return [line.rstrip() for line in lines[-max_lines:]]
1167 |     
1168 |     except Exception as e:
1169 |         logger.warning(f"Failed to read log tail from {log_path}: {e}")
1170 |         return []
1171 | 
1172 | 
1173 | def _build_snapshot(job_id: str) -> dict:
1174 |     """
1175 |     Build initial snapshot for SSE connection.
1176 |     
1177 |     Includes job status, meta, and log tails.
1178 |     """
1179 |     import time
1180 |     from datetime import datetime
1181 |     
1182 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id
1183 |     
1184 |     # Get RQ job status
1185 |     from app.workers.queue import get_job_status
1186 |     rq_status = get_job_status(job_id)
1187 |     
1188 |     # Get DB job for additional info
1189 |     db_job = Job.query.get(job_id)
1190 |     
1191 |     # Determine status
1192 |     status = rq_status.get('status', 'unknown')
1193 |     if db_job and db_job.status:
1194 |         # DB status is authoritative for completed/failed
1195 |         if db_job.status in ('completed', 'failed'):
1196 |             status = db_job.status
1197 |     
1198 |     # Normalize: RQ uses 'finished', we use 'completed'
1199 |     if status == 'finished':
1200 |         status = 'completed'
1201 |     
1202 |     # Build job info
1203 |     job_info = {
1204 |         "id": job_id,
1205 |         "status": status,
1206 |         "started_at": rq_status.get('started_at'),
1207 |         "ended_at": rq_status.get('ended_at'),
1208 |         "elapsed_seconds": None,
1209 |         "error_summary": None,
1210 |         "failed_step": None,
1211 |         "failed_step_label": None,
1212 |         "tool": None,
1213 |         "exit_code": None,
1214 |         "result_files": None,
1215 |         "meta": {},
1216 |     }
1217 |     
1218 |     # Calculate elapsed time
1219 |     if rq_status.get('started_at'):
1220 |         try:
1221 |             started = datetime.fromisoformat(rq_status['started_at'].replace('Z', '+00:00'))
1222 |             if rq_status.get('ended_at'):
1223 |                 ended = datetime.fromisoformat(rq_status['ended_at'].replace('Z', '+00:00'))
1224 |                 job_info["elapsed_seconds"] = (ended - started).total_seconds()
1225 |             else:
1226 |                 from datetime import timezone
1227 |                 now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
1228 |                 job_info["elapsed_seconds"] = (now - started).total_seconds()
1229 |         except Exception:
1230 |             pass
1231 |     
1232 |     # Get RQ job meta
1233 |     try:
1234 |         from app.workers.queue import get_queue
1235 |         q = get_queue()
1236 |         rq_job = q.fetch_job(job_id)
1237 |         if rq_job and rq_job.meta:
1238 |             job_info["meta"] = rq_job.meta
1239 |     except Exception:
1240 |         pass
1241 |     
1242 |     # If job failed, extract failure info
1243 |     if status == 'failed':
1244 |         if db_job and db_job.metrics:
1245 |             job_info["error_summary"] = db_job.metrics.get('error')
1246 |             job_info["failed_step"] = db_job.metrics.get('failed_step')
1247 |         
1248 |         # Try to get more from RQ result
1249 |         result = rq_status.get('result', {})
1250 |         if isinstance(result, dict):
1251 |             job_info["error_summary"] = job_info["error_summary"] or result.get('error')
1252 |             
1253 |         # Get failed step label from meta
1254 |         failed_step = job_info.get("failed_step")
1255 |         if failed_step and "steps" in job_info["meta"]:
1256 |             step_info = job_info["meta"]["steps"].get(failed_step, {})
1257 |             job_info["failed_step_label"] = step_info.get("label", failed_step)
1258 |             job_info["tool"] = step_info.get("tool")
1259 |     
1260 |     # If job completed, include result files
1261 |     if status == 'completed':
1262 |         job_info["result_files"] = {
1263 |             "tree_newick": f"/api/job/{job_id}/download/tree/newick",
1264 |             "tree_nexus": f"/api/job/{job_id}/download/tree/nexus",
1265 |             "fasta_original": f"/api/job/{job_id}/download/fasta/original",
1266 |         }
1267 |     
1268 |     # Read log tails (generous limits to capture most output for completed jobs)
1269 |     logs_dir = job_dir / "logs"
1270 |     log_tails = {
1271 |         "pipeline": _read_log_tail(logs_dir / "pipeline.log", max_lines=500),
1272 |         "alignment": _read_log_tail(logs_dir / "alignment.log", max_lines=500),
1273 |         "tree_builder": _read_log_tail(logs_dir / "tree_builder.log", max_lines=1000),
1274 |     }
1275 |     
1276 |     return {
1277 |         "job": job_info,
1278 |         "log_tails": log_tails,
1279 |     }
1280 | 
1281 | 
1282 | @bp.route('/job/<job_id>/events', methods=['GET'])
1283 | def job_events_stream(job_id):
1284 |     """
1285 |     SSE endpoint for real-time job status updates.
1286 |     
1287 |     Protocol:
1288 |     - Emits `event: snapshot` with initial job state and log tails
1289 |     - Emits plain `data:` lines for all subsequent live events
1290 |     - Emits `event: ping` with `data: {}` every 15s as keepalive
1291 |     
1292 |     On disconnect/reconnect, server sends fresh snapshot.
1293 |     """
1294 |     import json
1295 |     import time
1296 |     import redis
1297 |     from flask import Response, stream_with_context
1298 |     
1299 |     # Check authorization
1300 |     if not validate_job_id(job_id):
1301 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Invalid job ID format"}), 400
1302 | 
1303 |     db_job, error_msg, status_code = check_job_access(job_id)
1304 |     if error_msg:
1305 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": error_msg}), status_code
1306 |     
1307 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id
1308 |     
1309 |     def generate():
1310 |         # Connect to Redis for PubSub
1311 |         redis_url = Config.REDIS_URL
1312 |         r = redis.from_url(redis_url)
1313 |         pubsub = r.pubsub()
1314 |         
1315 |         channel = f"job:{job_id}:events"
1316 |         pubsub.subscribe(channel)
1317 |         
1318 |         try:
1319 |             # Send initial snapshot
1320 |             snapshot = _build_snapshot(job_id)
1321 |             yield f"event: snapshot\ndata: {json.dumps(snapshot)}\n\n"
1322 |             
1323 |             # Check if job is already terminal
1324 |             job_status = snapshot["job"]["status"]
1325 |             if job_status in ('completed', 'failed'):
1326 |                 # Still keep connection open briefly for any final events
1327 |                 pass
1328 |             
1329 |             # Throttle timers (use monotonic clock for reliable intervals)
1330 |             last_ping = time.monotonic()
1331 |             last_db_poll = 0.0  # Start at 0 to trigger immediate first poll
1332 |             
1333 |             # Tunable interval for DB polling (seconds)
1334 |             DB_POLL_INTERVAL = 1.0
1335 |             
1336 |             while True:
1337 |                 # Check for PubSub messages (non-blocking with short timeout)
1338 |                 # Use shorter timeout to allow responsive loop with brief sleep
1339 |                 message = pubsub.get_message(timeout=0.1)
1340 |                 
1341 |                 if message and message['type'] == 'message':
1342 |                     data = message['data']
1343 |                     if isinstance(data, bytes):
1344 |                         data = data.decode('utf-8')
1345 |                     yield f"data: {data}\n\n"
1346 |                     
1347 |                     # Check if this is a terminal event
1348 |                     try:
1349 |                         event = json.loads(data)
1350 |                         if event.get('type') == 'job_state' and event.get('status') in ('completed', 'failed'):
1351 |                             # Send final event and close after brief delay
1352 |                             time.sleep(0.5)
1353 |                             break
1354 |                     except json.JSONDecodeError:
1355 |                         pass
1356 |                 
1357 |                 now = time.monotonic()
1358 |                 
1359 |                 # Send keepalive ping every 15 seconds
1360 |                 if now - last_ping >= 15:
1361 |                     yield "event: ping\ndata: {}\n\n"
1362 |                     last_ping = now
1363 |                 
1364 |                 # Poll DB for job status at most once per DB_POLL_INTERVAL
1365 |                 if job_status not in ('completed', 'failed'):
1366 |                     if now - last_db_poll >= DB_POLL_INTERVAL:
1367 |                         last_db_poll = now
1368 |                         db.session.expire_all()
1369 |                         db_job_check = Job.query.get(job_id)
1370 |                         logger.debug(f"SSE DB poll for job {job_id}: status={db_job_check.status if db_job_check else 'None'}")
1371 |                         if db_job_check and db_job_check.status in ('completed', 'failed'):
1372 |                             job_status = db_job_check.status
1373 |                             # Give a moment for final events from Redis
1374 |                             time.sleep(1)
1375 |                             break
1376 |                 
1377 |                 # Brief sleep to prevent CPU spin (50-100ms effective with pubsub timeout)
1378 |                 time.sleep(0.05)
1379 |         
1380 |         finally:
1381 |             pubsub.unsubscribe()
1382 |             pubsub.close()
1383 |     
1384 |     response = Response(
1385 |         stream_with_context(generate()),
1386 |         mimetype='text/event-stream',
1387 |         headers={
1388 |             'Cache-Control': 'no-cache',
1389 |             'X-Accel-Buffering': 'no',  # Disable nginx buffering
1390 |             'Connection': 'keep-alive',
1391 |         }
1392 |     )
1393 |     return response
1394 | 
1395 | 
1396 | # =============================================================================
1397 | # Log Download Endpoints
1398 | # =============================================================================
1399 |

1402 |     """

1405 |     Valid log_name values:

1407 |     - alignment: alignment.log  
1408 |     - tree_builder: tree_builder.log
1409 |     """
1410 |     if not validate_job_id(job_id):
1411 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Invalid job ID format"}), 400
1412 | 
1413 |     # Check authorization
1414 |     _, error_msg, status_code = check_job_access(job_id)
1415 |     if error_msg:
1416 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": error_msg}), status_code
1417 |     
1418 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id

1435 |     if not validate_safe_file_path(log_path, job_dir / "logs"):
1436 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Log file not found or invalid"}), 404
1437 |     
1438 |     return send_file(
1439 |         log_path,
1440 |         as_attachment=True,
1441 |         download_name=f"{job_id}_{log_files[log_name]}"
1442 |     )

1444 | @bp.route('/log/client', methods=['POST'])
1445 | def log_client_error():
1446 |     """
1447 |     Log client-side errors to the server log.
1448 |     Expected JSON: { "message": "...", "stack": "...", "url": "...", "context": "..." }
1449 |     """
1450 |     from flask import current_app
1451 |     
1452 |     data = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}
1453 |     # Apply size limits to prevent log spam
1454 |     msg = (data.get("message") or "Unknown client error")[:2000]
1455 |     stack = (data.get("stack") or "")[:20000]
1456 |     context = (data.get("context") or "")[:1000]
1457 |     # Sanitize inputs to prevent log injection
1458 |     msg = msg.replace('\n', ' ').replace('\r', ' ')
1459 |     stack = stack.replace('\n', ' ').replace('\r', ' ')
1460 |     context = context.replace('\n', ' ').replace('\r', ' ')
1461 |     url = (data.get("url") or "")[:500].replace('\n', '').replace('\r', '')
1462 |     
1463 |     # Request metadata for debugging
1464 |     client_ip = request.headers.get("X-Forwarded-For", request.remote_addr) or "unknown"
1465 |     user_agent = (request.headers.get("User-Agent") or "")[:200]
1466 |     
1467 |     # Format log message with metadata
1468 |     log_msg = f"Client Error: {msg}"
1469 |     log_msg += f" [IP: {client_ip}]"
1470 |     if url:
1471 |         log_msg += f" [URL: {url}]"
1472 |     if context:
1473 |         log_msg += f" [Context: {context}]"
1474 |     if user_agent:
1475 |         log_msg += f" [UA: {user_agent}]"
1476 |     if stack:
1477 |         log_msg += f"\nStack: {stack}"
1478 |         
1479 |     current_app.logger.error(log_msg)
1480 |     return jsonify({"status": "logged"}), 200

1482 | @bp.route('/job/<job_id>/sequences/add', methods=['POST'])
1483 | def add_sequences_to_job(job_id):
1484 |     """
1485 |     Add sequences to an existing job's input file.
1486 |     
1487 |     Request: { "input": "<fasta or accession list>" }
1488 |     Response: { "status": "success", "count": int, "message": "..." }
1489 |     """
1490 |     # Check authorization
1491 |     _, error_msg, status_code = check_job_access(job_id)
1492 |     if error_msg:
1493 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": error_msg}), status_code
1494 |     
1495 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id
1496 |     if not job_dir.exists():
1497 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "Job not found"}), 404
1498 |         
1499 |     data = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}
1500 |     input_text = data.get("input", "").strip()
1501 |     
1502 |     if not input_text:
1503 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "No input provided"}), 400
1504 | 
1505 |     # 1. Raw Input Limit (e.g. 200KB characters)
1506 |     MAX_INPUT_CHARS = 200_000
1507 |     if len(input_text) > MAX_INPUT_CHARS:
1508 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": f"Input too large (max {MAX_INPUT_CHARS} chars)"}), 400
1509 |         
1510 |     try:
1511 |         from app.services.blast_service import fetch_fasta_for_accessions
1512 |         
1513 |         sequences_to_add = []
1514 |         is_accession_input = False
1515 |         
1516 |         # Heuristic: Check if input looks like a list of accessions (no > at start, short lines)
1517 |         first_line = input_text.splitlines()[0].strip()
1518 |         if not first_line.startswith(">"):
1519 |             # Assume accessions
1520 |             is_accession_input = True
1521 |             
1522 |             # Split by commas, spaces, newlines
1523 |             tokens = re.split(r'[,\s]+', input_text)
1524 |             accessions = [t.strip() for t in tokens if t.strip()]
1525 |             
1526 |             # Security: Limit number of accessions to prevent abuse
1527 |             MAX_ACCESSIONS = 200
1528 |             if len(accessions) > MAX_ACCESSIONS:
1529 |                  return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": f"Too many accessions (max {MAX_ACCESSIONS})"}), 400
1530 |             
1531 |             if accessions:
1532 |                 logger.info(f"Adding sequences from accessions: {accessions}")
1533 |                 fasta_content = fetch_fasta_for_accessions(accessions)
1534 |                 sequences_to_add = _parse_fasta_sequences(fasta_content)
1535 |         else:
1536 |             # Assume FASTA
1537 |             sequences_to_add = _parse_fasta_sequences(input_text)
1538 |             
1539 |         # Filter out sequences with empty sequence data
1540 |         sequences_to_add = [s for s in sequences_to_add if s.get('sequence', '').strip()]
1541 |         
1542 |         if not sequences_to_add:
1543 |             return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": "No valid sequences found in input"}), 400
1544 | 
1545 |         # 2. Sequence Count Limit
1546 |         MAX_SEQUENCES_TO_ADD = 500
1547 |         if len(sequences_to_add) > MAX_SEQUENCES_TO_ADD:
1548 |             return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": f"Too many sequences (max {MAX_SEQUENCES_TO_ADD})"}), 400
1549 |             
1550 |         # 3. Total Base Pair Limit
1551 |         MAX_TOTAL_BP_TO_ADD = 2_000_000
1552 |         total_bp = sum(len(s['sequence']) for s in sequences_to_add)
1553 |         if total_bp > MAX_TOTAL_BP_TO_ADD:
1554 |              return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": f"Total sequence length too large (max {MAX_TOTAL_BP_TO_ADD} bp)"}), 400
1555 |              
1556 |         # Append to input_raw.fasta
1557 |         input_path = job_dir / "input" / "input_raw.fasta"
1558 |         
1559 |         # Read existing IDs to check for duplicates
1560 |         existing_ids = set()
1561 |         if input_path.exists():
1562 |             try:
1563 |                 existing_seqs = _parse_fasta_sequences(input_path.read_text())
1564 |                 for s in existing_seqs:
1565 |                     sid, _ = _split_fasta_header(s['name'])
1566 |                     if sid:
1567 |                         existing_ids.add(sid)
1568 |             except Exception as e:
1569 |                 logger.warning(f"Failed to parse existing FASTA for deduplication: {e}")
1570 |                 pass # Continue anyway - will allow duplicates but won't crash
1571 |             
1572 |         added_count = 0
1573 |         with open(input_path, "a") as f:
1574 |             # Ensure newline at end of file before appending
1575 |             if input_path.stat().st_size > 0:
1576 |                 f.write("\n")
1577 |                  
1578 |             for seq in sequences_to_add:
1579 |                 # Sanitize header: remove control chars including \0
1580 |                 raw_header = seq.get('name', '')
1581 |                 # Keep only printable chars and tab
1582 |                 sanitized_header = "".join(ch for ch in raw_header if ord(ch) >= 32 or ch == '\t')
1583 |                 
1584 |                 # Split header to dedupe by ID properly
1585 |                 seq_id, rest = _split_fasta_header(sanitized_header)
1586 |                 
1587 |                 # Cap header lengths to prevent abuse (e.g., 200KB pasted headers)
1588 |                 MAX_SEQ_ID_LEN = 100
1589 |                 MAX_DESC_LEN = 300
1590 |                 seq_id = seq_id[:MAX_SEQ_ID_LEN]
1591 |                 rest = rest[:MAX_DESC_LEN]
1592 |                 
1593 |                 if not seq_id:
1594 |                     # Generate a unique ID if header is missing
1595 |                     seq_id = f"Sequence_{added_count + 1}"
1596 |                     
1597 |                 # Make unique ID
1598 |                 new_id = _make_unique_id(seq_id, existing_ids)
1599 |                 
1600 |                 # Reconstruct header
1601 |                 new_header = f"{new_id} {rest}".strip()
1602 |                 
1603 |                 f.write(f">{new_header}\n{seq['sequence']}\n")
1604 |                 added_count += 1
1605 |                     
1606 |         return jsonify({
1607 |             "status": "success", 
1608 |             "count": added_count,
1609 |             "message": f"Added {added_count} sequences."
1610 |         })
1611 |         
1612 |     except Exception as e:
1613 |         logger.error(f"Failed to add sequences: {e}", exc_info=True)
1614 |         return jsonify({"status": "error", "error": str(e)}), 500

  19 |

  60 | @bp.route('/job/<job_id>/view')
  61 | def job_viewer(job_id):
  62 |     # Check access control (View Mode)
  63 |     db_job, error_msg, status_code = check_job_access(job_id, mode="view")
  64 |     if error_msg:
  65 |         # Check specific status codes to provide better UX/privacy
  66 |         if status_code in (401, 403):
  67 |             # Privacy: don't reveal existence of protected jobs
  68 |             abort(404)
  69 |         # Default abort for other errors (e.g. 400)
  70 |         abort(status_code)
  71 | 
  72 |     # Determine View-Only status logic
  73 |     # view_only = True if the job has an owner and the current user is NOT that owner.
  74 |     # Legacy/Anonymous jobs (user_id=None) remain mutable by public (view_only=False).
  75 |     view_only = False
  76 |     if db_job and db_job.user_id is not None:
  77 |         if not current_user.is_authenticated or current_user.id != db_job.user_id:
  78 |             view_only = True
  79 | 
  80 |     # Fetch job details for display
  81 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id
  82 |     input_info_path = job_dir / "input_info.json"
  83 |     
  84 |     job_details = {}
  85 |     if validate_safe_file_path(input_info_path, job_dir):
  86 |         try:
  87 |             with open(input_info_path, 'r') as f:
  88 |                 job_details = json.load(f)
  89 |         except Exception:
  90 |             pass
  91 |             
  92 |     return render_template('job_viewer.html', job_id=job_id, job_details=job_details, view_only=view_only)

 104 | @bp.route('/todo', methods=['GET', 'POST'])
 105 | @csrf.exempt
 106 | @limiter.limit("10 per minute")
 107 | def todo():
 108 |     todo_file = os.path.join(current_app.root_path, 'static', 'todos.txt')
 109 |     MAX_FILE_SIZE = 5 * 1024 * 1024  # 5 MB
 110 |     
 111 |     if request.method == 'POST':
 112 |         # Ensure static directory exists only on POST
 113 |         os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(todo_file), exist_ok=True)
 114 |         
 115 |         name = request.form.get('name', '')
 116 |         suggestion = request.form.get('suggestion', '')
 117 | 
 118 |         # Trim leading/trailing whitespace
 119 |         name = name.strip()
 120 |         suggestion = suggestion.strip()
 121 | 
 122 |         # Input caps BEFORE sanitizing
 123 |         name = name[:60]
 124 |         suggestion = suggestion[:1000]
 125 | 
 126 |         # Sanitize input: allow only alphanumeric, spaces, and some punctuation, including Spanish characters, /, and :
 127 |         name = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z0-9 ./,:!?\'\-áéíóúüÁÉÍÓÚÜñÑ]', '', name)
 128 |         suggestion = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z0-9 ./,:!?\'\-áéíóúüÁÉÍÓÚÜñÑ]', '', suggestion)
 129 | 
 130 |         # Collapse repeated whitespace inside the string to single spaces
 131 |         name = re.sub(r'\s+', ' ', name)
 132 |         suggestion = re.sub(r'\s+', ' ', suggestion)
 133 |         
 134 |         # Trim again
 135 |         name = name.strip()
 136 |         suggestion = suggestion.strip()
 137 | 
 138 |         # Re-apply final caps
 139 |         name = name[:60]
 140 |         suggestion = suggestion[:1000]
 141 | 
 142 |         # Only append if both are non-empty after sanitization
 143 |         if name and suggestion:
 144 |             try:
 145 |                 # File-size cap (5 MB)
 146 |                 if os.path.exists(todo_file) and os.path.getsize(todo_file) > MAX_FILE_SIZE:
 147 |                     # Fail closed: don't append if file is too large
 148 |                     pass
 149 |                 else:
 150 |                     with open(todo_file, 'a', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n') as f:
 151 |                         f.write(f'{name}: {suggestion}\n')
 152 |             except OSError:
 153 |                 # Handle OSError safely (fail closed)
 154 |                 pass
 155 |                 
 156 |         return redirect(url_for('main.todo'))
 157 | 
 158 |     todos = []
 159 |     if os.path.exists(todo_file):
 160 |         try:
 161 |             # Defense-in-depth: errors='replace' to avoid breaking on malformed byte sequences
 162 |             with open(todo_file, 'r', encoding='utf-8', errors='replace') as f:
 163 |                 # Read only last 200 lines to optimize memory usage
 164 |                 todos = list(deque((line.strip() for line in f), maxlen=200))
 165 |         except Exception:
 166 |             pass
 167 |     return render_template('todo.html', todos=todos)
