========================================================================================
FILE: app/monitoring/services.py
========================================================================================

   1 | import os
   2 | import time
   3 | import json
   4 | import psutil
   5 | from datetime import datetime, timedelta
   6 | from pathlib import Path
   7 | from flask import current_app
   8 | from app.extensions import db
   9 | from app.models import Job
  10 | 
  11 | def check_system_health():
  12 |     """
  13 |     Perform lightweight health checks.
  14 |     """
  15 |     health = {
  16 |         "status": "ok",
  17 |         "timestamp": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
  18 |         "components": {
  19 |             "database": "unknown",
  20 |             "filesystem": "unknown"
  21 |         }
  22 |     }
  23 |     
  24 |     # 1. Check Database
  25 |     try:
  26 |         # Simple query
  27 |         db.session.execute("SELECT 1")
  28 |         health["components"]["database"] = "ok"
  29 |     except Exception as e:
  30 |         health["status"] = "degraded"
  31 |         health["components"]["database"] = f"error: {str(e)}"
  32 |         
  33 |     # 2. Check Job Storage
  34 |     try:
  35 |         job_dir = Path(current_app.config["JOB_DIR"])
  36 |         if job_dir.exists() and os.access(job_dir, os.W_OK):
  37 |             health["components"]["filesystem"] = "ok"
  38 |         else:
  39 |             health["status"] = "degraded"
  40 |             health["components"]["filesystem"] = "error: not writable"
  41 |     except Exception as e:
  42 |          health["status"] = "degraded"
  43 |          health["components"]["filesystem"] = f"error: {str(e)}"
  44 | 
  45 |     return health
  46 | 
  47 | def get_worker_status():
  48 |     """
  49 |     Check worker heartbeats in /var/phylojobs/workers/
  50 |     """
  51 |     worker_dir = Path("/var/phylojobs/workers")
  52 |     # Windows fallback for dev
  53 |     if os.name == 'nt':
  54 |         worker_dir = Path(current_app.config.get("WORKER_DIR", "var/workers"))
  55 |         
  56 |     workers = []
  57 |     if not worker_dir.exists():
  58 |         return {"workers": [], "status": "no_workers_dir"}
  59 |         
  60 |     now = time.time()
  61 |     
  62 |     for hb_file in worker_dir.glob("*.heartbeat"):
  63 |         try:
  64 |             mtime = hb_file.stat().st_mtime
  65 |             worker_id = hb_file.stem
  66 |             age = now - mtime
  67 |             
  68 |             status = "healthy"
  69 |             if age > 60:
  70 |                 status = "stale"
  71 |             if age > 300:
  72 |                 status = "dead"
  73 |                 
  74 |             workers.append({
  75 |                 "id": worker_id,
  76 |                 "last_heartbeat": datetime.fromtimestamp(mtime).isoformat(),
  77 |                 "age_seconds": round(age, 1),
  78 |                 "status": status
  79 |             })
  80 |         except Exception:
  81 |             continue
  82 |             
  83 |     return {"workers": workers}
  84 | 
  85 | def get_global_metrics():
  86 |     """
  87 |     Aggregate global job metrics.
  88 |     """
  89 |     # Total jobs
  90 |     total_jobs = Job.query.count()
  91 |     
  92 |     # Jobs by status
  93 |     status_counts = {}
  94 |     from sqlalchemy import func
  95 |     rows = db.session.query(Job.status, func.count(Job.status)).group_by(Job.status).all()
  96 |     for status, count in rows:
  97 |         status_counts[status] = count
  98 |         
  99 |     # Jobs in last 24h
 100 |     since = datetime.utcnow() - timedelta(hours=24)
 101 |     recent_failed = Job.query.filter(Job.status == "error", Job.updated_at > since).count()
 102 |     
 103 |     return {
 104 |         "total_jobs": total_jobs,
 105 |         "status_counts": status_counts,
 106 |         "recent_failed_24h": recent_failed
 107 |     }
 108 | 
 109 | def collect_system_metrics():
 110 |     """
 111 |     Collect CPU, Memory, Disk usage.
 112 |     """
 113 |     return {
 114 |         "cpu_percent": psutil.cpu_percent(interval=None),
 115 |         "memory_percent": psutil.virtual_memory().percent,
 116 |         "disk_usage": psutil.disk_usage(current_app.config["JOB_DIR"]).percent,
 117 |         "timestamp": datetime.utcnow().isoformat()
 118 |     }


========================================================================================
FILE: app/services/access_control.py
========================================================================================

   1 | from typing import Optional, Tuple
   2 | from flask_login import current_user
   3 | from app.config import Config
   4 | from app.models import Job
   5 | from app.services.security_utils import validate_job_id
   6 | 
   7 | def check_job_access(job_id: str, mode: str = "view") -> Tuple[Optional[Job], Optional[str], int]:
   8 |     """
   9 |     Check if current user can access the given job.
  10 |     
  11 |     Args:
  12 |         job_id: The job ID to check.
  13 |         mode: "view" (default) or "edit".
  14 |         
  15 |     Returns:
  16 |         (db_job, error_message, status_code)
  17 |         
  18 |         If access is granted: (db_job, None, 200)
  19 |         If error: (None, error_message, status_code)
  20 |     """
  21 |     if not validate_job_id(job_id):
  22 |         return None, "Invalid job ID format", 400
  23 | 
  24 |     db_job = Job.query.get(job_id)
  25 |     job_dir = Config.JOB_DIR / job_id
  26 |     
  27 |     # Check if job exists (either in DB or on disk)
  28 |     if not db_job and not job_dir.exists():
  29 |         return None, "Job not found", 404
  30 |     
  31 |     if mode == "view":
  32 |         # Public View Access:
  33 |         # Anyone with the link (job_id) can view the tree.
  34 |         return db_job, None, 200
  35 | 
  36 |     elif mode == "edit":
  37 |         # Edit Access:
  38 |         # If job has an owner, ONLY that owner can edit.
  39 |         if db_job and db_job.user_id is not None:
  40 |             if not current_user.is_authenticated:
  41 |                 return None, "Authentication required to edit this job", 401
  42 |             if current_user.id != db_job.user_id:
  43 |                 return None, "You do not have permission to edit this job", 403
  44 |         
  45 |         # If job has no owner (db_job.user_id is None) OR (db_job is None but dir exists):
  46 |         # Allow anonymous edits (Legacy behavior for anonymous jobs)
  47 |         return db_job, None, 200
  48 | 
  49 |     return None, f"Invalid access mode: {mode}", 400


========================================================================================

   1 | """
   2 | Alignment service module.
   3 | 
   4 | Provides functions for running multiple sequence alignment using various tools:
   5 | - MAFFT
   6 | - MUSCLE
   7 | - Clustal Omega
   8 | - IQ-TREE (built-in alignment)
   9 | 
  10 | When job_id is provided, streams log output to Redis for real-time SSE updates.

  12 | 
  13 | import os
  14 | import shutil
  15 | from pathlib import Path

  23 | def run_alignment(
  24 |     input_fasta: Path,
  25 |     output_fasta: Path,
  26 |     params: AlignmentParams,
  27 |     config: Config,
  28 |     logger,
  29 |     job_id: Optional[str] = None
  30 | ) -> None:
  31 |     """
  32 |     Run a multiple sequence alignment according to user-selected or default parameters.
  33 |     
  34 |     Supported methods:
  35 |       - mafft
  36 |       - muscle
  37 |       - clustalo
  38 |       - iqtree_builtin
  39 |       - default (beginner mode)
  40 |     
  41 |     Args:
  42 |         input_fasta: Path to input FASTA file
  43 |         output_fasta: Path for output aligned FASTA
  44 |         params: Alignment parameters
  45 |         config: Application config
  46 |         logger: Logger instance
  47 |         job_id: Optional job ID for real-time event streaming
  48 |     """
  49 |     method = params.method.lower()
  50 |     
  51 |     if method == "default":
  52 |         # Beginner mode default: use configured default aligner (e.g. mafft)
  53 |         method = config.BEGINNER_DEFAULT_ALIGNER.lower()
  54 |         logger.info(f"Using default aligner: {method}")
  55 | 
  56 |     logger.info(f"Starting alignment with method: {method}")
  57 |     
  58 |     try:
  59 |         if method == "none":
  60 |             logger.info("Skipping alignment (method='none'). Copying input to output.")
  61 |             shutil.copy(input_fasta, output_fasta)
  62 |         elif method == "mafft":
  63 |             _run_mafft(input_fasta, output_fasta, params, config, logger, job_id)
  64 |         elif method == "muscle":
  65 |             _run_muscle(input_fasta, output_fasta, params, config, logger, job_id)
  66 |         elif method == "clustalo":
  67 |             _run_clustalo(input_fasta, output_fasta, params, config, logger, job_id)
  68 |         elif method == "iqtree_builtin":
  69 |             _run_iqtree_builtin(input_fasta, output_fasta, params, config, logger, job_id)
  70 |         else:
  71 |             raise ValueError(f"Unsupported alignment method: {method}")
  72 |             
  73 |         if not output_fasta.exists() or output_fasta.stat().st_size == 0:
  74 |              raise RuntimeError(f"Alignment failed: Output file {output_fasta} is missing or empty.")
  75 | 
  76 |         logger.info(f"Alignment completed successfully. Output: {output_fasta}")
  77 | 
  78 |     except Exception as e:
  79 |         logger.error(f"Alignment failed: {e}")
  80 |         raise
  81 | 
  82 | 
  83 | def _get_thread_count():
  84 |     return min(8, os.cpu_count() or 1)
  85 | 
  86 | 
  87 | def _make_log_callback(job_id: Optional[str], step: str, stream: str):
  88 |     """Create a callback function for streaming output to Redis."""
  89 |     if not job_id:
  90 |         return None
  91 |     
  92 |     from app.workers.events import publish_log
  93 |     
  94 |     def callback(line: str):
  95 |         publish_log(job_id, step, stream, line)
  96 |     
  97 |     return callback

 100 | def _run_mafft(
 101 |     input_fasta: Path,
 102 |     output_fasta: Path,
 103 |     params: AlignmentParams,
 104 |     config: Config,
 105 |     logger,
 106 |     job_id: Optional[str] = None
 107 | ):
 108 |     """
 109 |     Run MAFFT alignment.
 110 |     
 111 |     MAFFT writes alignment to stdout, so we redirect stdout to file
 112 |     and stream stderr to Redis for progress updates.
 113 |     """
 114 |     threads = _get_thread_count()
 115 |     cmd = [config.MAFFT_BINARY, "--thread", "2", "--adjustdirectionaccurately"]
 116 |     
 117 |     # Check if this is a fast NJ tree build
 118 |     tree_method = params.advanced_options.get("tree_method", "").lower()
 119 |     
 120 |     # Add advanced options if any
 121 |     if params.advanced_options.get("auto", False):
 122 |          cmd.append("--auto")
 123 |     elif params.advanced_options.get("localpair", False):
 124 |          cmd.append("--localpair")
 125 |          cmd.append("--maxiterate")
 126 |          cmd.append("1000")
 127 |     elif params.advanced_options.get("globalpair", False):
 128 |          cmd.append("--globalpair")
 129 |          cmd.append("--maxiterate")
 130 |          cmd.append("1000")
 131 |     elif tree_method == "nj":
 132 |          # Fast settings for Neighbor-Joining quick tree
 133 |          cmd.extend(["--retree", "2", "--maxiterate", "2"])
 134 |     else:
 135 |          # Default reasonable option for ML/Bayesian trees
 136 |          cmd.append("--auto")
 137 | 
 138 |     cmd.append(str(input_fasta))
 139 |     
 140 |     log_file = output_fasta.parent.parent / "logs" / "alignment.log"
 141 |     
 142 |     if job_id:
 143 |         # Publish command line (displayed in green)
 144 |         from app.workers.events import publish_command
 145 |         publish_command(job_id, "align", cmd)
 146 |         
 147 |         # Use streaming runner: stdout → file, stderr → Redis + log
 148 |         exit_code, stats = run_command_streaming(
 149 |             cmd,
 150 |             stdout_path=output_fasta,  # MAFFT writes alignment to stdout
 151 |             stderr_path=log_file,
 152 |             on_stderr_line=_make_log_callback(job_id, "align", "stderr"),
 153 |         )
 154 |         
 155 |         if exit_code != 0:
 156 |             raise RuntimeError(f"MAFFT failed with exit code {exit_code}")
 157 |     else:
 158 |         # Fallback to non-streaming for backward compatibility
 159 |         returncode, stdout, stderr = run_command(cmd, log_file=log_file)
 160 |         
 161 |         if returncode != 0:
 162 |             raise RuntimeError(f"MAFFT failed with return code {returncode}. See logs.")
 163 |             
 164 |         with open(output_fasta, "w") as f:
 165 |             f.write(stdout)
 166 |

 243 | def _run_iqtree_builtin(
 244 |     input_fasta: Path,
 245 |     output_fasta: Path,
 246 |     params: AlignmentParams,
 247 |     config: Config,
 248 |     logger,
 249 |     job_id: Optional[str] = None
 250 | ):
 251 |     """
 252 |     Run IQ-TREE built-in alignment.
 253 |     
 254 |     Uses IQ-TREE's --align-only mode.
 255 |     """
 256 |     prefix = str(output_fasta.with_suffix(''))  # remove .fasta
 257 |     threads = _get_thread_count()
 258 |     
 259 |     cmd = [
 260 |         config.IQTREE_BINARY,
 261 |         "-s", str(input_fasta),
 262 |         "--align-only",
 263 |         "-nt", str(threads),
 264 |         "-pre", prefix,
 265 |         "-redo"  # Overwrite existing
 266 |     ]
 267 |     
 268 |     log_file = output_fasta.parent.parent / "logs" / "alignment.log"
 269 |     
 270 |     if job_id:
 271 |         # Publish command line (displayed in green)
 272 |         from app.workers.events import publish_command
 273 |         publish_command(job_id, "align", cmd)
 274 |         
 275 |         exit_code, stats = run_command_streaming(
 276 |             cmd,
 277 |             stderr_path=log_file,
 278 |             on_stderr_line=_make_log_callback(job_id, "align", "stderr"),
 279 |         )
 280 |         
 281 |         if exit_code != 0:
 282 |             raise RuntimeError(f"IQ-TREE alignment failed with exit code {exit_code}")
 283 |     else:
 284 |         returncode, stdout, stderr = run_command(cmd, log_file=log_file)
 285 |         
 286 |         if returncode != 0:
 287 |             raise RuntimeError(f"IQ-TREE alignment failed with return code {returncode}. See logs.")
 288 |     
 289 |     # IQ-TREE output file handling
 290 |     # Check for likely output files
 291 |     possible_outputs = [
 292 |         Path(f"{prefix}.fasta"),
 293 |         Path(f"{prefix}.fa"),
 294 |         Path(f"{prefix}.phy"),
 295 |         Path(f"{prefix}.nex")
 296 |     ]
 297 |     
 298 |     found = False
 299 |     for p in possible_outputs:
 300 |         if p.exists():
 301 |             if p != output_fasta:
 302 |                 import shutil
 303 |                 shutil.move(p, output_fasta)
 304 |             found = True
 305 |             break
 306 |             
 307 |     if not found:
 308 |         logger.warning("Could not locate IQ-TREE output file. Checking directory...")

FILE: app/services/blast_service.py

   1 | import hashlib
   2 | import json
   3 | import logging
   4 | import re
   5 | import time
   6 | import requests
   7 | import threading
   8 | import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
   9 | from pathlib import Path
  10 | from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple, Any
  11 | from app.config import Config
  12 | 
  13 | logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  14 | 
  15 | NCBI_BLAST_URL = "https://blast.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/Blast.cgi"
  16 | NCBI_EFETCH_URL = "https://eutils.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/entrez/eutils/efetch.fcgi"
  17 | 
  18 | # Global rate limiter state
  19 | _ncbi_lock = threading.Lock()
  20 | _last_request_time = 0.0
  21 | _MIN_REQUEST_GAP = 1.0 / 3.0  # At least 0.34s between requests (~3 req/s)
  22 | 
  23 | def _ncbi_request(method: str, url: str, **kwargs) -> requests.Response:
  24 |     """
  25 |     Helper to perform NCBI requests with:
  26 |     - Global rate limiting (min gap between requests)
  27 |     - User-Agent header
  28 |     - Retries with exponential backoff for suitable errors
  29 |     - Respect for Retry-After headers
  30 |     """
  31 |     global _last_request_time
  32 |     
  33 |     # 1. Apply Rate Limit
  34 |     with _ncbi_lock:
  35 |         now = time.monotonic()
  36 |         elapsed = now - _last_request_time
  37 |         if elapsed < _MIN_REQUEST_GAP:
  38 |             sleep_time = _MIN_REQUEST_GAP - elapsed
  39 |             time.sleep(sleep_time)
  40 |         _last_request_time = time.monotonic()
  41 | 
  42 |     # 2. Prepare headers
  43 |     headers = kwargs.get("headers", {})
  44 |     # Use a descriptive User-Agent
  45 |     if "User-Agent" not in headers:
  46 |         headers["User-Agent"] = f"Dikarya/1.0 ({Config.BLAST_EMAIL})"
  47 |     kwargs["headers"] = headers
  48 |     
  49 |     # 3. Execute with Retries
  50 |     # Retry on: Connection errors, Timeouts, HTTP 429, HTTP 5xx
  51 |     # Do NOT retry on: HTTP 4xx (except 429)
  52 |     max_retries = 5
  53 |     backoff = 1.0  # Start with 1s
  54 |     
  55 |     for attempt in range(max_retries):
  56 |         try:
  57 |             response = requests.request(method, url, **kwargs)
  58 |             
  59 |             # Check for 429 Too Many Requests
  60 |             if response.status_code == 429:
  61 |                 retry_after = response.headers.get("Retry-After")
  62 |                 if retry_after:
  63 |                     try:
  64 |                         wait_time = int(retry_after)
  65 |                     except ValueError:
  66 |                         wait_time = backoff
  67 |                 else:
  68 |                     wait_time = backoff
  69 |                 
  70 |                 logger.warning(f"NCBI 429 (Too Many Requests). Waiting {wait_time}s before retry {attempt + 1}/{max_retries}")
  71 |                 time.sleep(wait_time)
  72 |                 backoff *= 2 # Exponential backoff for next time if needed
  73 |                 continue
  74 |                 
  75 |             # Check for 5xx Server Errors
  76 |             if 500 <= response.status_code < 600:
  77 |                 logger.warning(f"NCBI {response.status_code} Error. Waiting {backoff}s before retry {attempt + 1}/{max_retries}")
  78 |                 time.sleep(backoff)
  79 |                 backoff *= 2
  80 |                 continue
  81 |             
  82 |             # For other statuses, letting raise_for_status handle or return response
  83 |             # Note: We return response here and let caller decide to raise for 4xx
  84 |             return response
  85 |             
  86 |         except (requests.ConnectionError, requests.Timeout) as e:
  87 |             logger.warning(f"NCBI Connection/Timeout error: {e}. Waiting {backoff}s before retry {attempt + 1}/{max_retries}")
  88 |             time.sleep(backoff)
  89 |             backoff *= 2
  90 |             
  91 |     # Final attempt (or if we ran out of retries)
  92 |     # If we exited loop, try one last time or re-raise
  93 |     raise requests.exceptions.RetryError(f"Max retries ({max_retries}) exceeded for NCBI request to {url}")
  94 | 
  95 | 
  96 | def blast_from_sequence(seq: str, config: Config, min_identity: float = 90.0, max_sequences: int = 50, logger=logger) -> Dict:
  97 |     """
  98 |     Perform remote BLAST using the given nucleotide sequence.
  99 |     Use caching if available.
 100 |     """
 101 |     query_hash = _hash_query(seq, min_identity, max_sequences)
 102 |     cached_result = _check_cache(query_hash, config)
 103 |     if cached_result:
 104 |         logger.info(f"BLAST cache hit for hash {query_hash}")
 105 |         return cached_result
 106 | 
 107 |     logger.info(f"BLAST cache miss for hash {query_hash}. Submitting to NCBI.")
 108 |     
 109 |     try:
 110 |         rid, rtoe = _submit_blast_request(seq, config, min_identity, max_sequences)
 111 |         logger.info(f"BLAST submitted. RID: {rid}, RTOE: {rtoe}")
 112 |         
 113 |         _poll_blast(rid, rtoe, config, logger)
 114 |         
 115 |         blast_result = _fetch_blast_results(rid)
 116 |         hit_accessions = blast_result.get("accessions", [])
 117 |         hit_details = blast_result.get("hit_details", [])
 118 |         logger.info(f"BLAST finished. Found {len(hit_accessions)} hits.")
 119 |         
 120 |         fasta_content = fetch_fasta_for_accessions(hit_accessions)
 121 |         logger.info(f"Downloaded FASTA for {len(hit_accessions)} accessions.")
 122 |         
 123 |         return _save_cache(query_hash, hit_accessions, hit_details, fasta_content, config)
 124 |         
 125 |     except Exception as e:
 126 |         logger.error(f"BLAST failed: {e}")
 127 |         # Re-raise to let caller handle failure (e.g. show user error)
 128 |         raise
 129 | 
 130 | def blast_from_accessions(accessions: List[str], config: Config, min_identity: float = 90.0, max_sequences: int = 50, logger=logger) -> Dict:
 131 |     """
 132 |     Retrieve sequences for the provided accessions.
 133 |     Then run remote BLAST using the combined sequence.
 134 |     Use caching.
 135 |     """
 136 |     # Sort to ensure stable hash
 137 |     sorted_accessions = sorted(accessions)
 138 |     query_str = ",".join(sorted_accessions)
 139 |     query_hash = _hash_query(query_str, min_identity, max_sequences)
 140 |     
 141 |     cached_result = _check_cache(query_hash, config)
 142 |     if cached_result:
 143 |         logger.info(f"BLAST cache hit for accessions hash {query_hash}")
 144 |         return cached_result
 145 | 
 146 |     logger.info(f"BLAST cache miss for accessions {query_hash}. Fetching sequences first.")
 147 |     
 148 |     try:
 149 |         # 1. Fetch initial sequences to use as query
 150 |         query_fasta = fetch_fasta_for_accessions(sorted_accessions)
 151 |         
 152 |         # 2. Run BLAST with these sequences
 153 |         rid, rtoe = _submit_blast_request(query_fasta, config, min_identity, max_sequences)
 154 |         logger.info(f"BLAST submitted. RID: {rid}, RTOE: {rtoe}")
 155 |         
 156 |         _poll_blast(rid, rtoe, config, logger)
 157 |         
 158 |         blast_result = _fetch_blast_results(rid)
 159 |         hit_accessions = blast_result.get("accessions", [])
 160 |         hit_details = blast_result.get("hit_details", [])
 161 |         logger.info(f"BLAST finished. Found {len(hit_accessions)} hits.")
 162 |         
 163 |         # Combine original accessions with hits to ensure we have everything
 164 |         all_accessions = list(set(sorted_accessions + hit_accessions))
 165 |         
 166 |         # Also add original accessions to hit_details if not present
 167 |         existing_accs = {h["accession"] for h in hit_details}
 168 |         for acc in sorted_accessions:
 169 |             if acc not in existing_accs:
 170 |                 hit_details.insert(0, {"accession": acc, "organism": "(query)"})
 171 |         
 172 |         fasta_content = fetch_fasta_for_accessions(all_accessions)
 173 |         logger.info(f"Downloaded FASTA for {len(all_accessions)} accessions.")
 174 |         
 175 |         return _save_cache(query_hash, all_accessions, hit_details, fasta_content, config)
 176 | 
 177 |     except Exception as e:
 178 |         logger.error(f"BLAST from accessions failed: {e}")
 179 |         raise
 180 | 
 181 | def _hash_query(query_str: str, min_identity: float = 90.0, max_sequences: int = 50) -> str:
 182 |     raw = f"{query_str}|{min_identity}|{max_sequences}"
 183 |     return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
 184 | 
 185 | def _check_cache(query_hash: str, config: Config) -> Optional[Dict]:
 186 |     # Validate hash format (SHA-256 = 64 hex chars)
 187 |     if not re.match(r'^[0-9a-f]{64}$', query_hash):
 188 |         return None
 189 |         
 190 |     cache_dir = config.BLAST_CACHE_DIR
 191 |     json_path = cache_dir / f"{query_hash}.json"
 192 |     fasta_path = cache_dir / f"{query_hash}.fasta"
 193 |     
 194 |     if json_path.exists() and fasta_path.exists():
 195 |         try:
 196 |             with open(json_path, 'r') as f:
 197 |                 metadata = json.load(f)
 198 |             return {
 199 |                 "cached": True,
 200 |                 "sequence_count": metadata["sequence_count"],
 201 |                 "hit_accessions": metadata["hit_accessions"],
 202 |                 "hit_details": metadata.get("hit_details", []),  # Include organism info
 203 |                 "fasta_path": str(fasta_path),
 204 |                 "metadata_path": str(json_path)
 205 |             }
 206 |         except Exception:
 207 |             return None
 208 |     return None
 209 | 
 210 | def _save_cache(query_hash: str, hit_accessions: List[str], hit_details: List[Dict], fasta_content: str, config: Config) -> Dict:
 211 |     cache_dir = config.BLAST_CACHE_DIR
 212 |     cache_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 213 |     
 214 |     json_path = cache_dir / f"{query_hash}.json"
 215 |     fasta_path = cache_dir / f"{query_hash}.fasta"
 216 |     
 217 |     metadata = {
 218 |         "query_hash": query_hash,
 219 |         "sequence_count": len(hit_accessions),
 220 |         "hit_accessions": hit_accessions,
 221 |         "hit_details": hit_details,  # Include organism info
 222 |         "timestamp": time.time()
 223 |     }
 224 |     
 225 |     with open(json_path, 'w') as f:
 226 |         json.dump(metadata, f, indent=2)
 227 |         
 228 |     with open(fasta_path, 'w') as f:
 229 |         f.write(fasta_content)
 230 |         
 231 |     return {
 232 |         "cached": False,
 233 |         "sequence_count": len(hit_accessions),
 234 |         "hit_accessions": hit_accessions,
 235 |         "hit_details": hit_details,
 236 |         "fasta_path": str(fasta_path),
 237 |         "metadata_path": str(json_path)
 238 |     }
 239 | 
 240 | def _submit_blast_request(seq: str, config: Config = None, min_identity: float = 90.0, max_sequences: int = 50) -> Tuple[str, int]:
 241 |     """Submit a BLAST request to NCBI and return RID and estimated wait time."""
 242 |     from app.config import Config as DefaultConfig
 243 |     if config is None:
 244 |         config = DefaultConfig
 245 |     
 246 |     # Validate query length
 247 |     if len(seq) > config.BLAST_MAX_QUERY_LENGTH:
 248 |         raise ValueError(f"Query too long: {len(seq)} chars (max: {config.BLAST_MAX_QUERY_LENGTH})")
 249 |     
 250 |     # Log sequence info
 251 |     seq_preview = seq[:100] + "..." if len(seq) > 100 else seq
 252 |     logger.info(f"Submitting BLAST request (ident={min_identity}%, max={max_sequences}), sequence preview: {seq_preview}")
 253 |     
 254 |     params = {
 255 |         "CMD": "Put",
 256 |         "PROGRAM": "blastn",
 257 |         "DATABASE": "nt",
 258 |         "QUERY": seq,
 259 |         "HITLIST_SIZE": max_sequences,
 260 |         "ALIGNMENTS": max_sequences,
 261 |         "PERC_IDENT": min_identity,
 262 |         "FORMAT_TYPE": "JSON2",
 263 |         "EMAIL": config.BLAST_EMAIL
 264 |     }
 265 |     
 266 |     # Use _ncbi_request helper
 267 |     response = _ncbi_request("POST", NCBI_BLAST_URL, data=params, timeout=(10, 60))
 268 |     # Raise if 4xx/5xx (though 5xx handled by retry, final attempt might fail)
 269 |     response.raise_for_status()
 270 |     
 271 |     logger.debug(f"BLAST submission response: {response.text[:500]}")
 272 |     
 273 |     # Parse RID and RTOE from response text
 274 |     # Response usually contains: "    RID = ...\n    RTOE = ..."
 275 |     rid = None
 276 |     rtoe = 30
 277 |     
 278 |     for line in response.text.splitlines():
 279 |         if "RID =" in line:
 280 |             rid = line.split("=")[1].strip()
 281 |         if "RTOE =" in line:
 282 |             try:
 283 |                 rtoe = int(line.split("=")[1].strip())
 284 |             except ValueError:
 285 |                 pass
 286 |                 
 287 |     if not rid:
 288 |         raise ValueError(f"Could not retrieve RID from NCBI BLAST submission. Response: {response.text[:500]}")
 289 |     
 290 |     logger.info(f"BLAST submission successful. RID={rid}, RTOE={rtoe}")
 291 |     return rid, rtoe
 292 | 
 293 | def _poll_blast(rid: str, rtoe: int, config: Config, logger) -> None:
 294 |     """Poll NCBI for BLAST job completion."""
 295 |     # 1. Wait initial RTOE
 296 |     logger.info(f"Waiting {rtoe} seconds for RTOE...")
 297 |     time.sleep(rtoe)
 298 |     
 299 |     start_time = time.time()
 300 |     max_wait = 600  # 10 minutes max
 301 |     
 302 |     # Use configured poll interval, but MINIMUM 60 seconds
 303 |     configured_interval = getattr(config, 'BLAST_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS', 60)
 304 |     poll_interval = max(60, configured_interval)
 305 |     
 306 |     # 2. Poll Loop
 307 |     while (time.time() - start_time) < max_wait:
 308 |         params = {
 309 |             "CMD": "Get",
 310 |             "FORMAT_OBJECT": "SearchInfo",
 311 |             "RID": rid
 312 |         }
 313 |         
 314 |         # Use helper
 315 |         try:
 316 |             response = _ncbi_request("GET", NCBI_BLAST_URL, params=params, timeout=(5, 30))
 317 |             # Don't strictly raise on 404/etc inside poll loop? Actually SearchInfo should exist.
 318 |             # If 404, maybe RID expired.
 319 |             if response.status_code != 200:
 320 |                 logger.warning(f"Poll got status {response.status_code}. Retrying next cycle.")
 321 |                 time.sleep(poll_interval)
 322 |                 continue
 323 |                 
 324 |             content = response.text
 325 |              # Log full poll response for debugging
 326 |             logger.debug(f"Poll response for RID {rid}: {content[:500]}")
 327 |             
 328 |             if "Status=WAITING" in content:
 329 |                 logger.info(f"BLAST Status: WAITING. Sleeping {poll_interval}s...")
 330 |                 time.sleep(poll_interval)
 331 |                 continue
 332 |             
 333 |             if "Status=FAILED" in content:
 334 |                 raise RuntimeError(f"BLAST failed for RID {rid}")
 335 |                 
 336 |             if "Status=UNKNOWN" in content:
 337 |                 raise RuntimeError(f"BLAST RID {rid} expired or unknown")
 338 |                 
 339 |             if "Status=READY" in content:
 340 |                 if "ThereAreHits=yes" in content:
 341 |                     logger.info("BLAST Status: READY with hits.")
 342 |                 else:
 343 |                     # Still try to fetch - sometimes this flag is wrong
 344 |                     logger.warning("BLAST Status: READY but ThereAreHits=no (will still attempt to fetch results).")
 345 |                 return
 346 |                 
 347 |         except Exception as e:
 348 |              logger.warning(f"Poll exception: {e}. Retrying next cycle.")
 349 |              time.sleep(poll_interval)
 350 |              continue
 351 | 
 352 |     raise TimeoutError("BLAST timed out waiting for results")
 353 | 
 354 | def _fetch_blast_results(rid: str) -> Dict[str, List]:
 355 |     """Fetch BLAST results from NCBI. Handle both raw JSON and ZIP-compressed responses.
 356 |     
 357 |     Returns:
 358 |         Dict with 'accessions' (list of str) and 'hit_details' (list of dicts with accession/organism)
 359 |     """
 360 |     import io
 361 |     import zipfile
 362 |     
 363 |     params = {
 364 |         "CMD": "Get",
 365 |         "FORMAT_TYPE": "JSON2",
 366 |         "RID": rid
 367 |     }
 368 |     
 369 |     response = _ncbi_request("GET", NCBI_BLAST_URL, params=params, timeout=(10, 120))
 370 |     response.raise_for_status()
 371 |     
 372 |     content_type = response.headers.get('content-type', '')
 373 |     logger.info(f"BLAST result content-type: {content_type}, length: {len(response.content)} bytes")
 374 |     
 375 |     # Handle ZIP-compressed response (NCBI sometimes returns JSON in a ZIP)
 376 |     if 'application/zip' in content_type or response.content[:2] == b'PK':
 377 |         logger.info("Detected ZIP-compressed BLAST response, extracting...")
 378 |         try:
 379 |             with zipfile.ZipFile(io.BytesIO(response.content)) as zf:
 380 |                 # Find the JSON file in the archive
 381 |                 json_files = [f for f in zf.namelist() if f.endswith('.json')]
 382 |                 logger.info(f"ZIP contains files: {zf.namelist()}")
 383 |                 
 384 |                 if not json_files:
 385 |                     logger.error("No JSON files found in ZIP archive")
 386 |                     return {"accessions": [], "hit_details": []}
 387 |                 
 388 |                 # Read the main JSON file (or first JSON ending with _1.json)
 389 |                 main_json = None
 390 |                 for jf in json_files:
 391 |                     if '_1.json' in jf:
 392 |                         main_json = jf
 393 |                         break
 394 |                 if not main_json:
 395 |                     main_json = json_files[0]
 396 |                 
 397 |                 logger.info(f"Reading JSON from ZIP: {main_json}")
 398 |                 content = zf.read(main_json).decode('utf-8')
 399 |         except Exception as e:
 400 |             logger.error(f"Failed to extract ZIP: {e}")
 401 |             return {"accessions": [], "hit_details": []}
 402 |     else:
 403 |         content = response.text
 404 |     
 405 |     logger.debug(f"BLAST JSON content length: {len(content)} chars")
 406 |     
 407 |     # Check if response looks like JSON
 408 |     if not content.strip().startswith('{') and not content.strip().startswith('['):
 409 |         logger.warning(f"BLAST response doesn't look like JSON, first 500 chars: {content[:500]}")
 410 |         # Try to extract accessions from text-based format as fallback
 411 |         import re
 412 |         accession_pattern = r'\b([A-Z]{1,2}_?\d{6,}(?:\.\d+)?)\b'
 413 |         matches = re.findall(accession_pattern, content)
 414 |         if matches:
 415 |             unique_accessions = list(dict.fromkeys(matches))[:50]
 416 |             logger.info(f"Extracted {len(unique_accessions)} accessions from text response")
 417 |             return {"accessions": unique_accessions, "hit_details": []}
 418 |         logger.warning("Could not extract accessions from non-JSON response")
 419 |         return {"accessions": [], "hit_details": []}
 420 |     
 421 |     try:
 422 |         data = json.loads(content)
 423 |         
 424 |         # Log top-level keys for debugging
 425 |         logger.info(f"BLAST JSON top-level keys: {list(data.keys())}")
 426 |         
 427 |         # Navigate JSON structure to find hits
 428 |         # BlastOutput2 can be either a dict (with 'report' key) or a list of dicts
 429 |         blast_output = data.get("BlastOutput2", {})
 430 |         
 431 |         if not blast_output:
 432 |             logger.warning(f"No BlastOutput2 in response. Full response keys: {list(data.keys())}")
 433 |             debug_path = Path("/tmp/blast_debug_response.json")
 434 |             debug_path.write_text(json.dumps(data, indent=2)[:10000])
 435 |             logger.info(f"Saved debug response to {debug_path}")
 436 |             return {"accessions": [], "hit_details": []}
 437 |         
 438 |         # Handle both dict and list formats
 439 |         if isinstance(blast_output, dict):
 440 |             # Direct structure: BlastOutput2 -> report -> results -> search -> hits
 441 |             report = blast_output.get("report", {})
 442 |         elif isinstance(blast_output, list) and len(blast_output) > 0:
 443 |             # List structure: BlastOutput2[0] -> report -> results -> search -> hits
 444 |             report = blast_output[0].get("report", {})
 445 |         else:
 446 |             logger.warning(f"Unexpected BlastOutput2 type: {type(blast_output)}")
 447 |             return {"accessions": [], "hit_details": []}
 448 |         
 449 |         logger.debug(f"Report keys: {list(report.keys())}")
 450 |         
 451 |         results = report.get("results", {})
 452 |         logger.debug(f"Results keys: {list(results.keys())}")
 453 |         
 454 |         search_results = results.get("search", {})
 455 |         logger.debug(f"Search results keys: {list(search_results.keys())}")
 456 |         
 457 |         hit_list = search_results.get("hits", [])
 458 |         
 459 |         logger.info(f"Found {len(hit_list)} hits in JSON response")
 460 |         
 461 |         if len(hit_list) == 0:
 462 |             message = search_results.get("message", "No message")
 463 |             logger.warning(f"No hits found. Search message: {message}")
 464 |         
 465 |         accessions = []
 466 |         hit_details = []  # List of {accession, organism} dicts
 467 |         for hit in hit_list:
 468 |             # description -> [ { "accession": "...", "sciname": "..." } ]
 469 |             descs = hit.get("description", [])
 470 |             if descs:
 471 |                 desc = descs[0]
 472 |                 acc = desc.get("accession")
 473 |                 organism = desc.get("sciname", "")
 474 |                 if acc:
 475 |                     accessions.append(acc)
 476 |                     hit_details.append({"accession": acc, "organism": organism})
 477 |                     
 478 |         logger.info(f"Extracted {len(accessions)} accessions from hits")
 479 |         return {"accessions": accessions[:50], "hit_details": hit_details[:50]}
 480 |         
 481 |     except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
 482 |         logger.error(f"JSON decode error: {e}, response preview: {content[:500]}")
 483 |         raise ValueError(f"Failed to parse BLAST JSON response: {e}")
 484 | 
 485 | def _fetch_genbank_xml_batch(accessions: List[str]) -> Optional[str]:
 486 |     """
 487 |     Fetch GenBank XML for a single batch of accessions.
 488 |     Returns XML string or None on failure.
 489 |     """
 490 |     if not accessions:
 491 |         return None
 492 |         
 493 |     ids = ",".join(accessions)
 494 |     params = {
 495 |         "db": "nuccore",
 496 |         "id": ids,
 497 |         "rettype": "gb",
 498 |         "retmode": "xml"
 499 |     }
 500 |     
 501 |     try:
 502 |         # Use existing helper with retries
 503 |         response = _ncbi_request("POST", NCBI_EFETCH_URL, data=params, timeout=(15, 90))
 504 |         response.raise_for_status()
 505 |         return response.text
 506 |     except Exception as e:
 507 |         logger.error(f"Failed to fetch GenBank XML for batch {accessions[:3]}...: {e}")
 508 |         return None
 509 | 
 510 | def _parse_genbank_xml(xml_text: str) -> Dict[str, Dict]:
 511 |     """
 512 |     Parse GenBank XML string.
 513 |     Returns a dict with 'by_acc' and 'by_ver' lookups mapping to parsed records.
 514 |     
 515 |     ParsedRecord schema:
 516 |     {
 517 |         "accession": str,
 518 |         "version": str,  # "Accession.Version"
 519 |         "organism": str,
 520 |         "sequence": str, # Uppercase, clean
 521 |         "raw_record": str, # Blob for keyword scanning
 522 |         "source_features": Dict[str, str], # qualifier_name -> value
 523 |         "type_material": Optional[str] # If found in source qualifiers
 524 |     }
 525 |     """
 526 |     result = {"by_acc": {}, "by_ver": {}}
 527 |     
 528 |     try:
 529 |         # Remove namespace prefixes if present to simplify parsing
 530 |         xml_text = re.sub(r'\sxmlns="[^"]+"', '', xml_text, count=1)
 531 |         root = ET.fromstring(xml_text)
 532 |         
 533 |         for gb_seq in root.findall(".//GBSeq"):
 534 |             # 1. Basic Info
 535 |             acc = gb_seq.findtext("GBSeq_primary-accession", "")
 536 |             ver = gb_seq.findtext("GBSeq_accession-version", "")
 537 |             organism = gb_seq.findtext("GBSeq_organism", "").strip()
 538 |             definition = gb_seq.findtext("GBSeq_definition", "").strip()
 539 |             
 540 |             # 2. Sequence
 541 |             raw_seq = gb_seq.findtext("GBSeq_sequence", "")
 542 |             # Clean sequence: strip whitespace, remove - and ., keep IUPAC, uppercase
 543 |             if raw_seq:
 544 |                 # Remove whitespace (newlines, spaces)
 545 |                 clean_seq = "".join(raw_seq.split())
 546 |                 # Remove gaps/align chars
 547 |                 clean_seq = re.sub(r'[-.]', '', clean_seq)
 548 |                 clean_seq = clean_seq.upper()
 549 |             else:
 550 |                 clean_seq = None
 551 |                 
 552 |             # 3. Source Features & Qualifiers
 553 |             source_quals = {}
 554 |             type_material = None
 555 |             
 556 |             features = gb_seq.find("GBSeq_feature-table")
 557 |             all_quals_text = [] # For blob scanning
 558 |             
 559 |             if features is not None:
 560 |                 for feature in features.findall("GBFeature"):
 561 |                     key = feature.findtext("GBFeature_key", "")
 562 |                     quals = feature.find("GBFeature_quals")
 563 |                     
 564 |                     if quals is not None:
 565 |                         for qual in quals.findall("GBQualifier"):
 566 |                             q_name = qual.findtext("GBQualifier_name", "")
 567 |                             q_val = qual.findtext("GBQualifier_value", "")
 568 |                             
 569 |                             all_quals_text.append(f"{q_name}={q_val}")
 570 |                             
 571 |                             if key == "source":
 572 |                                 # Keep all source qualifiers
 573 |                                 source_quals[q_name] = q_val
 574 |                                 if q_name == "type_material":
 575 |                                     type_material = q_val
 576 | 
 577 |             # 4. Blob for keyword scanning (Definition + Organism + All Qualifiers + Comments + Refs)
 578 |             # Add comments and refs if needed, for now def + org + quals is usually sufficient for types
 579 |             blob_parts = [definition, organism] + all_quals_text
 580 |             
 581 |             # Add comments
 582 |             comment = gb_seq.findtext("GBSeq_comment", "")
 583 |             if comment:
 584 |                 blob_parts.append(comment)
 585 |                 
 586 |             record = {
 587 |                 "accession": acc,
 588 |                 "version": ver,
 589 |                 "organism": organism,
 590 |                 "sequence": clean_seq,
 591 |                 "source_features": source_quals,
 592 |                 "type_material": type_material,
 593 |                 "blob": " ".join(blob_parts)
 594 |             }
 595 |             
 596 |             if acc:
 597 |                 result["by_acc"][acc] = record
 598 |             if ver:
 599 |                 result["by_ver"][ver] = record
 600 |                 
 601 |     except ET.ParseError as e:
 602 |         logger.error(f"XML Parse Error: {e}")
 603 |     except Exception as e:
 604 |         logger.error(f"Error parsing GenBank XML: {e}")
 605 |         
 606 |     return result
 607 | 
 608 | def _build_header(record: Dict) -> str:
 609 |     """
 610 |     Construct FASTA header from parsed record.
 611 |     Format: >{acc_ver} {organism} {sample_id} {extras} {type_status}
 612 |     """
 613 |     # 1. Base
 614 |     # Use version if available, else accession
 615 |     acc_ver = record.get("version") or record.get("accession")
 616 |     organism = record.get("organism") or "(unknown organism)"
 617 |     
 618 |     parts = [acc_ver, organism]
 619 |     
 620 |     # Helper to clean redundant organism name from values
 621 |     def _strip_organism(val: str, org_name: str) -> str:
 622 |         if not val:
 623 |             return val
 624 | 
 625 |         val = " ".join(str(val).split())
 626 | 
 627 |         org_name = " ".join(str(org_name or "").split())
 628 |         org_tokens = org_name.split()
 629 |         org_binomial = " ".join(org_tokens[:2]) if len(org_tokens) >= 2 else ""
 630 | 
 631 |         # Try stripping both the full organism string and the binomial.
 632 |         variants = [v for v in [org_name, org_binomial] if v]
 633 | 
 634 |         for v in variants:
 635 |             # Remove occurrences anywhere (case-insensitive)
 636 |             val = re.sub(re.escape(v), "", val, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
 637 | 
 638 |         # Clean up punctuation/separators left behind
 639 |         val = re.sub(r"\s*[:;,()\[\]{}]\s*", " ", val)
 640 |         val = re.sub(r"\bof\b", "", val, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
 641 |         val = " ".join(val.split()).strip()
 642 | 
 643 |         # Also remove trailing organism variants (repeated)
 644 |         for v in variants:
 645 |             val = re.sub(rf"(?:\s+{re.escape(v)})+\s*$", "", val, flags=re.IGNORECASE).strip()
 646 | 
 647 |         return val
 648 | 
 649 | 
 650 | 
 651 | 
 652 |     # If the assembled header accidentally ends with the organism repeated,
 653 |     # drop that suffix (token-aware).
 654 |     def _drop_trailing_organism_tokens(tokens: List[str], org_name: str) -> List[str]:
 655 |         if not tokens or not org_name:
 656 |             return tokens
 657 |         org_tokens = org_name.split()
 658 |         n = len(org_tokens)
 659 |         
 660 |         # Check if it ends with organism
 661 |         if len(tokens) >= n and [t.lower() for t in tokens[-n:]] == [t.lower() for t in org_tokens]:
 662 |             # Ensure we don't strip the PRIMARY organism (at index 1)
 663 |             # We assume index 0 is Accession, index 1 is Organism.
 664 |             # If the stripped range overlaps with index 1, don't strip (or stripped part is the primary one).
 665 |             # Indices of stripped part: len(tokens)-n to len(tokens)
 666 |             # Primary organism indices: 1 to 1+n (roughly, if split?) 
 667 |             # Actually, 'parts' list has strict slots. parts[1] is the Organism string.
 668 |             # If org_name is "Psilocybe cyanescens", parts[1] is "Psilocybe cyanescens".
 669 |             # If parts = [Acc, Org], then parts[-1] is Org.
 670 |             # logical check: Is the detected suffix the SAME element as parts[1]?
 671 |             
 672 |             # Simple heuristic: Don't strip if the result would have fewer than 2 elements (Acc + Org)?
 673 |             # Or if the match starts at index 1.
 674 |             start_index_of_match = len(tokens) - n
 675 |             if start_index_of_match <= 1:
 676 |                 return tokens
 677 |                 
 678 |             return tokens[:-n]
 679 |             
 680 |         return tokens
 681 | 
 682 |     # Extract type status so we can append it at the end without the "type_material" label.
 683 |     # Prefer structured /type_material, but reduce it to just known statuses when possible.
 684 |     TYPE_KEYWORDS = ["holotype", "isotype", "epitype", "lectotype", "neotype", "paratype", "syntype", "topotype"]
 685 | 
 686 |     def _extract_type_status(rec: Dict) -> List[str]:
 687 |         # 1) Structured
 688 |         t_val = rec.get("type_material")
 689 |         if t_val:
 690 |             t_val_norm = " ".join(str(t_val).split())
 691 |             t_val_norm = _strip_organism(t_val_norm, organism)
 692 |             low = t_val_norm.lower()
 693 |             found = [kw for kw in TYPE_KEYWORDS if re.search(rf"\b{re.escape(kw)}\b", low)]
 694 |             # If we found recognizable statuses, return those only (in stable order).
 695 |             if found:
 696 |                 return found
 697 |             # Otherwise return the cleaned structured value as-is (still appended at end)
 698 |             return [t_val_norm] if t_val_norm else []
 699 | 
 700 |         # 2) Fallback keyword scan
 701 |         blob = (rec.get("blob") or "").lower()
 702 |         found = [kw for kw in TYPE_KEYWORDS if re.search(rf"\b{re.escape(kw)}\b", blob)]
 703 |         return found
 704 | 
 705 |     # 2. Sample ID Priority
 706 |     # specimen_voucher > culture_collection > bio_material > isolate > strain
 707 |     quals = record.get("source_features", {})
 708 |     sample_id = None
 709 |     for key in ["specimen_voucher", "culture_collection", "bio_material", "isolate", "strain"]:
 710 |         if val := quals.get(key):
 711 |             # Clean redundant organism from sample ID value
 712 |             val = _strip_organism(val, organism)
 713 |             if val:
 714 |                 sample_id = f"{key} {val}"
 715 |                 break
 716 |             
 717 |     if sample_id:
 718 |         parts.append(sample_id)
 719 |         
 720 |     # 3. Extras (Location first; we want type status at the very end)
 721 |     # geo_loc_name, country
 722 |     extras = []
 723 |     current_len = sum(len(p) for p in parts) + len(parts) # approx
 724 |     MAX_LEN = 120 # Cap for extras
 725 |     
 726 |     for key in ["geo_loc_name", "country"]:
 727 |         if val := quals.get(key):
 728 |             val = " ".join(val.split())
 729 |             
 730 |             # Clean redundant organism from value
 731 |             val = _strip_organism(val, organism)
 732 |             if not val:
 733 |                  continue
 734 |             
 735 |             # Formating: just value for location fields, else key="val"
 736 |             if key in ["geo_loc_name", "country"]:
 737 |                 token = f"{val}"
 738 |             else:
 739 |                 token = f"{key}=\"{val}\""
 740 |             
 741 |             if len(extras) < 3 and (current_len + len(token)) < (current_len + 120): # Soft cap
 742 |                 extras.append(token)
 743 |                 current_len += len(token) + 1
 744 |                 
 745 |     if extras:
 746 |         parts.extend(extras)
 747 |         
 748 |     # 4. Type status LAST (no "type_material" label, and not in the middle)
 749 |     type_status = _extract_type_status(record)
 750 |     if type_status:
 751 |         parts.extend(type_status)
 752 |         
 753 |     # 5. Final cleanup: remove trailing organism if it still sneaks in
 754 |     parts = _drop_trailing_organism_tokens(parts, organism)
 755 |         
 756 |     return ">" + " ".join(parts)
 757 | 
 758 | def fetch_fasta_for_accessions(accessions: List[str]) -> str:
 759 |     """
 760 |     Fetch FASTA sequences from NCBI for the given accessions using GenBank XML
 761 |     fetching to rebuild detailed headers with type material info.
 762 |     """
 763 |     if not accessions:
 764 |         return ""
 765 |     
 766 |     # Validate accession format
 767 |     import re
 768 |     valid_pattern = re.compile(r'^[A-Z]{1,6}_?\d{5,9}(?:\.\d+)?$', re.IGNORECASE)
 769 |     validated = [a for a in accessions if valid_pattern.match(a)]
 770 |     
 771 |     if not validated:
 772 |         logger.warning(f"No valid accessions found in list of {len(accessions)}")
 773 |         return ""
 774 | 
 775 |     logger.info(f"Fetching sequences for {len(validated)} accessions via GenBank XML...")
 776 |     
 777 |     # 1. Fetch and Parse
 778 |     BATCH_SIZE = 50
 779 |     records_by_acc = {}
 780 |     records_by_ver = {}
 781 |     
 782 |     counters = {
 783 |         "xml_ok": 0,
 784 |         "type_structured": 0,
 785 |         "type_keyword": 0,
 786 |         "xml_missing_seq": 0,
 787 |         "batch_fail": 0
 788 |     }
 789 |     
 790 |     for i in range(0, len(validated), BATCH_SIZE):
 791 |         batch = validated[i : i + BATCH_SIZE]
 792 |         xml_text = _fetch_genbank_xml_batch(batch)
 793 |         
 794 |         if xml_text:
 795 |             parsed = _parse_genbank_xml(xml_text)
 796 |             records_by_acc.update(parsed["by_acc"])
 797 |             records_by_ver.update(parsed["by_ver"])
 798 |             
 799 |             # counting stats
 800 |             for r in parsed["by_acc"].values():
 801 |                 if r.get("type_material"): counters["type_structured"] += 1
 802 |                 elif "holotype" in r.get("blob", "").lower(): counters["type_keyword"] += 1 # approx check
 803 |         else:
 804 |             counters["batch_fail"] += 1
 805 |             
 806 |     # 2. Reassemble deterministically
 807 |     final_lines = []
 808 |     missing_for_fallback = []
 809 |     
 810 |     for acc in validated:
 811 |         # Try finding record
 812 |         record = records_by_ver.get(acc) or records_by_acc.get(acc)
 813 |         
 814 |         if record and record.get("sequence"):
 815 |             counters["xml_ok"] += 1
 816 |             
 817 |             header = _build_header(record)
 818 |             seq = record["sequence"]
 819 |             
 820 |             # Wrap sequence at 60 chars
 821 |             wrapped_seq = "\n".join(seq[i:i+60] for i in range(0, len(seq), 60))
 822 |             final_lines.append(f"{header}\n{wrapped_seq}")
 823 |         else:
 824 |             if record:
 825 |                 counters["xml_missing_seq"] += 1
 826 |             missing_for_fallback.append(acc)
 827 | 
 828 |     logger.info(f"XML Fetch Stats: OK={counters['xml_ok']}, TypeStruct={counters['type_structured']}, "
 829 |                 f"TypeKey={counters['type_keyword']}, MissingSeq={counters['xml_missing_seq']}, "
 830 |                 f"BatchFail={counters['batch_fail']}")
 831 | 
 832 |     # 3. Fallback for missing
 833 |     if missing_for_fallback:
 834 |         logger.warning(f"Falling back to legacy FASTA fetch for {len(missing_for_fallback)} accessions")
 835 |         
 836 |         # We can implement a simple fallback here using the OLD method (efetch fasta)
 837 |         # Construct parameters for fallback
 838 |         try:
 839 |             ids = ",".join(missing_for_fallback)
 840 |             params = {
 841 |                 "db": "nuccore",
 842 |                 "id": ids,
 843 |                 "rettype": "fasta",
 844 |                 "retmode": "text"
 845 |             }
 846 |             # Use same batching logic? Or just dump all if small?
 847 |             # Safe to assume fallback list might be small, but if batch fail, it could be large.
 848 |             # Let's just do one call if small, or reuse verify logic? 
 849 |             # Simple approach: Just request. If it's huge, requests might fail, but existing code didn't batch FASTA fetch anyway.
 850 |             # Wait, existing code did NOT batch FASTA fetch (it sent all IDs).
 851 |             
 852 |             response = _ncbi_request("POST", NCBI_EFETCH_URL, data=params, timeout=(10, 60))
 853 |             if response.status_code == 200:
 854 |                 final_lines.append(response.text.strip())
 855 |             else:
 856 |                 logger.error(f"Fallback FASTA fetch failed: {response.status_code}")
 857 |                 # Ultimate fallback: Simple headers with empty sequences? Or just omit?
 858 |                 # If we omit, the alignment step might fail. 
 859 |                 # Better to include barebones if possible, but we don't have sequence.
 860 |                 pass
 861 |         except Exception as e:
 862 |             logger.error(f"Fallback FASTA fetch exception: {e}")
 863 |             
 864 |     return "\n".join(final_lines)

   1 | """
   2 | FASTA utility module.
   3 | 
   4 | Provides functions for sanitizing FASTA headers to be compatible with tools
   5 | like RAxML, and restoring original headers in output files.
   6 | """
   7 | 
   8 | import logging
   9 | import re
  10 | from pathlib import Path
  11 | from typing import Dict
  12 | 
  13 | try:
  14 |     from Bio import SeqIO
  15 |     HAS_BIOPYTHON = True
  16 | except ImportError:
  17 |     HAS_BIOPYTHON = False
  18 | 
  19 | logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  20 | 
  21 | 
  22 | def clean_dna_sequence(raw_sequence: str, min_length: int = 100) -> str:
  23 |     """
  24 |     Clean a DNA sequence by extracting the longest contiguous run of valid nucleotides.
  25 |     
  26 |     This handles cases where:
  27 |     - The sequence has a FASTA header (>description...) on a separate line or same line
  28 |     - The sequence has garbage text at the start or end (species name, collection number, notes)
  29 |     - The sequence has whitespace/newlines
  30 |     
  31 |     Algorithm:
  32 |     1. Strip FASTA header markers (>) but keep remainder of line
  33 |     2. Remove all whitespace
  34 |     3. Find the longest contiguous run of valid IUPAC nucleotide characters
  35 |     4. Return that run if it meets minimum length, otherwise empty string
  36 |     
  37 |     Args:
  38 |         raw_sequence: Raw DNA sequence string that may contain non-DNA text
  39 |         min_length: Minimum length for a valid barcode (default 100bp for ITS)
  40 |         
  41 |     Returns:
  42 |         Cleaned DNA sequence containing only valid IUPAC nucleotide characters,
  43 |         or empty string if no valid run of sufficient length is found
  44 |     """
  45 |     if not raw_sequence:
  46 |         return ""
  47 |     
  48 |     # Valid IUPAC nucleotide characters (DNA + ambiguity codes + gap)
  49 |     valid_chars = set("ACGTRYSWKMBDHVNacgtryswkmbdhvn-")
  50 |     
  51 |     # Process lines: strip > marker but keep rest of each line
  52 |     lines = raw_sequence.strip().split('\n')
  53 |     processed_lines = []
  54 |     
  55 |     for line in lines:
  56 |         line = line.strip()
  57 |         if not line:
  58 |             continue
  59 |         
  60 |         # Strip FASTA header marker but keep the rest (may contain sequence)
  61 |         if line.startswith(">"):
  62 |             # keep only content after first whitespace (if any)
  63 |             line = line[1:]
  64 |             parts = line.split(None, 1)
  65 |             line = parts[1] if len(parts) == 2 else ""
  66 | 
  67 |         
  68 |         processed_lines.append(line)
  69 |     
  70 |     # Join all lines and remove whitespace
  71 |     combined = ''.join(''.join(line.split()) for line in processed_lines)
  72 |     
  73 |     if not combined:
  74 |         return ""
  75 |     
  76 |     # Find the longest contiguous run of valid DNA characters
  77 |     # This handles both prefix AND suffix garbage efficiently in O(n)
  78 |     best_start = 0
  79 |     best_length = 0
  80 |     current_start = None
  81 |     
  82 |     for i, c in enumerate(combined):
  83 |         if c in valid_chars:
  84 |             if current_start is None:
  85 |                 current_start = i
  86 |         else:
  87 |             if current_start is not None:
  88 |                 run_length = i - current_start
  89 |                 if run_length > best_length:
  90 |                     best_start = current_start
  91 |                     best_length = run_length
  92 |                 current_start = None
  93 |     
  94 |     # Check final run (if string ends with valid chars)
  95 |     if current_start is not None:
  96 |         run_length = len(combined) - current_start
  97 |         if run_length > best_length:
  98 |             best_start = current_start
  99 |             best_length = run_length
 100 |     
 101 |     # Extract the best run if it meets minimum length
 102 |     if best_length >= min_length:
 103 |         cleaned = combined[best_start:best_start + best_length]
 104 |         return cleaned.upper()
 105 |     
 106 |     # No valid run of sufficient length found
 107 |     return ""
 108 | 
 109 | 
 110 | def sanitize_fasta_headers(input_path: Path, output_path: Path) -> Dict[str, str]:
 111 |     """
 112 |     Read a FASTA file, rename sequences to safe IDs (e.g., SEQ0001),
 113 |     write the sanitized FASTA to output_path, and return a mapping
 114 |     of safe_id -> original_header.
 115 | 
 116 |     Args:
 117 |         input_path: Path to the original FASTA file.
 118 |         output_path: Path to write the sanitized FASTA file.
 119 | 
 120 |     Returns:
 121 |         Dictionary mapping sanitized IDs to original headers.
 122 |     """
 123 |     if not HAS_BIOPYTHON:
 124 |         raise RuntimeError("BioPython is required for FASTA sanitization.")
 125 | 
 126 |     mapping = {}
 127 |     
 128 |     # We use SeqIO to read/write to handle multiline sequences gracefully
 129 |     records = []
 130 |     
 131 |     try:
 132 |         # Use existing format if possible, otherwise generic fasta
 133 |         # We'll just assume generic fasta
 134 |         original_records = list(SeqIO.parse(str(input_path), "fasta"))
 135 |         
 136 |         for i, record in enumerate(original_records):
 137 |             original_header = record.description
 138 |             # Create a safe ID. Using a simple counter format.
 139 |             safe_id = f"SEQ{i+1:06d}"
 140 |             
 141 |             mapping[safe_id] = original_header
 142 |             
 143 |             # Update record
 144 |             record.id = safe_id
 145 |             record.description = ""  # Clear description to avoid extra text in header
 146 |             record.name = safe_id
 147 |             
 148 |             records.append(record)
 149 |             
 150 |         SeqIO.write(records, str(output_path), "fasta")
 151 |         
 152 |         logger.info(f"Sanitized {len(records)} sequences in {input_path}")
 153 |         return mapping
 154 | 
 155 |     except Exception as e:
 156 |         logger.error(f"Failed to sanitize FASTA headers: {e}")
 157 |         raise
 158 | 
 159 | 
 160 | def _quote_newick_name(name: str) -> str:
 161 |     """
 162 |     Quote a name for Newick format if it contains special characters.
 163 |     Standard Newick quoting uses single quotes, with internal single quotes doubled.
 164 |     """
 165 |     # Characters that definitely require quoting in Newick
 166 |     # Whitespace, colon, semicolon, comma, parens, brackets
 167 |     special_chars = set(":;,()[] ")
 168 |     
 169 |     # Check if quoting is needed
 170 |     if any(c in special_chars for c in name) or "'" in name:
 171 |         # Escape existing single quotes
 172 |         escaped_name = name.replace("'", "''")
 173 |         return f"'{escaped_name}'"
 174 |         
 175 |     return name
 176 | 
 177 | 
 178 | def restore_tree_names(tree_path: Path, mapping: Dict[str, str]) -> None:
 179 |     """
 180 |     Read a tree file (Newick, Nexus, etc.) as text, replace safe IDs
 181 |     with their original counterparts, and overwrite the file.
 182 |     
 183 |     Uses token-aware replacement (regex mapping) and ensures correct 
 184 |     Newick quoting for names containing special characters.
 185 | 
 186 |     Args:
 187 |         tree_path: Path to the tree file.
 188 |         mapping: Dictionary mapping safe IDs to original headers.
 189 |     """
 190 |     if not tree_path.exists():
 191 |         logger.warning(f"Tree file not found for name restoration: {tree_path}")
 192 |         return
 193 | 
 194 |     try:
 195 |         content = tree_path.read_text()
 196 |         
 197 |         # Helper for regex replacement
 198 |         def replace_match(match):
 199 |             safe_id = match.group(0)
 200 |             if safe_id in mapping:
 201 |                 return _quote_newick_name(mapping[safe_id])
 202 |             return safe_id
 203 | 
 204 |         # Replace all occurrences of SEQxxxxxx using a regex
 205 |         # We match word boundaries (\b) to ensure we don't partial-match
 206 |         # Pattern matches SEQ followed by one or more digits
 207 |         new_content = re.sub(r"\bSEQ\d+\b", replace_match, content)
 208 |             
 209 |         tree_path.write_text(new_content)
 210 |         logger.info(f"Restored names in {tree_path}")
 211 | 
 212 |     except Exception as e:
 213 |         logger.error(f"Failed to restore tree names in {tree_path}: {e}")
 214 |         raise

   1 | """
   2 | iNaturalist API service for DNA sequence extraction.
   3 | 
   4 | Fetches DNA Barcode ITS sequences from iNaturalist observations.
   5 | 
   6 | API best practices followed per https://www.inaturalist.org/pages/api+recommended+practices:
   7 | - Rate limit: 1 request per second
   8 | - Custom User-Agent header
   9 | - Batch requests using per_page (max 200)
  10 | - Use api.inaturalist.org for new API
  11 | """
  12 | 
  13 | import logging
  14 | import re
  15 | import time
  16 | import urllib.request
  17 | import urllib.parse
  18 | import urllib.error
  19 | import json
  20 | from typing import Optional, List, Dict, Tuple, Any
  21 | from urllib.parse import urlparse, parse_qs
  22 | 
  23 | logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  24 | 
  25 | # API Configuration
  26 | INATURALIST_API_BASE = "https://api.inaturalist.org/v1"
  27 | USER_AGENT = "Dikarya Phylogenetic Tree Builder 1.0 - For questions contact Alan Rockefeller"
  28 | REQUEST_TIMEOUT = 30
  29 | RATE_LIMIT_DELAY = 1.0  # seconds between requests
  30 | MAX_PER_PAGE = 200  # iNaturalist max per_page value
  31 | MAX_OBSERVATIONS = 10000  # API pagination limit
  32 | 
  33 | # Valid IUPAC nucleotide characters (DNA + ambiguity codes + gap)
  34 | VALID_DNA_CHARS = set('ATGCYRWSKMNatgcyrwskmn-')

  36 | 
  37 | def validate_inaturalist_url(url: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
  38 |     """
  39 |     Validate an iNaturalist URL and extract query parameters.
  40 |     
  41 |     Args:
  42 |         url: The URL to validate
  43 |         
  44 |     Returns:
  45 |         Dict with 'type' ('observation', 'observations_search') and relevant IDs/params,
  46 |         or None if invalid.
  47 |     """
  48 |     if not url:
  49 |         return None
  50 |     
  51 |     url = url.strip()
  52 |     
  53 |     # Must be from inaturalist.org domain (strict check)
  54 |     # Matches: inaturalist.org, www.inaturalist.org
  55 |     # Rejects: notinaturalist.org, inaturalist.org.fake.com
  56 |     try:
  57 |         parsed = urlparse(url)
  58 |         hostname = parsed.hostname or ""
  59 |         
  60 |         # Must be inaturalist.org or subdomain
  61 |         if not (hostname == "inaturalist.org" or 
  62 |                 hostname.endswith(".inaturalist.org")):
  63 |             logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: not an inaturalist.org URL: {url}")
  64 |             return None
  65 |     except Exception as e:
  66 |         logger.warning(f"URL parsing failed: {e}")
  67 |         return None
  68 |     
  69 |     # Check for single observation URL pattern: /observations/12345
  70 |     single_obs_match = re.search(r'/observations/(\d+)(?:\?|$|#)', url)
  71 |     if single_obs_match:
  72 |         obs_id = single_obs_match.group(1)
  73 |         logger.info(f"Validated single observation URL, ID: {obs_id}")
  74 |         return {
  75 |             'type': 'single_observation',
  76 |             'observation_id': obs_id
  77 |         }
  78 |     
  79 |     # Check for observations list/search URL: /observations or /observations?...
  80 |     if '/observations' in parsed.path:
  81 |         # Parse query parameters, keeping blank values (e.g. field:DNA Barcode ITS=)
  82 |         query_params = parse_qs(parsed.query, keep_blank_values=True)
  83 |         
  84 |         # Security: Require at least one meaningful filter param to prevent massive/broad queries
  85 |         # We don't want users scraping *all* observations or causing backend abuse.
  86 |         meaningful_filters = {
  87 |             'taxon_id', 'place_id', 'user_id', 'project_id', 'list_id', 'id', 
  88 |             'identified_by', 'q'
  89 |         }
  90 |         has_meaningful_filter = any(k in meaningful_filters for k in query_params.keys())
  91 |         has_field_filter = any(k.startswith('field:') for k in query_params.keys())
  92 |         
  93 |         if not (has_meaningful_filter or has_field_filter):
  94 |             logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: Search URL missing required filter params (taxon_id, user_id, etc.): {url}")
  95 |             return None
  96 |             
  97 |         logger.info(f"Validated observations search URL with params: {list(query_params.keys())}")
  98 |         return {
  99 |             'type': 'observations_search',
 100 |             'query_params': query_params,
 101 |             'raw_query': parsed.query
 102 |         }
 103 |     
 104 |     logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: unrecognized iNaturalist URL format: {url}")
 105 |     return None

 111 | def get_field_value(observation_data: Dict, field_name: str) -> Optional[str]:
 112 |     """
 113 |     Extract value from observation field values (ofvs) by field name.
 114 |     
 115 |     Args:
 116 |         observation_data: iNaturalist observation data dict
 117 |         field_name: Name of the field to extract (case-insensitive)
 118 |         
 119 |     Returns:
 120 |         Field value if found, None otherwise
 121 |     """
 122 |     field_name_lower = field_name.lower()
 123 |     for field in observation_data.get('ofvs', []):
 124 |         # Handle both 'name' (from API) and check for nested 'observation_field'
 125 |         name = field.get('name', '')
 126 |         if not name and 'observation_field' in field:
 127 |             name = field['observation_field'].get('name', '')
 128 |         
 129 |         if name.lower() == field_name_lower:
 130 |             return field.get('value')
 131 |     
 132 |     return None

 134 | 
 135 | def _make_api_request(url: str, max_retries: int = 3) -> Dict:
 136 |     """
 137 |     Make a request to the iNaturalist API with proper headers and 429 retry logic.
 138 |     
 139 |     Args:
 140 |         url: Full API URL to request
 141 |         max_retries: Number of retries for 429 errors
 142 |         
 143 |     Returns:
 144 |         Parsed JSON response as dict
 145 |         
 146 |     Raises:
 147 |         Exception on network or API errors after retries exhausted
 148 |     """
 149 |     import random
 150 |     
 151 |     opener = urllib.request.build_opener()
 152 |     opener.addheaders = [
 153 |         ('User-Agent', USER_AGENT),
 154 |         ('Accept', 'application/json')
 155 |     ]
 156 |     
 157 |     attempts = 0
 158 |     while attempts <= max_retries:
 159 |         try:
 160 |             with opener.open(url, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT) as resp:
 161 |                 content = resp.read()
 162 |                 return json.loads(content.decode('utf-8'))
 163 |                 
 164 |         except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
 165 |             if e.code == 429 and attempts < max_retries:
 166 |                 # Calculate backoff: 2s, 4s, 8s... + jitter
 167 |                 sleep_time = (2 ** (attempts + 1)) + random.uniform(0, 1)
 168 |                 logger.warning(f"iNaturalist 429 Rate Limit hit. Retrying in {sleep_time:.2f}s... (Attempt {attempts + 1}/{max_retries})")
 169 |                 time.sleep(sleep_time)
 170 |                 attempts += 1
 171 |                 continue
 172 |                 
 173 |             error_msg = f"HTTP error {e.code}: {e.reason}"
 174 |             logger.error(f"iNaturalist API error: {error_msg}")
 175 |             raise Exception(error_msg)
 176 |             
 177 |         except urllib.error.URLError as e:
 178 |             error_msg = f"Network error: {e.reason}"
 179 |             logger.error(f"iNaturalist API error: {error_msg}")
 180 |             raise Exception(error_msg)
 181 |         except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
 182 |             logger.error(f"Failed to parse API response: {e}")
 183 |             raise Exception("Invalid response from iNaturalist API")
 184 |         except TimeoutError:
 185 |             error_msg = f"Request timed out after {REQUEST_TIMEOUT}s"
 186 |             logger.error(error_msg)
 187 |             raise Exception(error_msg)
 188 |             
 189 |     # Should be unreachable given the logic above, but fail-safe
 190 |     raise Exception("Max retries exceeded")
 191 | 
 192 | 
 193 | def fetch_single_observation(observation_id: str) -> Dict:
 194 |     """
 195 |     Fetch a single observation by ID.
 196 |     
 197 |     Args:
 198 |         observation_id: iNaturalist observation ID (numeric string)
 199 |         
 200 |     Returns:
 201 |         Observation data dict
 202 |     """
 203 |     # Sanitize observation_id - must be numeric only
 204 |     if not observation_id.isdigit():
 205 |         raise ValueError("Invalid observation ID - must be numeric")
 206 |     
 207 |     url = f"{INATURALIST_API_BASE}/observations/{observation_id}"
 208 |     logger.info(f"Fetching single observation: {observation_id}")
 209 |     
 210 |     response = _make_api_request(url)
 211 |     
 212 |     results = response.get('results', [])
 213 |     if not results:
 214 |         raise Exception(f"Observation {observation_id} not found")
 215 |     
 216 |     return results[0]
