 218 | 
 219 | def build_base_params(query_params: Dict[str, List[str]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
 220 |     """
 221 |     Build sanitized base parameters from URL query parameters.
 222 |     
 223 |     Args:
 224 |         query_params: Dict of query parameters (from parse_qs)
 225 |         
 226 |     Returns:
 227 |         Dict of sanitized parameters for API calls
 228 |     """
 229 |     base_params = {}
 230 |     
 231 |     # Copy relevant params from the URL, sanitizing as we go
 232 |     # Comprehensive whitelist based on https://www.inaturalist.org/pages/search+urls
 233 |     allowed_params = {
 234 |         # Taxon filters
 235 |         'taxon_id', 'taxon_ids', 'taxon_name', 'exact_taxon_id',
 236 |         'without_taxon_id', 'without_direct_taxon_id',
 237 |         'ident_taxon_id', 'ident_taxon_id_exclusive',
 238 |         'iconic_taxa', 'list_id',
 239 |         
 240 |         # Taxon status
 241 |         'native', 'introduced', 'threatened',
 242 |         
 243 |         # Place/geographic filters
 244 |         'place_id', 'not_in_place',
 245 |         'nelat', 'nelng', 'swlat', 'swlng',  # Bounding box
 246 |         'lat', 'lng', 'radius',  # Circle
 247 |         'geo', 'mappable',
 248 |         
 249 |         # Accuracy and geoprivacy
 250 |         'acc', 'acc_above', 'acc_below',
 251 |         'geoprivacy', 'taxon_geoprivacy',
 252 |         
 253 |         # Date and time filters
 254 |         'd1', 'd2', 'month', 'day', 'year', 'hour',
 255 |         
 256 |         # Observer filters
 257 |         'user_id', 'not_user_id',
 258 |         'user_after', 'user_before',
 259 |         
 260 |         # Identification filters
 261 |         'identified', 'ident_user_id', 'without_ident_user_id',
 262 |         'disagreements',
 263 |         
 264 |         # Project filters
 265 |         'project_id', 'not_in_project',
 266 |         'apply_project_rules_for', 'not_matching_project_rules_for',
 267 |         'members_of_project',
 268 |         
 269 |         # Quality and verification
 270 |         'quality_grade', 'captive', 'verifiable',
 271 |         
 272 |         # Media and license filters
 273 |         'photos', 'sounds',
 274 |         'photo_license', 'photo_licensed',
 275 |         'sound_license', 'license', 'licensed',
 276 |         
 277 |         # Observation ID filters
 278 |         'id', 'not_id',
 279 |         
 280 |         # Description/tag search
 281 |         'q', 'search_on',
 282 |         
 283 |         # Annotation filters
 284 |         'term_id', 'term_value_id',
 285 |         'without_term_id', 'without_term_value_id',
 286 |         
 287 |         # Observation field filters
 288 |         'without_field',
 289 |         
 290 |         # App source (read-only info)
 291 |         'oauth_application_id',
 292 |         
 293 |         # NOTE: We intentionally EXCLUDE pagination/ordering params (per_page, page, 
 294 |         # order, order_by) to prevent abuse - we control these ourselves.
 295 |         # We also exclude 'reviewed' as it's user-session-specific.
 296 |     }
 297 |     
 298 |     # Also allow any field: prefixed params (preserve user intent if they added them)
 299 |     for key, values in query_params.items():
 300 |         if key in allowed_params or key.startswith('field:'):
 301 |             # Sanitize values - no script injection
 302 |             safe_values = [re.sub(r'[<>]', '', str(v)) for v in values]
 303 |             # Use empty string for blank values (e.g. field:Name=) to mean "must be present"
 304 |             val = safe_values[0] if len(safe_values) == 1 else safe_values
 305 |             if val == "": 
 306 |                  val = "" # Explicitly keep empty string value
 307 |             base_params[key] = val
 308 |             
 309 |     # Default required params
 310 |     base_params['per_page'] = MAX_PER_PAGE
 311 |     base_params['order_by'] = 'id'
 312 |     base_params['order'] = 'asc'
 313 |     
 314 |     return base_params
 315 | 
 316 | 
 317 | def fetch_observations_with_field_filter(base_params: Dict, field_name: str) -> Dict:
 318 |     """
 319 |     Fetch observations enforcing a specific observation field filter.
 320 |     
 321 |     Args:
 322 |         base_params: Base query parameters
 323 |         field_name: Name of the field to enforce (e.g. 'DNA Barcode ITS')
 324 |         
 325 |     Returns:
 326 |         Dict with 'observations', 'truncated', 'total_available'
 327 |     """
 328 |     # Strip any existing field: parameters to avoid intersection/conflict
 329 |     # We want these queries to be independent based on the base filters (taxon, place, etc.)
 330 |     params = {k: v for k, v in base_params.items() if not k.startswith("field:")}
 331 |     
 332 |     # Enforce field presence (empty value means "any value" in iNat API)
 333 |     params[f"field:{field_name}"] = ""
 334 |     
 335 |     all_observations = []
 336 |     page = 1
 337 |     
 338 |     logger.info(f"Fetching observations with field '{field_name}' (params: {list(params.keys())})")
 339 |     
 340 |     while True:
 341 |         params['page'] = page
 342 |         query_string = urllib.parse.urlencode(params, doseq=True)
 343 |         url = f"{INATURALIST_API_BASE}/observations?{query_string}"
 344 |         
 345 |         # Log less frequently to avoid spam
 346 |         if page % 5 == 1:
 347 |             logger.info(f"Fetching page {page} for '{field_name}' query")
 348 |             
 349 |         response = _make_api_request(url)
 350 |         
 351 |         results = response.get('results', [])
 352 |         total_results = response.get('total_results', 0)
 353 |         
 354 |         if not results:
 355 |             break
 356 |             
 357 |         all_observations.extend(results)
 358 |         
 359 |         if len(all_observations) >= total_results or len(all_observations) >= MAX_OBSERVATIONS:
 360 |             break
 361 |             
 362 |         page += 1
 363 |         time.sleep(RATE_LIMIT_DELAY)
 364 |         
 365 |     truncated = len(all_observations) >= MAX_OBSERVATIONS and len(all_observations) < total_results
 366 |     
 367 |     logger.info(f"Fetched {len(all_observations)} observations for '{field_name}' (total_available={total_results})")
 368 |     
 369 |     return {
 370 |         'observations': all_observations,
 371 |         'fetched_count': len(all_observations),
 372 |         'truncated': truncated,
 373 |         'total_available': total_results
 374 |     }
 375 | 
 376 | 
 377 | def analyze_observations(observations: List[Dict]) -> Dict:
 378 |     """
 379 |     Analyze observations for DNA Barcode ITS and Provisional Species Name fields.
 380 |     
 381 |     Args:
 382 |         observations: List of observation dicts
 383 |         
 384 |     Returns:
 385 |         Analysis dict with counts and species breakdown
 386 |     """
 387 |     dna_observations = []
 388 |     provisional_species_counts = {}
 389 |     
 390 |     for obs in observations:
 391 |         dna_value = get_field_value(obs, 'DNA Barcode ITS')
 392 |         
 393 |         if dna_value:
 394 |             # Clean the DNA and check if it's valid
 395 |             cleaned = clean_dna_sequence(dna_value)
 396 |             if cleaned:  # clean_dna_sequence already enforces min_length
 397 |                 dna_observations.append({
 398 |                     'observation': obs,
 399 |                     'raw_dna': dna_value,
 400 |                     'cleaned_dna': cleaned
 401 |                 })
 402 |                 
 403 |                 # Track provisional species name
 404 |                 prov_species = get_field_value(obs, 'Provisional Species Name')
 405 |                 if prov_species:
 406 |                     # Sanitize the species name for display
 407 |                     prov_species = re.sub(r'[<>]', '', str(prov_species)).strip()
 408 |                     if prov_species:
 409 |                         provisional_species_counts[prov_species] = \
 410 |                             provisional_species_counts.get(prov_species, 0) + 1
 411 |     
 412 |     # Sort provisional species by count (descending) like "sort | uniq -c | sort -n"
 413 |     sorted_species = sorted(
 414 |         provisional_species_counts.items(),
 415 |         key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])  # Sort by count desc, then name asc
 416 |     )
 417 |     
 418 |     return {
 419 |         'total_observations': len(observations),
 420 |         'dna_observations': dna_observations,
 421 |         'dna_count': len(dna_observations),
 422 |         'provisional_species': [
 423 |             {'name': name, 'count': count}
 424 |             for name, count in sorted_species
 425 |         ]
 426 |     }
 427 | 
 428 | 
 429 | def analyze_provisional_species(observations: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
 430 |     """
 431 |     Analyze observations specifically for Provisional Species Names statistics.
 432 |     
 433 |     Args:
 434 |         observations: List of observations from PSN query
 435 |         
 436 |     Returns:
 437 |         List of dicts [{'name': '...', 'count': 123}, ...]
 438 |     """
 439 |     provisional_species_counts = {}
 440 |     
 441 |     for obs in observations:
 442 |         prov_species = get_field_value(obs, 'Provisional Species Name')
 443 |         if prov_species:
 444 |             # Sanitize the species name for display
 445 |             prov_species = re.sub(r'[<>]', '', str(prov_species)).strip()
 446 |             if prov_species:
 447 |                 provisional_species_counts[prov_species] = \
 448 |                     provisional_species_counts.get(prov_species, 0) + 1
 449 |                     
 450 |     # Sort by count desc, then name asc
 451 |     sorted_species = sorted(
 452 |         provisional_species_counts.items(),
 453 |         key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])
 454 |     )
 455 |     
 456 |     return [
 457 |         {'name': name, 'count': count}
 458 |         for name, count in sorted_species
 459 |     ]
 460 | 
 461 | 
 462 | def extract_sequences_from_observations(dna_observations: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
 463 |     """
 464 |     Convert analyzed observations to sequence dicts for the queue.
 465 |     
 466 |     Args:
 467 |         dna_observations: List of observation analysis dicts
 468 |         
 469 |     Returns:
 470 |         List of sequence dicts compatible with the sequence queue
 471 |     """
 472 |     sequences = []
 473 |     
 474 |     for obs_data in dna_observations:
 475 |         obs = obs_data['observation']
 476 |         cleaned_dna = obs_data['cleaned_dna']
 477 |         
 478 |         # Build a meaningful name from observation data
 479 |         obs_id = obs.get('id', 'unknown')
 480 |         
 481 |         # Priority: 
 482 |         # 1. Species Name Override (manual override)
 483 |         # 2. Provisional Species Name (more specific for DNA-barcoded specimens)
 484 |         # 3. Taxon name from iNaturalist identification
 485 |         
 486 |         species_name = get_field_value(obs, 'Species Name Override')
 487 |         if not species_name:
 488 |             species_name = get_field_value(obs, 'Provisional Species Name')
 489 |         
 490 |         if not species_name:
 491 |             taxon = obs.get('taxon', {})
 492 |             if taxon:
 493 |                 species_name = taxon.get('name', '')
 494 |         
 495 |         # Sanitize species name
 496 |         species_name = re.sub(r'[<>"\']', '', str(species_name)).strip()
 497 |         
 498 |         # Build sequence name: iNat_<id>
 499 |         seq_name = f"iNat_{obs_id}"
 500 |         
 501 |         sequences.append({
 502 |             'name': seq_name,
 503 |             'organism': species_name,
 504 |             'sequence': cleaned_dna,
 505 |             'source': 'inaturalist',
 506 |             'observation_id': str(obs_id)
 507 |         })
 508 |     
 509 |     return sequences
 510 | 
 511 | 
 512 | def fetch_inaturalist_data(url: str, mode: str = 'all') -> Dict:
 513 |     """
 514 |     Main entry point: validate URL and fetch/analyze observations.
 515 |     
 516 |     Args:
 517 |         url: iNaturalist URL (observation or search)
 518 |         mode: 'all' (default) to fetch both ITS and PSN data (for analysis),
 519 |               or 'its_only' to fetch only ITS sequences (for adding to queue).
 520 |         
 521 |     Returns:
 522 |         Dict with analysis results and extracted sequences
 523 |     """
 524 |     # Validate and parse URL
 525 |     url_info = validate_inaturalist_url(url)
 526 |     if not url_info:
 527 |         raise ValueError(
 528 |             "Invalid iNaturalist URL. Please enter a valid observation URL "
 529 |             "(e.g., https://www.inaturalist.org/observations/12345) or "
 530 |             "observations search URL."
 531 |         )
 532 |     
 533 |     # Fetch observations based on URL type
 534 |     truncated = False
 535 |     
 536 |     # Initialize result containers
 537 |     final_result = {
 538 |         'total_observations': 0,
 539 |         'total_its_observations': 0,
 540 |         'total_psn_observations': 0,
 541 |         'dna_count': 0,
 542 |         'dna_observations': [],
 543 |         'provisional_species': [],
 544 |         'sequences': [],
 545 |         'is_single': url_info['type'] == 'single_observation',
 546 |         'truncated': False,
 547 |         'total_available': 0
 548 |     }
 549 |     
 550 |     if url_info['type'] == 'single_observation':
 551 |         # Single observation: fetch once, analyze for both
 552 |         observation = fetch_single_observation(url_info['observation_id'])
 553 |         
 554 |         # Analyze ITS
 555 |         analysis = analyze_observations([observation])
 556 |         sequences = extract_sequences_from_observations(analysis['dna_observations'])
 557 |         
 558 |         # Analyze PSN independently (regardless of whether ITS exists)
 559 |         psn_histogram = analyze_provisional_species([observation])
 560 |         
 561 |         final_result.update({
 562 |             'total_observations': 1,
 563 |             'total_its_observations': 1 if analysis['dna_count'] > 0 else 0,
 564 |             'total_psn_observations': 1 if psn_histogram else 0,
 565 |             'fetched_its_count': 1,
 566 |             'fetched_psn_count': 1,
 567 |             'dna_count': analysis['dna_count'],
 568 |             'dna_observations': analysis['dna_observations'],
 569 |             'provisional_species': psn_histogram,
 570 |             'sequences': sequences,
 571 |         })
 572 |         
 573 |     else:
 574 |         # Search URL: perform queries based on mode
 575 |         base_params = build_base_params(url_info.get('query_params', {}))
 576 |         
 577 |         # 1. Fetch ITS observations (needed in all modes)
 578 |         its_result = fetch_observations_with_field_filter(base_params, "DNA Barcode ITS")
 579 |         its_obs = its_result['observations']
 580 |         
 581 |         # Analyze ITS results
 582 |         its_analysis = analyze_observations(its_obs)
 583 |         sequences = extract_sequences_from_observations(its_analysis['dna_observations'])
 584 |         
 585 |         final_result.update({
 586 |              # Use ITS available count as primary "match" metric for sequences
 587 |             'total_observations': its_result['total_available'],
 588 |             'total_its_observations': its_result['total_available'],
 589 |             'fetched_its_count': its_result['fetched_count'],
 590 |             'dna_count': its_analysis['dna_count'],
 591 |             'dna_observations': its_analysis['dna_observations'],
 592 |             'sequences': sequences,
 593 |             'truncated': its_result['truncated'],
 594 |             'total_available': its_result['total_available']
 595 |         })
 596 | 
 597 |         # 2. Fetch PSN observations (only if mode is 'all')
 598 |         if mode == 'all':
 599 |             psn_result = fetch_observations_with_field_filter(base_params, "Provisional Species Name")
 600 |             psn_obs = psn_result['observations']
 601 |             psn_histogram = analyze_provisional_species(psn_obs)
 602 |             
 603 |             final_result.update({
 604 |                 'total_psn_observations': psn_result['total_available'],
 605 |                 'fetched_psn_count': psn_result['fetched_count'],
 606 |                 'provisional_species': psn_histogram,
 607 |                 # Update truncated status to reflect if ANY query was truncated
 608 |                 'truncated': its_result['truncated'] or psn_result['truncated'],
 609 |                  # Update total available to be the max of either query
 610 |                 'total_available': max(its_result['total_available'], psn_result['total_available'])
 611 |             })
 612 |         
 613 |     return final_result

FILE: app/services/mycomap_service.py

   1 | """
   2 | Mycomap FASTA downloader service.
   3 | 
   4 | Fetches BLAST result sequences from Mycomap URLs for use in the tree builder.
   5 | Based on standalone script by Alan Rockefeller - June 30, 2025.
   6 | """
   7 | 
   8 | import html.parser
   9 | import logging
  10 | import re
  11 | import urllib.request
  12 | import urllib.parse
  13 | import urllib.error
  14 | from typing import Optional, Tuple
  15 | 
  16 | logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  17 | 
  18 | # Timeout for network requests in seconds
  19 | REQUEST_TIMEOUT = 10
  20 | 
  21 | # Mycomap API base URL
  22 | MYCOMAP_BASE_URL = "https://mycomap.com/index.php"
  23 | 
  24 | 
  25 | def validate_mycomap_url(url: str) -> Optional[str]:
  26 |     """
  27 |     Validate a Mycomap URL and extract the blast_id.
  28 |     
  29 |     Uses strict hostname checking to prevent bypass attacks like:
  30 |     - https://evil.com/?q=mycomap.com/r12345
  31 |     - https://mycomap.com.evil.com/r12345
  32 |     
  33 |     Args:
  34 |         url: The URL to validate (e.g., "https://mycomap.com/...r12345...")
  35 |         
  36 |     Returns:
  37 |         The blast_id (digits only) if valid, None otherwise.
  38 |     """
  39 |     if not url:
  40 |         return None
  41 |     
  42 |     try:
  43 |         parsed = urllib.parse.urlparse(url)
  44 |     except Exception:
  45 |         logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: could not parse URL: {url}")
  46 |         return None
  47 |     
  48 |     # Strict hostname check - must be exactly mycomap.com or www.mycomap.com
  49 |     hostname = (parsed.hostname or '').lower()
  50 |     valid_hostnames = ['mycomap.com', 'www.mycomap.com']
  51 |     if hostname not in valid_hostnames:
  52 |         logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: invalid hostname '{hostname}' (expected mycomap.com): {url}")
  53 |         return None
  54 |     
  55 |     # Enforce http or https protocol
  56 |     if parsed.scheme not in ('http', 'https'):
  57 |         logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: invalid scheme '{parsed.scheme}' (expected http/https): {url}")
  58 |         return None
  59 |     
  60 |     # Extract the r<digits> pattern from path or query
  61 |     # Use word boundary to avoid matching middle of other tokens
  62 |     search_text = parsed.path + '?' + (parsed.query or '')
  63 |     match = re.search(r'(?:^|[^a-zA-Z0-9])r(\d+)', search_text)
  64 |     if not match:
  65 |         logger.warning(f"URL validation failed: no r<digits> pattern found in: {url}")
  66 |         return None
  67 |     
  68 |     blast_id = match.group(1)
  69 |     logger.info(f"Validated Mycomap URL, extracted blast_id: {blast_id}")
  70 |     return blast_id
  71 | 
  72 | 
  73 | def _count_fasta_sequences(fasta_bytes: bytes) -> int:
  74 |     """Return the number of sequences in a FASTA file (bytes)."""
  75 |     return sum(1 for line in fasta_bytes.splitlines() if line.startswith(b'>'))
  76 | 
  77 | 
  78 | def _fetch_fasta(blast_id: str, endpoint: str) -> Tuple[bytes, Optional[str]]:
  79 |     """
  80 |     Fetch FASTA content from Mycomap.
  81 |     
  82 |     Args:
  83 |         blast_id: The blast ID to fetch
  84 |         endpoint: Either 'fasta' (NCBI) or 'localFasta' (local/MycoBLAST)
  85 |         
  86 |     Returns:
  87 |         Tuple of (fasta_bytes, error_message). If error, fasta_bytes will be empty.
  88 |     """
  89 |     # Double-check blast_id is only digits
  90 |     if not blast_id.isdigit():
  91 |         return b'', "Invalid blast_id format"
  92 | 
  93 |     params = urllib.parse.urlencode({
  94 |         'app': 'genbank',
  95 |         'module': 'genbank',
  96 |         'controller': 'blast',
  97 |         'do': endpoint,
  98 |         'id': blast_id
  99 |     })
 100 |     url = f"{MYCOMAP_BASE_URL}?{params}"
 101 |     
 102 |     opener = urllib.request.build_opener()
 103 |     opener.addheaders = [
 104 |         ('User-Agent', 'Dikarya-TreeBuilder/1.0'),
 105 |         ('Accept', '*/*')
 106 |     ]
 107 |     
 108 |     try:
 109 |         with opener.open(url, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT) as resp:
 110 |             content = resp.read()
 111 |         return content, None
 112 |     except urllib.error.URLError as e:
 113 |         error_msg = f"Network error fetching {endpoint}: {e}"
 114 |         logger.error(error_msg)
 115 |         return b'', error_msg
 116 |     except TimeoutError:
 117 |         error_msg = f"Request timed out after {REQUEST_TIMEOUT}s for {endpoint}"
 118 |         logger.error(error_msg)
 119 |         return b'', error_msg
 120 |     except Exception as e:
 121 |         error_msg = f"Unexpected error fetching {endpoint}: {e}"
 122 |         logger.error(error_msg, exc_info=True)
 123 |         return b'', error_msg
 124 | 
 125 | 
 126 | def fetch_mycomap_fasta(
 127 |     blast_id: str,
 128 |     include_ncbi: bool = True,
 129 |     include_local: bool = True
 130 | ) -> dict:
 131 |     """
 132 |     Fetch FASTA sequences from Mycomap BLAST results.
 133 |     
 134 |     Args:
 135 |         blast_id: The Mycomap blast ID (digits only)
 136 |         include_ncbi: Whether to include NCBI BLAST results
 137 |         include_local: Whether to include local MycoBLAST results
 138 |         
 139 |     Returns:
 140 |         Dict with keys:
 141 |         - fasta_content: str - Combined FASTA content
 142 |         - ncbi_count: int - Number of NCBI sequences fetched
 143 |         - local_count: int - Number of local sequences fetched
 144 |         - errors: list[str] - Any error messages
 145 |     """
 146 |     result = {
 147 |         'fasta_content': '',
 148 |         'ncbi_count': 0,
 149 |         'local_count': 0,
 150 |         'errors': []
 151 |     }
 152 |     
 153 |     if not include_ncbi and not include_local:
 154 |         result['errors'].append("At least one result type must be selected")
 155 |         return result
 156 |     
 157 |     fasta_parts = []
 158 |     
 159 |     # Fetch NCBI results
 160 |     if include_ncbi:
 161 |         logger.info(f"Fetching NCBI FASTA for blast_id: {blast_id}")
 162 |         ncbi_bytes, ncbi_error = _fetch_fasta(blast_id, 'fasta')
 163 |         if ncbi_error:
 164 |             result['errors'].append(ncbi_error)
 165 |         else:
 166 |             result['ncbi_count'] = _count_fasta_sequences(ncbi_bytes)
 167 |             if ncbi_bytes:
 168 |                 fasta_parts.append(ncbi_bytes.decode('utf-8', errors='replace'))
 169 |             logger.info(f"NCBI: fetched {len(ncbi_bytes)} bytes, {result['ncbi_count']} sequences")
 170 |     
 171 |     # Fetch local/MycoBLAST results
 172 |     if include_local:
 173 |         logger.info(f"Fetching local FASTA for blast_id: {blast_id}")
 174 |         local_bytes, local_error = _fetch_fasta(blast_id, 'localFasta')
 175 |         if local_error:
 176 |             result['errors'].append(local_error)
 177 |         else:
 178 |             result['local_count'] = _count_fasta_sequences(local_bytes)
 179 |             if local_bytes:
 180 |                 fasta_parts.append(local_bytes.decode('utf-8', errors='replace'))
 181 |             logger.info(f"Local: fetched {len(local_bytes)} bytes, {result['local_count']} sequences")
 182 |     
 183 |     # Combine FASTA content
 184 |     result['fasta_content'] = '\n'.join(fasta_parts)
 185 | 
 186 |     return result

 189 | # =============================================================================

 193 | class _BlastTableParser(html.parser.HTMLParser):
 194 |     """Stdlib HTML table extractor. Collects all tables as lists of rows of cell strings."""
 195 | 
 196 |     def __init__(self):
 197 |         super().__init__()
 198 |         self.tables = []       # list of tables
 199 |         self._cur_table = None  # list of rows while inside <table>
 200 |         self._cur_row = None    # list of cells while inside <tr>
 201 |         self._cur_cell = None   # str accumulator while inside <td>/<th>
 202 |         self._depth = 0         # table nesting depth
 203 | 
 204 |     def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
 205 |         if tag == 'table':
 206 |             self._depth += 1
 207 |             if self._depth == 1:
 208 |                 self._cur_table = []
 209 |         elif tag == 'tr' and self._cur_table is not None:
 210 |             self._cur_row = []
 211 |         elif tag in ('td', 'th') and self._cur_row is not None:
 212 |             self._cur_cell = []
 213 | 
 214 |     def handle_endtag(self, tag):
 215 |         if tag == 'table':
 216 |             if self._depth == 1 and self._cur_table is not None:
 217 |                 self.tables.append(self._cur_table)
 218 |                 self._cur_table = None
 219 |             self._depth = max(0, self._depth - 1)
 220 |         elif tag == 'tr' and self._cur_table is not None and self._cur_row is not None:
 221 |             self._cur_table.append(self._cur_row)
 222 |             self._cur_row = None
 223 |         elif tag in ('td', 'th') and self._cur_row is not None and self._cur_cell is not None:
 224 |             self._cur_row.append(' '.join(''.join(self._cur_cell).split()))
 225 |             self._cur_cell = None
 226 | 
 227 |     def handle_data(self, data):
 228 |         if self._cur_cell is not None:
 229 |             self._cur_cell.append(data)
 230 | 
 231 | 
 232 | def _parse_blast_metrics_table(rows: list) -> dict:
 233 |     """
 234 |     Given raw table rows, find the header row and parse per-accession metrics.
 235 | 
 236 |     Returns dict[bare_accession -> {identity, query_cover, subject_cover}].
 237 |     Returns {} if no usable header found or identity column missing.
 238 |     """
 239 |     if not rows:
 240 |         return {}
 241 | 
 242 |     header_idx = None
 243 |     header = []
 244 |     for i, row in enumerate(rows[:5]):
 245 |         lower = [c.lower() for c in row]
 246 |         if any('identity' in c or 'ident' in c or 'coverage' in c or 'cover' in c for c in lower):
 247 |             header_idx = i
 248 |             header = lower
 249 |             break
 250 | 
 251 |     if header_idx is None or not header:
 252 |         return {}
 253 | 
 254 |     # Detect column indices
 255 |     def find_col(*keywords):
 256 |         for j, h in enumerate(header):
 257 |             if any(kw in h for kw in keywords):
 258 |                 return j
 259 |         return None
 260 | 
 261 |     acc_col = find_col('accession', 'subject id', 'subject', 'id')
 262 |     if acc_col is None:
 263 |         acc_col = 0
 264 |     ident_col = find_col('identity', 'ident')
 265 |     qcov_col = find_col('query cov', 'query cover', 'q. cov', 'qcov')
 266 |     scov_col = find_col('subject cov', 'subj cov', 's. cov', 'scov')
 267 | 
 268 |     if ident_col is None:
 269 |         return {}
 270 | 
 271 |     def _to_float(val):
 272 |         if val is None:
 273 |             return None
 274 |         try:
 275 |             return float(val.strip().rstrip('%').strip())
 276 |         except (ValueError, AttributeError):
 277 |             return None
 278 | 
 279 |     result = {}
 280 |     for row in rows[header_idx + 1:]:
 281 |         if len(row) <= max(acc_col, ident_col):
 282 |             continue
 283 |         raw_acc = row[acc_col].split()[0] if row[acc_col] else ''
 284 |         if not raw_acc:
 285 |             continue
 286 |         bare_acc = raw_acc.split('.')[0]
 287 |         identity = _to_float(row[ident_col] if len(row) > ident_col else None)
 288 |         query_cover = _to_float(row[qcov_col] if qcov_col is not None and len(row) > qcov_col else None)
 289 |         subject_cover = _to_float(row[scov_col] if scov_col is not None and len(row) > scov_col else None)
 290 |         result[bare_acc] = {
 291 |             'identity': identity,
 292 |             'query_cover': query_cover,
 293 |             'subject_cover': subject_cover,
 294 |         }
 295 |         # Also store with version suffix as a fallback key
 296 |         if raw_acc != bare_acc:
 297 |             result.setdefault(raw_acc, result[bare_acc])
 298 | 
 299 |     return result
 300 | 
 301 | 
 302 | def fetch_mycomap_blast_metrics(blast_id: str) -> dict:
 303 |     """
 304 |     Fetch BLAST metrics from the MycoMap user-facing BLAST results page.
 305 | 
 306 |     Args:
 307 |         blast_id: Digits-only blast ID string.
 308 | 
 309 |     Returns:
 310 |         dict[bare_accession -> {identity, query_cover, subject_cover}]
 311 |         Returns {} on any error (non-fatal).
 312 |     """
 313 |     if not blast_id.isdigit():
 314 |         logger.warning(f"fetch_mycomap_blast_metrics: invalid blast_id '{blast_id}'")
 315 |         return {}
 316 | 
 317 |     url = f"https://mycomap.com/genetics/blast-search/r{blast_id}"
 318 | 
 319 |     opener = urllib.request.build_opener()
 320 |     opener.addheaders = [
 321 |         ('User-Agent', 'Dikarya-TreeBuilder/1.0'),
 322 |         ('Accept', 'text/html,*/*')
 323 |     ]
 324 | 
 325 |     try:
 326 |         with opener.open(url, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT) as resp:
 327 |             content = resp.read().decode('utf-8', errors='replace')
 328 |     except Exception as e:
 329 |         logger.warning(f"fetch_mycomap_blast_metrics: could not fetch page: {e}")
 330 |         return {}
 331 | 
 332 |     try:
 333 |         parser = _BlastTableParser()
 334 |         parser.feed(content)
 335 |         combined = {}
 336 |         for table_rows in parser.tables:
 337 |             metrics = _parse_blast_metrics_table(table_rows)
 338 |             # Later tables don't overwrite earlier ones (prefer first match)
 339 |             for acc, m in metrics.items():
 340 |                 combined.setdefault(acc, m)
 341 |         logger.info(f"fetch_mycomap_blast_metrics: parsed {len(combined)} accession(s) from {len(parser.tables)} table(s)")
 342 |         return combined
 343 |     except Exception as e:
 344 |         logger.warning(f"fetch_mycomap_blast_metrics: parse error: {e}", exc_info=True)
 345 |         return {}


========================================================================================
FILE: app/services/orientation_service.py
========================================================================================

   1 | """
   2 | Sequence orientation service module.
   3 | 
   4 | Auto-orients fungal ITS sequences using conserved motifs (ITS1-F, 5.8S core, ITS4).
   5 | Based on fixfasta.py by Alan Rockefeller, adapted for pipeline integration.
   6 | 
   7 | Key features:
   8 | - Uses IUPAC-aware fuzzy matching (≤4 mismatches over 20bp)
   9 | - Conservative reversal: requires 5.8S core + at least one flanking motif
  10 | - Safe for non-ITS sequences: without strong motif evidence, sequences pass unchanged
  11 | """
  12 | 
  13 | import logging
  14 | import re
  15 | from dataclasses import dataclass
  16 | from pathlib import Path
  17 | from textwrap import wrap
  18 | from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
  19 | 
  20 | logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  21 | 
  22 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  23 | # Constants
  24 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  25 | 
  26 | # Conserved ITS motifs in forward orientation
  27 | FWD_MOTIFS = [
  28 |     "TCCGTAGGTGAACCTGCGG",    # ITS1-F  (18S end)
  29 |     "GCATCGATGAAGAACGCAGC",   # 5.8S core
  30 |     "TCCTCCGCTTATTGATATGC"    # ITS4    (28S start)
  31 | ]
  32 | 
  33 | DEFAULT_MAX_MM = 4   # Per-motif mismatch ceiling
  34 | TIE_EPS = 0.3        # Score delta below which we call it a tie
  35 | WRAP_WIDTH = 80      # Output FASTA line length
  36 | 
  37 | CORE_IDX = 1          # 5.8S core is motif #1
  38 | FLANK_IDXS = (0, 2)   # ITS1-F and ITS4
  39 | CORE_MAX_MM = 2       # Require a reasonably good 5.8S hit to reverse
  40 | 
  41 | # IUPAC complement table
  42 | IUPAC_COMP = str.maketrans(
  43 |     "ACGTRYMKWSVHDBNacgtrymkwsvhdbn-",
  44 |     "TGCAYRKMWSBDHVNtgcayrkmwsbdhvn-"
  45 | )
  46 | 
  47 | # Precomputed bitmask lookup for IUPAC codes
  48 | I2M: Dict[str, int] = {
  49 |     **{b: 1 << i for i, b in enumerate("ACGT")},
  50 |     "R": 5, "Y": 10, "S": 6, "W": 9,
  51 |     "K": 12, "M": 3, "B": 14, "D": 13,
  52 |     "H": 11, "V": 7, "N": 15, "-": 0
  53 | }
  54 | I2M.update({k.lower(): v for k, v in I2M.items()})
  55 | 
  56 | 
  57 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  58 | # Helper Functions
  59 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  60 | 
  61 | def revcomp(seq: str) -> str:
  62 |     """Return reverse complement of a sequence."""
  63 |     return seq.translate(IUPAC_COMP)[::-1]
  64 | 
  65 | 
  66 | # Precompute reverse complements of motifs
  67 | REV_MOTIFS = [revcomp(m) for m in FWD_MOTIFS]
  68 | 
  69 | 
  70 | @dataclass(frozen=True)
  71 | class Hit:
  72 |     """Represents a motif hit with mismatches and position."""
  73 |     mism: int
  74 |     pos: int
  75 | 
  76 | 
  77 | def best_hit(seq: str, motif: str, max_mm: int = DEFAULT_MAX_MM) -> Optional[Hit]:
  78 |     """
  79 |     Find best (fewest mismatches, earliest) fuzzy occurrence of motif in seq.
  80 | 
  81 |     IMPORTANT: 'N' and '-' are treated as mismatches (no evidence), to avoid
  82 |     false motif hits in low-quality/ambiguous regions.
  83 |     """
  84 |     motif_len = len(motif)
  85 |     seq_len = len(seq)
  86 |     if seq_len < motif_len:
  87 |         return None
  88 | 
  89 |     best: Optional[Hit] = None
  90 |     seq_upper = seq.upper()
  91 |     motif_upper = motif.upper()
  92 | 
  93 |     motif_masks = [I2M.get(c, 0) for c in motif_upper]
  94 | 
  95 |     for i in range(seq_len - motif_len + 1):
  96 |         mism = 0
  97 |         for j in range(motif_len):
  98 |             c = seq_upper[i + j]
  99 | 
 100 |             # Treat ambiguous/unknown as mismatch
 101 |             if c == "N" or c == "-":
 102 |                 mism += 1
 103 |                 if mism > max_mm:
 104 |                     break
 105 |                 continue
 106 | 
 107 |             seq_mask = I2M.get(c, 0)
 108 |             if not (seq_mask & motif_masks[j]):
 109 |                 mism += 1
 110 |                 if mism > max_mm:
 111 |                     break
 112 |         else:
 113 |             if best is None or (mism, i) < (best.mism, best.pos):
 114 |                 best = Hit(mism, i)
 115 |                 if mism == 0:
 116 |                     return best
 117 | 
 118 |     return best
 119 | 
 120 | 
 121 | @dataclass
 122 | class OrientationStats:
 123 |     """Statistics for orientation decision."""
 124 |     hits: List[Hit]
 125 |     total_mm: int
 126 |     best: Optional[Hit]
 127 |     hit_map: Dict[int, Hit]
 128 | 
 129 | 
 130 | def collect_stats(seq: str, motifs: List[str], max_mm: int = DEFAULT_MAX_MM) -> OrientationStats:
 131 |     """Collect statistics for a set of motifs."""
 132 |     hits: List[Hit] = []
 133 |     hit_map: Dict[int, Hit] = {}
 134 | 
 135 |     for idx, motif in enumerate(motifs):
 136 |         hit = best_hit(seq, motif, max_mm)
 137 |         if hit:
 138 |             hits.append(hit)
 139 |             hit_map[idx] = hit
 140 | 
 141 |     if not hits:
 142 |         return OrientationStats([], float('inf'), None, hit_map={})
 143 | 
 144 |     total_mm = sum(h.mism for h in hits)
 145 |     best = min(hits, key=lambda h: (h.mism, h.pos))
 146 | 
 147 |     return OrientationStats(hits, total_mm, best, hit_map=hit_map)
 148 | 
 149 | 
 150 | def has_core_and_flank(stats: OrientationStats) -> bool:
 151 |     """Check if stats have both core and at least one flanking motif."""
 152 |     return (CORE_IDX in stats.hit_map) and any(i in stats.hit_map for i in FLANK_IDXS)
 153 | 
 154 | 
 155 | def decide_orientation(seq: str, max_mm: int = DEFAULT_MAX_MM) -> Tuple[str, OrientationStats, OrientationStats]:
 156 |     """
 157 |     Determine sequence orientation based on motif matches.
 158 | 
 159 |     Returns: (orientation, forward_stats, reverse_stats)
 160 |     orientation is one of: "forward", "reverse", "uncertain"
 161 |     """
 162 |     fwd = collect_stats(seq, FWD_MOTIFS, max_mm)
 163 |     rev = collect_stats(seq, REV_MOTIFS, max_mm)
 164 | 
 165 |     # No hits at all
 166 |     if not fwd.hits and not rev.hits:
 167 |         return "uncertain", fwd, rev
 168 | 
 169 |     def reverse_allowed() -> bool:
 170 |         """Check if reversing is allowed based on conservative criteria."""
 171 |         # Check 1: Must have core + at least one flank
 172 |         if not has_core_and_flank(rev):
 173 |             return False
 174 | 
 175 |         # Check 2: Require a decent 5.8S-core hit in the reverse orientation
 176 |         rev_core = rev.hit_map.get(CORE_IDX)
 177 |         if rev_core is None or rev_core.mism > CORE_MAX_MM:
 178 |             return False
 179 | 
 180 |         # Check 3: Positional sanity. In a reversed raw sequence:
 181 |         # rev(ITS4) [idx 2] should be BEFORE core [idx 1].
 182 |         # rev(ITS1-F) [idx 0] should be AFTER core [idx 1].
 183 |         if 2 in rev.hit_map and rev.hit_map[2].pos > rev_core.pos:
 184 |             return False
 185 |         if 0 in rev.hit_map and rev.hit_map[0].pos < rev_core.pos:
 186 |             return False
 187 | 
 188 |         return True
 189 | 
 190 |     # Primary winner selection (hits, then total mismatches, then best hit)
 191 |     if len(fwd.hits) != len(rev.hits):
 192 |         winner = "forward" if len(fwd.hits) > len(rev.hits) else "reverse"
 193 |     elif fwd.total_mm != rev.total_mm:
 194 |         winner = "forward" if fwd.total_mm < rev.total_mm else "reverse"
 195 |     else:
 196 |         # Final tie-breaker: earliest best hit (position scaled)
 197 |         assert fwd.best is not None and rev.best is not None
 198 |         score_f = fwd.best.mism * 10 + fwd.best.pos / 1000
 199 |         score_r = rev.best.mism * 10 + rev.best.pos / 1000
 200 | 
 201 |         if abs(score_f - score_r) < TIE_EPS:
 202 |             return "uncertain", fwd, rev
 203 | 
 204 |         winner = "forward" if score_f < score_r else "reverse"
 205 | 
 206 |     # Enforce conservative reversal rule
 207 |     if winner == "reverse" and not reverse_allowed():
 208 |         # If forward has at least one real hit, keep forward; otherwise uncertain
 209 |         return ("forward" if fwd.hits else "uncertain"), fwd, rev
 210 | 
 211 |     return winner, fwd, rev
 212 | 
 213 | 
 214 | def fasta_reader(fasta_text: str) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
 215 |     """
 216 |     Parse FASTA text into list of (header, sequence) tuples.
 217 |     
 218 |     Handles stray '>' symbols in sequence lines.
 219 |     """
 220 |     records: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
 221 |     header: Optional[str] = None
 222 |     sequence_parts: List[str] = []
 223 |     line_num = 0
 224 | 
 225 |     for raw_line in fasta_text.splitlines():
 226 |         line_num += 1
 227 |         line = raw_line.rstrip()
 228 | 
 229 |         if line.startswith(">"):
 230 |             # Yield previous sequence if exists
 231 |             if header is not None:
 232 |                 records.append((header, "".join(sequence_parts)))
 233 | 
 234 |             # Start new sequence
 235 |             header = line[1:].strip() or f"<empty_header_line_{line_num}>"
 236 |             sequence_parts = []
 237 |         else:
 238 |             # Handle stray '>' symbols in sequence lines
 239 |             if ">" in line:
 240 |                 parts = line.split(">")
 241 |                 sequence_parts.append(parts[0].strip())
 242 | 
 243 |                 # Each subsequent part becomes a new sequence
 244 |                 for i, part in enumerate(parts[1:], 1):
 245 |                     logger.warning(
 246 |                         f"Line {line_num}: Stray '>' found, creating new sequence "
 247 |                         f"'{part.strip() or f'<empty_from_stray_{line_num}_{i}'}'"
 248 |                     )
 249 |                     if header is not None:
 250 |                         records.append((header, "".join(sequence_parts)))
 251 |                     header = part.strip() or f"<empty_from_stray_{line_num}_{i}>"
 252 |                     sequence_parts = []
 253 |             else:
 254 |                 # Normal sequence line
 255 |                 sequence_parts.append(line.strip())
 256 | 
 257 |     # Don't forget the last sequence
 258 |     if header is not None:
 259 |         records.append((header, "".join(sequence_parts)))
 260 | 
 261 |     return records
 262 | 
 263 | 
 264 | def format_fasta(header: str, seq: str, wrap_width: int = WRAP_WIDTH) -> str:
 265 |     """Format a single FASTA record with wrapped sequence lines."""
 266 |     lines = [f">{header}"]
 267 |     for chunk in wrap(seq, wrap_width):
 268 |         lines.append(chunk)
 269 |     return "\n".join(lines)
 270 | 
 271 | 
 272 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 273 | # Main Service Function
 274 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 275 | 
 276 | def fix_sequence_orientation(
 277 |     fasta_text: str,
 278 |     max_mm: int = DEFAULT_MAX_MM,
 279 | ) -> Tuple[str, Dict[str, int]]:
 280 |     """
 281 |     Auto-orient ITS sequences using conserved motifs.
 282 |     
 283 |     This function analyzes each sequence for ITS motifs and reverse-complements
 284 |     sequences that appear to be in the wrong orientation. Non-ITS sequences
 285 |     (or sequences without strong motif evidence) pass through unchanged.
 286 |     
 287 |     Args:
 288 |         fasta_text: Input FASTA text content
 289 |         max_mm: Maximum mismatches per motif (default: 4)
 290 |     
 291 |     Returns:
 292 |         Tuple of (fixed_fasta_text, stats_dict)
 293 |         stats_dict contains: total, forward, reverse, uncertain, empty
 294 |     """
 295 |     records = fasta_reader(fasta_text)
 296 |     
 297 |     stats = {
 298 |         "total": 0,
 299 |         "forward": 0,
 300 |         "reverse": 0,
 301 |         "uncertain": 0,
 302 |         "empty": 0,
 303 |     }
 304 |     
 305 |     output_records: List[str] = []
 306 |     reversed_headers: List[str] = []
 307 |     uncertain_headers: List[str] = []
 308 |     
 309 |     for header, seq in records:
 310 |         stats["total"] += 1
 311 |         
 312 |         if not seq:
 313 |             stats["empty"] += 1
 314 |             logger.warning(f"Empty sequence for '{header}'")
 315 |             output_records.append(format_fasta(header, seq))
 316 |             continue
 317 |         
 318 |         # Validate sequence (warn about non-IUPAC characters)
 319 |         invalid_chars = re.findall(r"[^ACGTRYMKWSVHDBNacgtrymkwsvhdbn\-]", seq)
 320 |         if invalid_chars:
 321 |             unique_chars = sorted(set(invalid_chars))
 322 |             logger.warning(f"Non-IUPAC symbols in '{header}': {', '.join(unique_chars)}")
 323 |         
 324 |         # Determine orientation
 325 |         orientation, fwd_stats, rev_stats = decide_orientation(seq, max_mm)
 326 |         stats[orientation] += 1
 327 |         
 328 |         # Track reversed and uncertain sequences
 329 |         if orientation == "reverse":
 330 |             reversed_headers.append(header)
 331 |             output_records.append(format_fasta(header, revcomp(seq).upper()))
 332 |         elif orientation == "uncertain":
 333 |             uncertain_headers.append(header)
 334 |             output_records.append(format_fasta(header, seq.upper()))
 335 |         else:
 336 |             # Forward - keep as is
 337 |             output_records.append(format_fasta(header, seq.upper()))
 338 |         
 339 |         # Log uncertain orientations
 340 |         if orientation == "uncertain":
 341 |             logger.debug(f"Uncertain orientation for '{header}'")
 342 |     
 343 |     # Log summary
 344 |     if reversed_headers:
 345 |         logger.info(f"Reversed {len(reversed_headers)} sequence(s): {reversed_headers[:5]}{'...' if len(reversed_headers) > 5 else ''}")
 346 |     
 347 |     if uncertain_headers:
 348 |         logger.debug(f"Uncertain orientation for {len(uncertain_headers)} sequence(s)")
 349 |     
 350 |     output_text = "\n".join(output_records) + "\n" if output_records else ""
 351 |     
 352 |     return output_text, stats
 353 | 
 354 | 
 355 | def fix_sequence_orientation_file(
 356 |     input_path: Path,
 357 |     output_path: Optional[Path] = None,
 358 |     max_mm: int = DEFAULT_MAX_MM,
 359 | ) -> Dict[str, int]:
 360 |     """
 361 |     Auto-orient ITS sequences in a file.
 362 |     
 363 |     Args:
 364 |         input_path: Path to input FASTA file
 365 |         output_path: Path to output FASTA file (defaults to overwriting input)
 366 |         max_mm: Maximum mismatches per motif
 367 |     
 368 |     Returns:
 369 |         Stats dictionary with orientation counts
 370 |     """
 371 |     if output_path is None:
 372 |         output_path = input_path
 373 |     
 374 |     fasta_text = input_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
 375 |     fixed_text, stats = fix_sequence_orientation(fasta_text, max_mm)
 376 |     output_path.write_text(fixed_text, encoding="utf-8")
 377 |     
 378 |     return stats

FILE: app/services/raxml_validator.py

   1 | import re
   2 | import random
   3 | from typing import Dict, Any, Tuple, Optional
   4 | from dataclasses import dataclass
   5 | 
   6 | # Constants
   7 | MIN_BOOTSTRAP_CAP = 100
   8 | MAX_BOOTSTRAP_CAP = 2000
   9 | DEFAULT_BOOTSTRAP_CAP = 500  # "Standard" preset cap
  10 | 
  11 | MAX_TOTAL_START_TREES = 40
  12 | 
  13 | # Presets Definitions
  14 | RUN_PRESETS = {
  15 |     'fast_good': {'pars': 2, 'rand': 0},
  16 |     'standard': {'pars': 5, 'rand': 0},
  17 |     'publication': {'pars': 10, 'rand': 10},
  18 |     'maximum': {'pars': 20, 'rand': 20},
  19 | }
  20 | 
  21 | BOOTSTRAP_PRESETS = {
  22 |     'standard': 500,
  23 |     'publication': 1000,
  24 |     'maximum': 2000,
  25 | }
  26 | 
  27 | # Supported Models (Base matrices)
  28 | VALID_DNA_MODELS = {
  29 |     'JC', 'K80', 'F81', 'HKY', 'TN93ef', 'TN93', 'K81', 'K81uf', 
  30 |     'TPM2', 'TPM2uf', 'TPM3', 'TPM3uf', 'TIM1', 'TIM1uf', 'TIM2', 'TIM2uf', 
  31 |     'TIM3', 'TIM3uf', 'TVMef', 'TVM', 'SYM', 'GTR', 'BIN', 'DNA010010',
  32 |     # Diploid / specialized models
  33 |     'GT10', 'GTGTR4', 'GTJC', 'GTHKY4', 'GTGTR', 
  34 |     'GT16', 'GT16JC', 'GT16GTR'
  35 | }
  36 | 
  37 | VALID_AA_MODELS = {
  38 |     m.upper() for m in {
  39 |         'Blosum62', 'cpREV', 'Dayhoff', 'DCMut', 'DEN', 'FLAVI', 'FLU', 'HIVb',
  40 |         'HIVw', 'JTT', 'JTT-DCMut', 'LG', 'mtART', 'mtINV', 'mtMAM', 'mtMET', 
  41 |         'mtREV', 'mtVER', 'mtZOA', 'PMB', 'Q.pfam', 'Q.bird', 'Q.insect', 
  42 |         'Q.mammal', 'Q.plant', 'Q.yeast', 'rtREV', 'stmtREV', 'VT', 'WAG', 
  43 |         'LG4M', 'LG4X', 'PROTGTR'
  44 |     }
  45 | }
  46 | 
  47 | # Regex for common modifiers (G, I, R, F, ASC, etc.)
  48 | # Matches:
  49 | # +G, +G4, +G[rate], +G{alpha}
  50 | # +R, +R4, +R{...}
  51 | # +F, +FC, +FO, +FE, +FU{...}
  52 | # +I, +IC, +IO, +IU{...}
  53 | # +ASC_LEWIS, +ASC_FELS{...}, +ASC_STAM{...}
  54 | # +M{...}
  55 | VALID_MODIFIER_REGEX = re.compile(
  56 |     r"^\+("
  57 |     r"G\d*[ma]?|"          # Gamma (G, G4, G4m, G4a)
  58 |     r"GA|"                 # Gamma median
  59 |     r"R\d*|"               # FreeRate (R, R4)
  60 |     r"F[COEU]?|"           # Frequencies (F, FC, FO, FE, FU)
  61 |     r"I[COU]?|"            # Invariant (I, IO, IC, IU)
  62 |     r"ASC_(LEWIS|FELS|STAM)|" # Ascertainment
  63 |     r"M[i]?"               # Character mapping
  64 |     r")(\{[^}]+\})?$"      # Optional parameters in braces
  65 | )
  66 | 
  67 | def _normalize_raxml_model(model_str: str) -> str:
  68 |     """
  69 |     Normalize RAxML model string.
  70 |     - Removes ALL whitespace.
  71 |     - Removes trailing commas.
  72 |     - Fixes spaced partition assignments (e.g., ", noname = " -> ",noname=").
  73 |     """
  74 |     if not model_str:
  75 |         return ""
  76 |     
  77 |     # Remove all whitespace
  78 |     s = re.sub(r"\s+", "", model_str)
  79 |     
  80 |     # Remove trailing commas
  81 |     while s.endswith(","):
  82 |         s = s[:-1]
  83 |         
  84 |     return s
  85 | 
  86 | def _split_model_string(model_str: str, delimiter: str = ',') -> list[str]:
  87 |     """
  88 |     Split model string by delimiter, respecting braces {...}.
  89 |     Commas inside braces (e.g. inside a map or generic parameter) are ignored.
  90 |     """
  91 |     parts = []
  92 |     current = ""
  93 |     depth = 0
  94 |     
  95 |     for char in model_str:
  96 |         if char == '{':
  97 |             depth += 1
  98 |             current += char
  99 |         elif char == '}':
 100 |             depth -= 1
 101 |             current += char
 102 |         elif char == delimiter and depth == 0:
 103 |             parts.append(current)
 104 |             current = ""
 105 |         else:
 106 |             current += char
 107 |             
 108 |     if current:
 109 |         parts.append(current)
 110 |     elif parts: # Case where string ends with delimiter?
 111 |         parts.append("")
 112 |         
 113 |     return parts
 114 | 
 115 | @dataclass
 116 | class ResolvedRaxmlParams:
 117 |     """Validated and resolved parameters ready for command generation."""
 118 |     enable_bootstrap: bool
 119 |     bootstrap_cap: int
 120 |     start_tree_spec: str
 121 |     model: str
 122 |     seed: int
 123 |     data_type: str  # DNA or AA
 124 |     warnings: list[str]
 125 |     outgroup: Optional[str] = None
 126 |     enable_moose: bool = False
 127 |     enable_early_stopping: bool = False
 128 | 
 129 | 
 130 | def validate_and_resolve_raxml_params(params: Dict[str, Any], data_type: str = 'DNA') -> ResolvedRaxmlParams:
 131 |     """
 132 |     Validate raw input parameters and resolve them to safe, executable values.
 133 |     
 134 |     Args:
 135 |         params: Raw dictionary from client request (TreeBuilderParams fields).
 136 |         data_type: 'DNA' or 'AA' (detected from MSA usually).
 137 |         
 138 |     Returns:
 139 |         ResolvedRaxmlParams object with safe values and warnings list.
 140 |     """
 141 |     warnings = []
 142 | 
 143 |     # 1. Bootstrapping Logic
 144 |     enable_bootstrap = bool(params.get('enable_bootstrap', True))  # Default True matches legacy behavior somewhat
 145 |     bootstrap_cap = DEFAULT_BOOTSTRAP_CAP
 146 |     
 147 |     if enable_bootstrap:
 148 |         # Determine requested cap
 149 |         req_cap = None
 150 |         
 151 |         # Check custom cap first (if provided and valid)
 152 |         if params.get('bootstrap_cap') is not None:
 153 |              try:
 154 |                  req_cap = int(params['bootstrap_cap'])
 155 |              except (ValueError, TypeError):
 156 |                  warnings.append("Invalid bootstrap_cap provided; using preset default.")
 157 |                  pass
 158 |         
 159 |         # Fallback to preset if no custom cap
 160 |         if req_cap is None:
 161 |             bs_preset = params.get('bootstrap_preset', 'standard')
 162 |             req_cap = BOOTSTRAP_PRESETS.get(bs_preset, DEFAULT_BOOTSTRAP_CAP)
 163 | 
 164 |         # Clamp
 165 |         if req_cap < MIN_BOOTSTRAP_CAP:
 166 |             warnings.append(f"Requested bootstrap cap {req_cap} too low; clamped to {MIN_BOOTSTRAP_CAP}.")
 167 |             bootstrap_cap = MIN_BOOTSTRAP_CAP
 168 |         elif req_cap > MAX_BOOTSTRAP_CAP:
 169 |             warnings.append(f"Requested bootstrap cap {req_cap} exceeded limit; clamped to {MAX_BOOTSTRAP_CAP}.")
 170 |             bootstrap_cap = MAX_BOOTSTRAP_CAP
 171 |         else:
 172 |             bootstrap_cap = req_cap
 173 |     else:
 174 |         bootstrap_cap = 0  # Irrelevant but safe value
 175 | 
 176 |     # 2. Starting Trees Logic
 177 |     run_preset = params.get('run_preset', 'fast_good')
 178 |     preset_trees = RUN_PRESETS.get(run_preset, RUN_PRESETS['fast_good'])
 179 |     
 180 |     pars_n = preset_trees['pars']
 181 |     rand_n = preset_trees['rand']
 182 | 
 183 |     # Check override
 184 |     override_str = params.get('start_tree_override')
 185 |     if override_str:
 186 |         # Expected format: "pars{N}" or "pars{N},rand{M}" or "rand{M}"
 187 |         # Parse flexible inputs
 188 |         p_match = re.search(r'pars\{(\d+)\}', override_str)
 189 |         r_match = re.search(r'rand\{(\d+)\}', override_str)
 190 |         
 191 |         if p_match or r_match:
 192 |             try:
 193 |                 ov_pars = int(p_match.group(1)) if p_match else 0
 194 |                 ov_rand = int(r_match.group(1)) if r_match else 0
 195 |                 
 196 |                 if ov_pars + ov_rand == 0:
 197 |                      warnings.append("Start tree override resulted in 0 trees; ignored.")
 198 |                 elif ov_pars + ov_rand > MAX_TOTAL_START_TREES:
 199 |                      warnings.append(f"Requested total start trees ({ov_pars + ov_rand}) exceeds limit ({MAX_TOTAL_START_TREES}); using preset '{run_preset}' instead.")
 200 |                 else:
 201 |                     pars_n = ov_pars
 202 |                     rand_n = ov_rand
 203 |             except ValueError:
 204 |                 warnings.append("Failed to parse start tree override; using preset.")
 205 |         else:
 206 |              warnings.append("Invalid start tree override format; using preset.")
 207 | 
 208 |     # Construct validated spec
 209 |     specs = []
 210 |     if pars_n > 0:
 211 |         specs.append(f"pars{{{pars_n}}}")
 212 |     if rand_n > 0:
 213 |         specs.append(f"rand{{{rand_n}}}")
 214 |     start_tree_spec = ",".join(specs)
 215 |     if not start_tree_spec:
 216 |         # Fallback safety
 217 |         start_tree_spec = "pars{2}"
 218 | 
 219 |     # 3. Model Logic
 220 |     model = params.get('model', '').strip()
 221 |     enable_moose = bool(params.get('moose_enabled', False))
 222 |     
 223 |     if not model and not enable_moose:
 224 |         # Default defaults
 225 |         model = "GTR+G" if data_type == "DNA" else "LG+G"
 226 | 
 227 |     if model and not enable_moose:
 228 |         # Support Partitioned Models: "Model, Part=Range, ..."
 229 |         # 1. Split top-level commas
 230 |         raw_parts = _split_model_string(model, ',')
 231 |         
 232 |         validated_parts = []
 233 |         model_valid = True
 234 |         
 235 |         for part in raw_parts:
 236 |             # Normalize part (remove spaces)
 237 |             part_norm = _normalize_raxml_model(part)
 238 |             
 239 |             if not part_norm:
 240 |                 continue
 241 |                 
 242 |             # Check if it's a Partition Definition (contains '=')
 243 |             # Example: part1=1-100 or gene1 = 1-500/2
 244 |             # But wait, modifiers don't usually have = outside braces.
 245 |             # Base models don't either.
 246 |             if '=' in part_norm:
 247 |                  # Clean validation for partition assignment
 248 |                  # part_name = range_spec
 249 |                  # Split on first =
 250 |                  p_name, p_range = part_norm.split('=', 1)
 251 |                  
 252 |                  # Validate Name
 253 |                  if not re.match(r"^[A-Za-z0-9_]+$", p_name):
 254 |                       warnings.append(f"Invalid partition name '{p_name}'.")
 255 |                       model_valid = False
 256 |                  
 257 |                  # Validate Range spec
 258 |                  # Allow digits, dash, comma, slash (codons)
 259 |                  if not re.match(r"^[0-9\-\,\/]+$", p_range):
 260 |                       warnings.append(f"Invalid partition range '{p_range}'.")
 261 |                       model_valid = False
 262 |                       
 263 |                  validated_parts.append(part_norm)
 264 |                  continue
 265 | 
 266 |             # Identify if this is a Model string (Base + Modifiers)
 267 |             # 1. Parse into Base + Modifiers
 268 |             model_parts = []
 269 |             current = ""
 270 |             depth = 0
 271 |             for char in part_norm:
 272 |                 if char == '{': depth += 1
 273 |                 elif char == '}': depth -= 1
 274 |                 
 275 |                 if char == '+' and depth == 0:
 276 |                     model_parts.append(current)
 277 |                     current = ""
 278 |                 else:
 279 |                     current += char
 280 |             model_parts.append(current) 
 281 |             
 282 |             base_part = model_parts[0]
 283 |             modifiers = model_parts[1:]
 284 |             
 285 |             # 2. Validate Base
 286 |             base_name_match = re.match(r"^([A-Za-z0-9_\.]+)(\{.*\})?$", base_part)
 287 |             
 288 |             part_is_valid = True
 289 |             
 290 |             if not base_name_match:
 291 |                  warnings.append(f"Invalid model component '{base_part}'.")
 292 |                  part_is_valid = False
 293 |             else:
 294 |                  base_name = base_name_match.group(1).upper()
 295 |                  base_param = base_name_match.group(2)
 296 |                  
 297 |                  valid_set = VALID_DNA_MODELS if data_type == "DNA" else VALID_AA_MODELS
 298 |                  is_multi = base_name.startswith("MULTI") and ("_MK" in base_name or "_GTR" in base_name)
 299 |                  
 300 |                  if base_name not in valid_set and not is_multi:
 301 |                       warnings.append(f"Unknown substitution model '{base_name}'.")
 302 |                       part_is_valid = False
 303 |                       
 304 |                  if base_param and not _is_safe_model_param(base_param[1:-1]):
 305 |                       warnings.append(f"Base parameter '{base_param}' ignored/unsafe.")
 306 |                       part_is_valid = False
 307 | 
 308 |             # 3. Validate Modifiers
 309 |             if part_is_valid:
 310 |                 for mod in modifiers:
 311 |                     mod_str = "+" + mod 
 312 |                     if not VALID_MODIFIER_REGEX.match(mod_str):
 313 |                          warnings.append(f"Unknown modifier '{mod_str}'.")
 314 |                          part_is_valid = False
 315 |                     else:
 316 |                          p_match = re.search(r"\{([^}]+)\}", mod)
 317 |                          if p_match:
 318 |                              if not _is_safe_model_param(p_match.group(1)):
 319 |                                  warnings.append(f"Modifier param '{p_match.group(0)}' unsafe.")
 320 |                                  part_is_valid = False
 321 |             
 322 |             if part_is_valid:
 323 |                 validated_parts.append(part_norm)
 324 |             else:
 325 |                 model_valid = False
 326 |                 break # Stop checking if any part fails
 327 | 
 328 |         if not model_valid:
 329 |              warnings.append(f"Model string rejected; reverting to default.")
 330 |              model = "GTR+G" if data_type == "DNA" else "LG+G"
 331 |         else:
 332 |              # Reconstruct normalized string
 333 |              model = ",".join(validated_parts)
 334 |     
 335 |     # 4. Seed Logic
 336 |     seed = params.get('seed')
 337 |     if seed is None:
 338 |         seed = random.randint(1, 2147483647)
 339 |     else:
 340 |         try:
 341 |             seed = int(seed)
 342 |             # Enforce 32-bit signed range roughly, but avoid INT_MIN
 343 |             if not (-2147483647 <= seed <= 2147483647):
 344 |                  warnings.append("Seed out of valid range; using random seed.")
 345 |                  seed = random.randint(1, 2147483647)
 346 |         except (ValueError, TypeError):
 347 |              warnings.append("Invalid seed provided; using random seed.")
 348 |              seed = random.randint(1, 2147483647)
 349 | 
 350 |     # 5. Outgroup Logic
 351 |     outgroup = params.get('outgroup')
 352 |     if outgroup:
 353 |         outgroup = str(outgroup).strip()
 354 |         # Reject whitespace/quotes
 355 |         if any(ch.isspace() for ch in outgroup) or '"' in outgroup or "'" in outgroup:
 356 |              warnings.append("Outgroup contained whitespace or quotes; ignored.")
 357 |              outgroup = None
 358 |         # Strict char check
 359 |         elif not re.match(r"^[A-Za-z0-9_\.\-\|:@]+$", outgroup):
 360 |              warnings.append(f"Outgroup '{outgroup}' contains invalid characters; ignored.")
 361 |              outgroup = None
 362 | 
 363 |     # 6. Early Stopping
 364 |     # NOTE: Does NOT check if supported by binary here. That check happens in service.
 365 |     enable_early_stopping = bool(params.get('enable_early_stopping', params.get('early_stopping', False)))
 366 |     
 367 |     # Force default OFF for high-quality presets
 368 |     if run_preset in ('publication', 'maximum') and enable_early_stopping:
 369 |         warnings.append(f"Early stopping disabled for '{run_preset}' quality to ensure thorough search.")
 370 |         enable_early_stopping = False
 371 | 
 372 |     return ResolvedRaxmlParams(
 373 |         enable_bootstrap=enable_bootstrap,
 374 |         bootstrap_cap=bootstrap_cap,
 375 |         start_tree_spec=start_tree_spec,
 376 |         model=model,
 377 |         seed=seed,
 378 |         data_type=data_type,
 379 |         warnings=warnings,
 380 |         outgroup=outgroup,
 381 |         enable_moose=enable_moose,
 382 |         enable_early_stopping=enable_early_stopping
 383 |     )

   1 | import re
   2 | from pathlib import Path
   3 | from typing import Optional, Tuple
   4 | 
   5 | 
   6 | # Valid job_id pattern: UUID4 format only
   7 | JOB_ID_PATTERN = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-4[0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$', re.IGNORECASE)
   8 | 
   9 | def validate_job_id(job_id: str) -> bool:
  10 |     """Validate job_id is a valid UUID4 format. Prevents directory traversal."""
  11 |     if not job_id or not isinstance(job_id, str):
  12 |         return False
  13 |     return bool(JOB_ID_PATTERN.match(job_id))
  14 | 
  15 | def sanitize_fasta_sequence(seq: str) -> str:
  16 |     """Remove any non-standard characters from FASTA sequence."""
  17 |     # Allow only valid nucleotide/amino acid characters
  18 |     allowed = set('ACGTUNRYKMSWBDHVacgtunrykmsv-')
  19 |     return ''.join(c for c in seq if c in allowed)
  20 | 
  21 | def validate_blast_query(query: str) -> Tuple[bool, Optional[str]]:
  22 |     """Validate BLAST query for dangerous patterns."""
  23 |     if not query:
  24 |         return False, "Empty query"
  25 |     
  26 |     # Check for suspicious shell-like patterns
  27 |     # Check for suspicious shell-like patterns
  28 |     # Allow newlines (\n, \r) as they are common in FASTA/sequences
  29 |     dangerous_patterns = [';', '`', '$', '&&']
  30 |     for pattern in dangerous_patterns:
  31 |         if pattern in query:
  32 |             return False, f"Query contains invalid character: {pattern}"
  33 |     
  34 |     return True, None
  35 | 
  36 | def validate_safe_file_path(path: Path, base_dir: Path) -> bool:
  37 |     """
  38 |     Ensure path is a real file inside base_dir and not a symlink.
  39 |     Returns True if safe, False otherwise.
  40 |     
  41 |     Checks:
  42 |     1. File exists
  43 |     2. Is a file (not directory)
  44 |     3. Is NOT a symlink (to prevent planting symlinks to /etc/passwd)
  45 |     4. Resolves to a path inside base_dir
  46 |     """
  47 |     try:
  48 |         # 1. Must exist (otherwise we can't check what it is)
  49 |         if not path.exists():
  50 |             return False
  51 |             
  52 |         # 3. Must NOT be a symlink (check before resolve)
  53 |         # We start with this because looking at stats of a symlink that points nowhere is tricky
  54 |         if path.is_symlink():
  55 |             return False
  56 | 
  57 |         # 2. Must be a file
  58 |         if not path.is_file():
  59 |             return False
  60 |             
  61 |         # 4. Resolve and check location (prevent traversal via ..)
  62 |         resolved_path = path.resolve()
  63 |         resolved_base = base_dir.resolve()
  64 |         
  65 |         # Ensure it is strictly inside
  66 |         return resolved_path.is_relative_to(resolved_base)
  67 |     except Exception:
  68 |         return False
  69 | 
  70 |

FILE: app/services/subprocess_utils.py

   1 | import subprocess
   2 | import logging
   3 | from pathlib import Path
   4 | from typing import List, Optional, Tuple
   5 | 
   6 | logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   7 | 
   8 | def run_command(args: List[str], cwd: Optional[Path] = None, log_file: Optional[Path] = None) -> Tuple[int, str, str]:
   9 |     """
  10 |     Run an external command safely.
  11 | 
  12 |     - args: list of command and arguments (no shell=True).
  13 |     - cwd: optional working directory.
  14 |     - log_file: optional path to append stdout/stderr.
  15 | 
  16 |     Returns (returncode, stdout, stderr).
  17 |     Raises no exceptions; caller decides what to do.
  18 |     """
  19 |     try:
  20 |         import os
  21 |         # Augment PATH if it's too restricted (e.g. in some systemd environments)
  22 |         env = os.environ.copy()
  23 |         current_path = env.get("PATH", "")
  24 |         if "/usr/bin" not in current_path:
  25 |             env["PATH"] = f"{current_path}:/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"
  26 |             
  27 |         logger.info(f"Running command: {' '.join(args)}")
  28 |         
  29 |         # Ensure cwd exists if provided
  30 |         if cwd and not cwd.exists():
  31 |             return -1, "", f"Working directory does not exist: {cwd}"
  32 | 
  33 |         result = subprocess.run(
  34 |             args,
  35 |             cwd=cwd,
  36 |             env=env,
  37 |             capture_output=True,
  38 |             text=True,
  39 |             check=False
  40 |         )
  41 |         
  42 |         stdout = result.stdout
  43 |         stderr = result.stderr
  44 |         
  45 |         if log_file:
  46 |             try:
  47 |                 with open(log_file, 'a') as f:
  48 |                     f.write(f"CMD: {' '.join(args)}\n")
  49 |                     f.write(f"STDOUT:\n{stdout}\n")
  50 |                     f.write(f"STDERR:\n{stderr}\n")
  51 |                     f.write("-" * 40 + "\n")
  52 |             except Exception as e:
  53 |                 logger.error(f"Failed to write to log file {log_file}: {e}")
  54 | 
  55 |         return result.returncode, stdout, stderr
  56 | 
  57 |     except Exception as e:
  58 |         logger.exception(f"Exception running command: {args}")
  59 |         return -1, "", str(e)
  60 | 
  61 | 
  62 | def run_command_streaming(
  63 |     args: List[str],
  64 |     cwd: Optional[Path] = None,
  65 |     stdout_path: Optional[Path] = None,
  66 |     stderr_path: Optional[Path] = None,
  67 |     on_stdout_line: Optional[callable] = None,
  68 |     on_stderr_line: Optional[callable] = None,
  69 |     timeout: Optional[int] = None,
  70 | ) -> Tuple[int, dict]:
  71 |     """
  72 |     Run an external command with streaming output.
  73 |     
  74 |     This is designed for real-time log streaming during job execution.
  75 |     
  76 |     Behavior:
  77 |     - If stdout_path provided: stdout goes directly to file (e.g., MAFFT alignment)
  78 |     - Otherwise: stdout streams via on_stdout_line callback
  79 |     - If stderr_path provided: stderr goes to file AND on_stderr_line callback
  80 |     - If no stderr_path: stderr only via callback
  81 |     
  82 |     Args:
  83 |         args: Command and arguments as list
  84 |         cwd: Working directory
  85 |         stdout_path: File to write stdout to (for MAFFT output)
  86 |         stderr_path: File to write stderr to (for logs)
  87 |         on_stdout_line: Callback for each stdout line (if not stdout_path)
  88 |         on_stderr_line: Callback for each stderr line
  89 |         timeout: Optional timeout in seconds
  90 |     
  91 |     Returns:
  92 |         (exit_code, stats_dict)
  93 |         
  94 |         stats_dict contains:
  95 |         - stdout_lines: int (count)
  96 |         - stderr_lines: int (count)
  97 |         - stderr_tail: List[str] (last 30 lines for error reporting)
  98 |         - duration_seconds: float
  99 |     """
 100 |     import os
 101 |     import time
 102 |     from collections import deque
 103 |     
 104 |     start_time = time.time()
 105 |     
 106 |     stats = {
 107 |         "stdout_lines": 0,
 108 |         "stderr_lines": 0,
 109 |         "stderr_tail": [],
 110 |         "duration_seconds": 0.0,
 111 |     }
 112 |     
 113 |     # Ring buffer for last 30 stderr lines
 114 |     stderr_tail_buffer: deque = deque(maxlen=30)
 115 |     
 116 |     try:
 117 |         # Augment PATH
 118 |         env = os.environ.copy()
 119 |         current_path = env.get("PATH", "")
 120 |         if "/usr/bin" not in current_path:
 121 |             env["PATH"] = f"{current_path}:/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin"
 122 |         
 123 |         logger.info(f"Running command (streaming): {' '.join(args)}")
 124 |         
 125 |         # Ensure cwd exists
 126 |         if cwd and not cwd.exists():
 127 |             stats["stderr_tail"] = [f"Working directory does not exist: {cwd}"]
 128 |             return -1, stats
 129 |         
 130 |         # Open stdout file if specified
 131 |         stdout_file = None
 132 |         if stdout_path:
 133 |             stdout_file = open(stdout_path, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n')
 134 |         
 135 |         # Open stderr file if specified
 136 |         stderr_file = None
 137 |         if stderr_path:
 138 |             stderr_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 139 |             stderr_file = open(stderr_path, 'a')
 140 |             stderr_file.write(f"CMD: {' '.join(args)}\n")
 141 |             stderr_file.write("-" * 40 + "\n")
 142 |         
 143 |         process = None
 144 |         try:
 145 |             # Start process
 146 |             process = subprocess.Popen(
 147 |                 args,
 148 |                 cwd=cwd,
 149 |                 env=env,
 150 |                 stdout=subprocess.PIPE if not stdout_file else stdout_file,
 151 |                 stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
 152 |                 text=True,
 153 |                 bufsize=1,  # Line buffered
 154 |             )
 155 |             
 156 |             # If stdout goes to file, we only need to handle stderr
 157 |             if stdout_file:
 158 |                 # Stream stderr only
 159 |                 for line in process.stderr:
 160 |                     stats["stderr_lines"] += 1
 161 |                     stderr_tail_buffer.append(line.rstrip())
 162 |                     
 163 |                     if stderr_file:
 164 |                         stderr_file.write(line)
 165 |                         stderr_file.flush()
 166 |                     
 167 |                     if on_stderr_line:
 168 |                         try:
 169 |                             on_stderr_line(line.rstrip())
 170 |                         except Exception:
 171 |                             pass  # Don't fail job due to callback error
 172 |             else:
 173 |                 # Stream both stdout and stderr using select for non-blocking reads
 174 |                 # This is more complex but handles interleaved output properly
 175 |                 import selectors
 176 |                 
 177 |                 sel = selectors.DefaultSelector()
 178 |                 if process.stdout:
 179 |                     sel.register(process.stdout, selectors.EVENT_READ, 'stdout')
 180 |                 if process.stderr:
 181 |                     sel.register(process.stderr, selectors.EVENT_READ, 'stderr')
 182 |                 
 183 |                 while sel.get_map():
 184 |                     ready = sel.select(timeout=0.1)
 185 |                     for key, _ in ready:
 186 |                         stream = key.fileobj
 187 |                         stream_name = key.data
 188 |                         
 189 |                         line = stream.readline()
 190 |                         if not line:
 191 |                             sel.unregister(stream)
 192 |                             continue
 193 |                         
 194 |                         line = line.rstrip()
 195 |                         
 196 |                         if stream_name == 'stdout':
 197 |                             stats["stdout_lines"] += 1
 198 |                             if on_stdout_line:
 199 |                                 try:
 200 |                                     on_stdout_line(line)
 201 |                                 except Exception:
 202 |                                     pass
 203 |                         else:  # stderr
 204 |                             stats["stderr_lines"] += 1
 205 |                             stderr_tail_buffer.append(line)
 206 |                             
 207 |                             if stderr_file:
 208 |                                 stderr_file.write(line + "\n")
 209 |                                 stderr_file.flush()
 210 |                             
 211 |                             if on_stderr_line:
 212 |                                 try:
 213 |                                     on_stderr_line(line)
 214 |                                 except Exception:
 215 |                                     pass
 216 |                 
 217 |                 sel.close()
 218 |             
 219 |             # Wait for process to complete
 220 |             process.wait(timeout=timeout)
 221 |             exit_code = process.returncode
 222 |             
 223 |         finally:
 224 |             if stdout_file:
 225 |                 stdout_file.close()
 226 |             if stderr_file:
 227 |                 stderr_file.write("-" * 40 + "\n")
 228 |                 stderr_file.write(f"Exit code: {process.returncode if 'process' in dir() else 'N/A'}\n")
 229 |                 stderr_file.close()
 230 |         
 231 |         stats["duration_seconds"] = time.time() - start_time
 232 |         stats["stderr_tail"] = list(stderr_tail_buffer)
 233 |         
 234 |         return exit_code, stats
 235 |         
 236 |     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
 237 |         logger.error(f"Command timed out after {timeout}s: {args}")
 238 |         if process:
 239 |             process.kill()
 240 |         stats["duration_seconds"] = time.time() - start_time
 241 |         stats["stderr_tail"] = list(stderr_tail_buffer)
 242 |         stats["stderr_tail"].append(f"[TIMEOUT] Command killed after {timeout}s")
 243 |         return -1, stats
 244 |         
 245 |     except Exception as e:
 246 |         logger.exception(f"Exception running command: {args}")
 247 |         stats["duration_seconds"] = time.time() - start_time
 248 |         stats["stderr_tail"] = list(stderr_tail_buffer)
 249 |         stats["stderr_tail"].append(f"[ERROR] {str(e)}")
 250 |         return -1, stats
