FILE: CLAUDE.md
========================================================================================

   1 | # CLAUDE.md
   2 | 
   3 | This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
   4 | 
   5 | ## What This Is
   6 | 
   7 | Dikarya is a Flask web application for fungal phylogenetic analysis. Users submit DNA sequences (FASTA or GenBank accessions), which are run through a configurable bioinformatics pipeline (alignment → trimming → tree building) via background workers, with real-time status updates via Server-Sent Events. Results are displayed in an interactive tree viewer.
   8 | 
   9 | **Stack:** Flask + Gunicorn, PostgreSQL + SQLAlchemy, Redis + RQ, Biopython, Tailwind (template-based UI).
  10 | 
  11 | ## Commands
  12 | 
  13 | ```bash
  14 | # Activate the virtual environment first
  15 | source .venv/bin/activate
  16 | 
  17 | # Database migrations
  18 | flask db migrate -m "description"
  19 | flask db upgrade
  20 | 
  21 | # Run web app (dev)
  22 | export FLASK_APP=wsgi.py
  23 | export FLASK_ENV=development
  24 | flask run --port=5000
  25 | 
  26 | # Start background worker (required for job processing)
  27 | flask run-worker
  28 | 
  29 | # Start metrics collector
  30 | flask run-metrics
  31 | 
  32 | # Add a What's New changelog entry (agents should use this to announce changes)
  33 | flask whats-new-add --title "Title here" --body "Description of what changed." --category feature
  34 | # Categories: feature, fix, improvement, update
  35 | 
  36 | # List existing What's New entries
  37 | flask whats-new-list
  38 | ```
  39 | 
  40 | Production runs under systemd as `dikarya-web.service` (Gunicorn) and `dikarya-worker.service` (RQ worker).     Many changes require dikarya-web to be restarted, which requires root access, so ask me if you need it to be restarted.
  41 | 
  42 | ## Architecture
  43 | 
  44 | ### Request Flow
  45 | 
  46 | 1. User submits sequences at `/tree` → job created in PostgreSQL, enqueued in Redis Queue
  47 | 2. `app/workers/tasks.py:run_phylo_job()` orchestrates the pipeline in a background worker
  48 | 3. Each step publishes events to Redis PubSub channel `job:{job_id}`
  49 | 4. Frontend subscribes via SSE at `/api/job/<id>/events` for real-time updates
  50 | 5. Results stored on disk at `var/jobs/{job_id}/`; tree viewer loads from `/job/<id>/view`
  51 | 
  52 | ### Pipeline Steps (in order)
  53 | 
  54 | `INPUT → ORIENT → BLAST → ALIGN → TRIM → TREE → POST`
  55 | 
  56 | ORIENT (sequence orientation), BLAST (homolog search), and TRIM are optional. The tree viewer at `/job/<id>/view` allows post-hoc pruning, rerooting, and renaming.
  57 | 
  58 | ### Blueprints
  59 | 
  60 | - `journal/` — public-facing journal website pages
  61 | - `main/` — sequence entry form, job status page, tree viewer
  62 | - `auth/` — login/register/logout
  63 | - `user/` — user job dashboard
  64 | - `api/` — all JSON endpoints used by the frontend JS
  65 | - `monitoring/` — health checks, worker status, system metrics
  66 | 
  67 | ### Services (`app/services/`)
  68 | 
  69 | Each bioinformatics step has its own service module. All external tool invocations go through `subprocess_utils.py`. Input validation and path safety live in `security_utils.py`. The `access_control.py` module enforces job ownership.
  70 | 
  71 | ### Job Parameters
  72 | 
  73 | `app/models.py` defines the dataclasses: `JobParams`, `AlignmentParams`, `TreeBuilderParams`, `TrimmingParams`. These are serialized to `var/jobs/{job_id}/input_info.json`.
  74 | 
  75 | ### File Layout on Disk
  76 | 
  77 | ```
  78 | var/jobs/{job_id}/
  79 |   input.fasta, oriented.fasta, aligned.fasta, trimmed.fasta
  80 |   blast_results.json
  81 |   tree/tree.newick, tree.nexus, tree_metadata.json, tree_state.json
  82 |   logs/{step}.log
  83 | ```
  84 | 
  85 | ### External Tool Binaries
  86 | 
  87 | Configured via environment variables (with fallback defaults in `app/config.py`): `RAXML_BINARY`, `IQTREE_BINARY`, `MRBAYES_BINARY`, `MAFFT_BINARY`, `MUSCLE_BINARY`, `CLUSTALO_BINARY`, `TRIMAL_BINARY`, `BMGE_BINARY`, `FASTTREE_BINARY`.
  88 | 
  89 | ## Frontend
  90 | 
  91 | ### Tree Builder Page (`/tree`)
  92 | 
  93 | The main Tree Builder UI is implemented in `templates/sequence_entry.html`.
  94 | 
  95 | This template contains:
  96 | 
  97 | - HTML for imports, BLAST, queue, and submit controls
  98 | - A large inline `<script>` block that owns page state and behavior:
  99 |   - `sequenceQueue` state
 100 |   - Queue rendering (`updateQueueDisplay`)
 101 |   - MycoMap / iNaturalist import handlers
 102 |   - BLAST submission
 103 |   - Submit payload construction
 104 | 
 105 | Shared frontend helpers live in `templates/base_modern.html` (global `showStatus()`, dark mode, layout shell).
 106 | 
 107 | ### Tree Viewer Page (`/job/<id>/view`)
 108 | 
 109 | Three large JS files handle the tree viewer:
 110 | 
 111 | - `app/static/js/tree_viewer_controller.js` (orchestration)
 112 | - `app/static/js/tree_viewer_phylotree_v2.js` (D3 rendering)
 113 | - `app/static/js/phylotree.js` (library)
 114 | 
 115 | **Change comment convention for `.js`:** Every edit to files ending in .js (Javascript) must include an inline comment in the format `// Alan M/D/YY - <brief description of what changed and why>` placed on the line immediately above the changed code.
 116 | 
 117 | Job status updates live in `job_status.js`.
 118 | 
 119 | ### Frontend Data Contracts
 120 | 
 121 | #### `sequenceQueue` Item Shape
 122 | 
 123 | `sequenceQueue` is an array of objects stored in the inline JS in `templates/sequence_entry.html`. Queue operations should preserve extra metadata fields on entries (do not strip unknown keys).
 124 | 
 125 | ```json
 126 | {
 127 |   "name": "string (required)",
 128 |   "sequence": "string (required)",
 129 |   "organism": "string (optional)",
 130 |   "source": "string (optional, e.g., 'mycomap')",
 131 |   "hit_source": "string | null (e.g., 'ncbi', 'local')",
 132 |   "query_cover": "number | null",
 133 |   "subject_cover": "number | null",
 134 |   "identity": "number | null",
 135 |   "blast_metrics_available": "boolean (optional)"
 136 | }
 137 | ```
 138 | 
 139 | **Note:** Not all queue entries will have source-specific fields (e.g., manual FASTA, file upload, iNaturalist), so UI/filter logic must handle missing values safely.
 140 | 
 141 | #### API Response Conventions (Frontend-facing)
 142 | 
 143 | When changing `/api/` endpoints used by `sequence_entry.html`:
 144 | 
 145 | - Prefer additive JSON changes (new optional fields) over breaking changes.
 146 | - Do not rename/remove existing fields unless explicitly requested.
 147 | - Preserve current success/error response behavior expected by existing frontend handlers.
 148 | 
 149 | Endpoints frequently used by `/tree`:
 150 | 
 151 | - `/api/blast`
 152 | - `/api/mycomap`
 153 | - `/api/inaturalist`
 154 | - `/api/job`
 155 | 
 156 | ### MycoMap Integration Notes (Important)
 157 | 
 158 | `services/mycomap_service.py` currently fetches FASTA from MycoMap endpoints:
 159 | 
 160 | - `fasta` (NCBI results)
 161 | - `localFasta` (local/MycoBLAST results)
 162 | 
 163 | If a feature needs BLAST metrics (e.g., query coverage / subject coverage / identity), it typically requires:
 164 | 
 165 | 1. An additional fetch/parsing step for the BLAST results table/page.
 166 | 2. Matching parsed hit rows back to imported FASTA entries.
 167 | 3. Preserving those metrics on queue entries for frontend filtering.
 168 | 
 169 | Current `validate_mycomap_url(...)` does strict hostname and scheme validation and extracts `r<digits>` blast IDs. Preserve this security behavior.
 170 | 
 171 | ## Restarting Dikarya services
 172 | 
 173 | Agents may restart the Dikarya systemd services when needed after making changes. The `tree` user has limited passwordless sudo access to three root-owned wrapper scripts only:
 174 | 
 175 | ```bash
 176 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-web
 177 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-worker
 178 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-metrics
 179 | ```
 180 | 
 181 | Use these wrappers instead of running sudo systemctl restart ... directly.
 182 | 
 183 | The most commonly needed restart is:
 184 | 
 185 | sudo /usr/local/sbin/restart-dikarya-web
 186 | 
 187 | Run this after changes that affect the web application runtime, routes, views/templates, Python application code, configuration read by the web process, dependencies, or other behavior served by the Dikarya web app.
 188 | 
 189 | Use the worker or metrics restart wrappers only when the change affects those specific background services.
 190 | 
 191 | Do not attempt to use sudo for anything else. The tree user is intentionally restricted and should not be able to open a root shell, run arbitrary systemctl commands, edit sudoers, or restart unrelated services.
 192 | 
 193 | ## What's New / Changelog System
 194 | 
 195 | The "What's New" page at `/whats-new` shows changelog entries stored in PostgreSQL. It is linked in the header nav between "Journal Home" and "Tree Builder".
 196 | 
 197 | **When you (an agent) make a user-visible change, add an entry so users know about it.   At the end of the agent output, tell the user that you have done this, and tell the user the exact text added to the What's New page**
 198 | 
 199 | ### Adding an entry
 200 | 
 201 | ```bash
 202 | source .venv/bin/activate
 203 | FLASK_APP=wsgi.py flask whats-new-add \
 204 |   --title "Short description of the change" \
 205 |   --body "Longer explanation for users. Can include plain text or simple HTML." \
 206 |   --category feature
 207 | ```
 208 | 
 209 | Categories:
 210 | - `feature` — new capability added
 211 | - `fix` — bug fixed
 212 | - `improvement` — existing feature made better
 213 | - `update` — dependency, content, or configuration change
 214 | 
 215 | ### How it works
 216 | 
 217 | - `WhatsNewEntry` model in `app/models.py` stores entries (title, body, category, published_at).
 218 | - `WhatsNewView` tracks each user's (or IP's) last visit to the page.
 219 | - Entries newer than the visitor's last view are shown with full contrast + a gold "New" badge.
 220 | - Older entries are slightly dimmed.
 221 | - A gold dot on the nav link appears whenever there are unseen entries.
 222 | - The context processor in `app/__init__.py` (`inject_whats_new_badge`) injects `whats_new_has_new` into all templates.
 223 | 
 224 | ### Listing existing entries
 225 | 
 226 | ```bash
 227 | FLASK_APP=wsgi.py flask whats-new-list
 228 | ```
 229 | 
 230 | ## Key Conventions & UI Patterns
 231 | 
 232 | - Flask app factory in `app/__init__.py`; extensions initialized in `app/extensions.py`.
 233 | - All API responses use JSON; the frontend is a SPA-style UI talking to `/api/` endpoints.
 234 | - FASTA sequence headers are sanitized on input and restored on download/display (see `fasta_utils.py`).
 235 | - RAxML-NG jobs use named presets (`fast_good`, `standard`, `publication`, `maximum`) defined in `tree_builder_service.py`.
 236 | - Job IDs are UUIDs; always validate with regex before using in file paths.
 237 | - Use existing Tailwind utility style patterns from `templates/sequence_entry.html` and `templates/partials/*.html`.
 238 | - Reuse modal structure from `templates/partials/add_sequences_modal.html`.
 239 | - Support dark mode (`dark:*` classes) for all new UI.
 240 | - Reuse existing color/style tokens (`journal-dark`, `journal-gold`, etc.).
 241 | - `showStatus()` is global and provided by `templates/base_modern.html`.
 242 | 
 243 | ## Change Strategy for AI Agents (Minimal Churn)
 244 | 
 245 | - **Do NOT use TDD** — just make the requested changes. The human developer will test the code manually.
 246 | - Prefer targeted edits and minimal, production-safe modifications over large refactors.
 247 | - Prefer small helper functions over large rewrites when adding features.
 248 | - Do NOT split large inline scripts into separate JS files unless explicitly requested by the user.
 249 | - Preserve existing DOM IDs and event handler wiring whenever possible.
 250 | - Preserve queue behavior strictly:
 251 |   - Count badge updates.
 252 |   - Empty/non-empty state toggles.
 253 |   - Clear/remove actions.
 254 |   - Outgroup dropdown refresh (`populateOutgroupDropdown`) after queue changes.
 255 |   - Duplicate handling in `addSequences(...)`.
 256 | 
 257 | ## Manual QA Checklist for `/tree` Changes
 258 | 
 259 | > **Note for AI:** The following is a manual QA checklist for the human developer. You do not need to execute these tests or write test code for them; just ensure your code changes do not break these existing flows.
 260 | 
 261 | ### Queue Basics
 262 | 
 263 | - Add custom FASTA sequences works.
 264 | - File upload → parse → add to queue works.
 265 | - Remove one sequence (×) works.
 266 | - Clear all works.
 267 | - Sequence count badge updates correctly.
 268 | 
 269 | ### Imports
 270 | 
 271 | - MycoMap import works (NCBI/local checkboxes respected).
 272 | - MycoMap duplicate removal checkbox behavior still works.
 273 | - iNaturalist analyze + add/BLAST flows still work.
 274 | - BLAST from manual input works.
 275 | 
 276 | ### Submission
 277 | 
 278 | - One-Click Quick Tree still submits successfully.
 279 | - Advanced panel toggle still works.
 280 | - Standard submit still sends valid payload.
 281 | - Edit/clone job modes still work (`edit_job_id`, `clone_job_id`).
 282 | 
 283 | ### UX
 284 | 
 285 | - New UI works in dark mode.
 286 | - Status messages display via `showStatus()`.
 287 | - No console JS errors.
 288 | 
 289 | ## Ops / Debugging Notes
 290 | 
 291 | - Production runs under systemd (Gunicorn web + worker services).
 292 | - If route changes do not appear in production, Gunicorn reload/restart may be required.
 293 | - Check app logs for 500 errors before changing code.
 294 | 
 295 | ## Documentation Split
 296 | 
 297 | - **CLAUDE.md** = implementation guidance, conventions, and safe-edit rules.
 298 | - **ARCHITECTURE.md** = deeper system design and reference details.
 299 | 
 300 | If making substantial changes, consult `ARCHITECTURE.md` for subsystem context, but keep code changes aligned with the conventions in this file.

FILE: debug_nexus.py
========================================================================================

   1 | from Bio import Phylo
   2 | import sys
   3 | 
   4 | try:
   5 |     path = "/var/www/dikarya/var/jobs/6ef2764f-1344-4c26-9b4c-7a9010ae0667/tree/mrbayes_input.nex.con.tre"
   6 |     trees = list(Phylo.parse(path, "nexus"))
   7 |     print(f"Found {len(trees)} trees")
   8 |     for tree in trees:
   9 |         print(f"Tree: {tree}")
  10 |         
  11 |     Phylo.write(trees, "debug_out.newick", "newick")
  12 |     print("Write success")
  13 | except Exception as e:
  14 |     print(f"Error: {e}")
  15 |     import traceback
  16 |     traceback.print_exc()
